Fixed-format evaluation and the next security target, 2026-10-08
---------------------------------------------------------------
RecursiveMultiplication proves two concrete scalar phase products. The first
multiplies two linear phases without any auxiliary hint: for phases b1+c1*s
and b2+c2*s, output T=-outer(c1,c2), c=b2*c1+b1*c2 and b=b1*b2. Its recursive
phase is their exact product. The second publicly relinearizes arbitrary
recursive phases using an explicitly defined hint table. Index decryption
features by none (one), some(inl i) (s_i) and some(inr(i,j)) (s_i*s_j), with
N=n*n+n+1 features. The corresponding public ciphertext coefficients are
b, c_i and -T_ij. The table entries have plaintext

  gadget(level)*feature(s,left)*feature(s,right).

There are N^2*levels entries. Decompose each public coefficient product into
short base digits and sum digit-weighted table ciphertexts. The exact output
phase is the input-phase product plus the sum of digit-weighted hint errors.
For a uniform hint error bound H, that extra error is at most
N^2*levels*(base-1)*H. Actual private generation under the single binary secret
has hint errors at most (n+1)*B for bounded scalar errors B. The full plaintext
product error is m1*e2+m2*e1+e1*e2 plus this relinearization error, with a checked
centered-magnitude bound. This is raw phase arithmetic, not direct multiplication
of the previous half-modulus Boolean encoding or a bootstrap theorem.

The table includes gadget multiples of s_i*s_j*s_k*s_l. Four Boolean cube
differences prove that a weight-one entry with four distinct coordinates cannot
be a quadratic polynomial, even on the binary-secret corners. Therefore the
existing fixed quadratic-message ordinary-LWE theorem does not prove security
of this publication. No degree-four KDM assumption is adopted; the table is an
alternative algebraic interface with a precisely identified unproved security
obligation, not a replacement for the ordinary-LWE-only objective.

A more direct evaluation interface is now formalized in RecursiveFeatureGSW.
A public, computable finite enumeration maps recursive coefficient triples
bijectively to vectors of length N. The derived vector gamma(s)=(1,s_i,s_i*s_j)
uses the original secret only. Decryption is the gamma/coefficient pairing.
Flattening each column gives a rectangular GSW matrix; unflattening is inverse
for every matrix. Applying the existing public GSW gadget/digit multiplication
and unflattening keeps exactly the same column format. It needs no quartic
relinearization table and draws no new secret. Its exact error is

  error1*digitMatrix(ciphertext2) + message1*error2.

The bit-product bound is N*levels*(base-1)*E1+E2. The public NAND and the
nearest-codeword decoder at the constant feature's selected gadget column have
checked correctness from this bound and the explicit public separation margin.
These representation and evaluation definitions are computable; full arithmetic
cost and final parameter instantiations are not yet proved. Security of the
native scalar-secret GSW key sampler cannot be transferred to this derived key
by its algebraic form alone. No LWE hardness premise for gamma is asserted.

There is a checked incompatibility with the previous public encryption.
Its Regev embedding has T=0, so every quadratic flattened mask coordinate is
zero. Adding bit*gadget then reveals the bit in a quadratic diagonal gadget
entry at level zero. A public-coordinate observer recovers it for every raw
linear ciphertext choice in a nontrivial modulus ring, without any secret input.
Thus the previous linear public key cannot serve as the full mask for this GSW
representation. The next constructive security step is to sample full recursive
zero-ciphertext public columns, retain their entire law through the existing
ordinary-LWE reduction, and prove the uniform full-coordinate subset-mask law.
This is a specific algorithm/distribution proof to implement, not a new assumption.

For refresh through original-bit GSW controls, their gadget-feature plaintexts
are s_i*gadget(level)*feature(s,index). A weight-one quadratic-feature column
contains s_i*s_j*s_k. Three Boolean cube differences show it is not quadratic
on three independent binary coordinates. Therefore even this control route
needs security beyond the current quadratic-message proof. Direct private
fresh generation of controls for all of gamma would include the degree-four
targets above. Alternatively the checked GSW bit-product identity provides a
route to forming quadratic-feature controls from pairs of secure original-bit
controls. Those would have the product's error law, not the independently fresh
quartic law. Packaging their joint-view security as public postprocessing still
needs the original-bit control reduction and efficient evaluation classes.
Neither a secure control sampler nor reusable refresh is constructed here.
The feature-capacity obstruction remains an obstruction to affine-only feature
lifting, not a general impossibility theorem for ordinary-LWE FHE.

The primary BV11KDM comparison was rechecked in Sections 1.1 and 4:
https://www.iacr.org/archive/crypto2011/68410501/68410501.pdf
Its recursive higher-degree encryption length/decryption degree grows with the
message degree, so it does not directly supply degree-three/four messages in
our fixed quadratic law. This reading supports keeping the missing fixed-format
security target explicit; no RLWE assumption or generic KDM amplification claim
is imported into either module. The author-hosted paper is already in refs;
no new reference or manual download was needed.

Full-coordinate recursive-feature public encryption, 2026-10-08
--------------------------------------------------------------
RecursiveFeaturePublicKey now fills the previously missing public-mask step.
It publishes p fresh recursive zero-ciphertext columns in all N=n*n+n+1
flattened coordinates. No extra secret is sampled. The published context still
contains r original linear public-key rows and k fixed quadratic-message hints.
The ordinary-LWE observer reduction covers this entire view in one experiment.
The retained original raw view is exactly uniform in the justified ideal branch:
an explicit equivalence retains all public coordinates and accounts for every
unused raw mask. Publishing/splitting this view is another equivalence. This
proves the whole ideal law, rather than asserting hardness for gamma(s).

The masking adversary chooses its two bit messages after the complete public
view. Joint GSW column masking costs N*levels*sqrt(q^N/2^p)/2. The module
materializes the stored public table, keeps the original secret for decryption,
and identifies the standard VCVio one-time IND-CPA experiment with the masking
experiment. Its real advantage is bounded by the existing uniform-secret
ordinary-LWE reduction terms plus the actual error-sampler implementation loss
and the joint masking term. There is no binary-LWE, KDM, or derived-key hardness
premise added to these inequalities.

Correctness retains correlated recursive zero-column errors honestly. A public
column's phase has centered magnitude at most (n+1)*B under supported binary
keys and scalar errors bounded by B. Binary subset selectors give a GSW error
bound p*(n+1)*B; gadget addition cancels exactly. The generic nearest-codeword
margin proves all supported outcomes of the standard CorrectExp equal true.

RecursiveFeaturePublicKeyParameters uses t=lambda+1, n=2*t^5, q=2^(t^2),
B=2^t, levels=t^2, base=2 and p=(n*n+n+1)*t^2+2*lambda+1.
The source row count is r+(p+k)*(n+1). Explicit natural-coefficient polynomials
represent p and the row count for polynomial r and k. The masking term is at
most N*t^2*2^(-lambda) and is negligible. The implementation loss is negligible
at those row counts. Eventually 4*p*(n+1)*B<q, so the selected highest gadget
level has enough separation for probability-one supported fresh correctness.
These conservative parameters prove fresh public encryption, not final refresh
or arithmetic running costs. General uniform samplers/key generation remain
noncomputable Lean descriptions; ordinary-LWE game transfer to the ideal
Gaussian and efficient observer/reduction classes still need packaging.

The next central research target remains original-bit cubic control security
in this same reusable format, while retaining the entire public mask and hint
view. A direct fresh control encrypts s_i*gadget(level)*feature(s,index); the
quadratic feature coordinates require cubic messages. The existing quadratic
proof does not cover them, and the checked cube-difference lemma shows that
a change of quadratic message coefficients alone cannot fix this. A new
simulation/reduction, or a different bootstrap-compatible control law proved
from ordinary LWE, is required. No secure reusable bootstrapper is claimed.

required_control_not_quadratic_at_level shows that every nonzero gadget level
in the three-coordinate original-bit control target has this obstruction,
not only the previously checked weight-one level. Changing gadget levels

Recursive-feature same-key refresh and compatible circuit parameters, 2026-10-08
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
RecursiveFeatureBootstrap supplies the missing public correctness interface for
the recursive-feature GSW format. The expanded binary vector has L=n*n+n+1
coordinates: one, the original bits, and their ordered pairwise products. It is
only a representation of the original secret. The constant control is the public
gadget. Quadratic controls are public GSW products of original-bit controls, with
error bound (M+1)*H for M=L*levels*(base-1) and original bound H. They are not
freshly generated quartic-message ciphertexts. All expanded controls, original
private control material, and refreshed outputs have explicit finite backing data.

Negating every coefficient of the selected constant-feature gadget column gives
a public mask vector; the extracted body is zero. With the constant binary
feature included, its phase is exactly the original recursive decryption phase.
The generic accumulator already accepts this feature-secret vector. Public floor
rounding and its checked binary residual bound therefore give a refresh error
P*L*M*(M+1)*H. NAND followed by this refresh restores the same bound. The actual
private recursive control generator has error f+dot(e,s), bounded by (n+1)*B
when all scalar errors have centered magnitude at most B. Its required cubic
plaintexts are retained honestly; no security reduction for this generator is
asserted.

RecursiveFeatureBootstrapParameters matches the existing security family with
 t=lambda+1, n=2*t^5, Q=2^(t^2), B=2^t, levels=t^2, base=2.
Set k=clog_2(8*L), P=2^k and d=2^(t^2-k). The checked bounds are
8*L<=P<=16*L, and eventually k<=t^2 with d*P=Q. For H=(n+1)*B,
let O=P*L*M*(M+1)*H and E=(M+1)*O. An explicit natural-coefficient
polynomial bounds P*E/B. Negligibility of polynomial times 2^(-lambda)
proves P*E<Q eventually. It implies E<=d, the strict rounding margin
2*(E+(L+1)*(d-1))<d*(P/2), and the strict output decoding margin
2*O<d*(P/2). The selected highest gadget is exactly up_d(P/2).
The existing public-encryption error, publicSamples*(n+1)*B, is at most O.
These are Lean eventual inequalities; the earlier hand-derived threshold 71
is not used as a kernel-checked finite cutoff.

RecursiveFeatureCircuit applies the stored shared NAND-DAG evaluator to this
operation and proves the same invariant on every wire and correct decoding of
any selected output. Every gate retains the same original-bit control data,
secret and L-by-(L*levels) output shape. The counted evaluator returns exactly
the number of DAG gates. This counts gate invocations, not scalar arithmetic or
bit operations. The concrete parameter half-code specialization is proof-only
noncomputable to avoid expensive code-generator type reduction; halfCode and
the generic public evaluator remain computable with explicit public parameters.

These modules advance correctness in the format already used by the ordinary-
LWE fresh-encryption development. They do not complete its joint evaluation-key
security proof. The central remaining research requirement is the reduction of
the actual original-bit cubic control view, together with the full public mask
view, to ordinary LWE. Efficient sampler/evaluator/reduction classes, Gaussian
model transfer, and full all-parameter scheme/security packaging remain open.
The original one-secret-key, ordinary-LWE-only bootstrappable-FHE goal remains
active and unfinished.

Actual complete recursive-feature security experiment, 2026-10-08
----------------------------------------------------------------
RecursiveFeatureSecurityView now instantiates the security experiment with the
actual private generator used by RecursiveFeatureBootstrap. A single binary
secret generates p recursive zero public-key columns and n*(L*levels) original-
bit control columns, where L=n*n+n+1. All masks are uniform in one joint matrix
and all (p+n*L*levels)*(n+1) scalar errors are sampled IID. Both the public table
and the controls are materialized before publication. The generator does not
substitute a fixed quadratic context for the real cubic controls.

The checked public-column phase is f+dot(e,s). The checked stored-control view
is exactly freshOriginal, with phase bit_i*phase(gadgetBatch)_column+f+dot(e,s).
For quadratic feature coordinates this message contains the same cubic term
identified by the weighted cube-difference obstruction. The original-bit
secret is retained only in the private part of the key-generation output.

The public key type includes the entire stored original control vector.
chooseMessages receives it before the IND-CPA challenge; distinguish can retain
it in adversary state. Computed product controls and repeated circuit evaluation
use this same published data. No additional secret or key schedule is sampled.
The standard stored-encryption one-time IND-CPA game is proved distributionally
equal to the existing GSWMasking experiment with this exact joint key sampler.

The resulting bound is

  |signed IND-CPA advantage| <= J + (L*levels)*sqrt(q^L/2^p)/2,

where J is the absolute winning-probability difference between this real game
and a game that keeps the exact real marginal of all original controls but
replaces the public zero table by independent uniform coordinates. There is
no claim that the controls are independent of the real table. J is a defined
computational experiment, not an adopted circular/KDM hardness assumption.
The general theorem is unconditional but does not bound J by ordinary LWE.

RecursiveFeatureSecurityParameters specializes this bound to the existing
binary dimension 2*(parameter+1)^5, q=2^((parameter+1)^2), base two, levels
(parameter+1)^2, and p=L*levels+2*parameter+1. Its secret is directly uniform
over binary vectors and its scalar errors use the existing finite-budget
UniformInterval sampler. The statistical term is the already proved negligible
maskingBound, and a Nat polynomial evaluates exactly to the full scalar error
count including original controls. A polynomial sample count does not establish
polynomial runtime for the full algorithm or reduction.

This isolates the security obligation at the actual complete same-key view.
The decisive ordinary-LWE reduction for J, including cubic control plaintexts
and their shared hidden secret, remains unproved. No independent binary-LWE,
leakage, KDM or circular-security premise has been inserted to close it. Strict
efficiency classes, Gaussian model transfer and final all-parameter FHE security
packaging also remain required. Existing refs were sufficient for this step;
no additional paper was downloaded. The original goal remains unfinished.

Robust quadratic approximation of cubic controls, 2026-10-08
------------------------------------------------------------
The previous weighted cube-difference theorem excluded exact quadratic
representation, but did not exclude a quadratic approximation that only fails
on a negligible fraction of the full key space. RecursiveFeatureCubicApproximation
now checks that candidate for the actual original-bit quadratic-feature message.

Write |x|_q for the centered magnitude in ZMod q. For any fixed quadratic
message P and cubic weight w, on every three-bit slice with all other key bits
fixed the alternating sum of w*s_0*s_1*s_2-P(s) is w. If all eight discrepancies
had magnitude at most D, the modular triangle inequality would imply
|w|_q <= 8*D. Therefore 8*D < |w|_q forces at least one failure on EACH slice,
not just a single rare corner of the full key space. Restricting a quadratic
polynomial to the slice is proved explicitly, including its linear cross terms
and constant tail contribution. An injection from all tails into the failing
key set proves at least 2^(n-3) bad keys. The failure probability under a uniform
binary key is consequently at least 1/8.

control_discrepancy_eq connects this discrepancy to the actual controlMessage
for bit 0, quadratic feature (1,2), at any selected gadget level. Two ciphertexts
with the same hidden secret, whose phase errors are each bounded by H, cannot
be equal when their messages differ by centered magnitude greater than 2*H.
The checked bounded_ciphertext_coupling_bad_count applies even when the two
error functions are arbitrary functions of the key; it counts failures rather
than assuming independent errors or inferring a public attack.

For the existing parameter family H=(n+1)*B is the fresh recursive-control phase
bound, and the top gadget weight has centered magnitude q/2. The existing
eventual fresh-encryption margin 4*p*H < q and the new all-parameter bound p>=8
give 32*H < q. Since q is even, 8*(2*H) < q/2. Thus, eventually, ANY fixed
quadratic approximation differs from that required cubic control message by
more than twice the fresh phase-error bound on at least one eighth of all keys.
The asymptotic probability result quantifies over every such quadratic P.

This rejects the proposed fixed-quadratic message compiler followed by bounded
phase-error absorption with the hidden key fixed. It does not bound the public
IND-CPA advantage: the phase comparison uses the hidden secret, and the actual
security experiment does not publish it. It is not a general impossibility
theorem for cubic KDM or ordinary-LWE FHE. A reduction using different secret
transport, source-output-dependent processing, or another ciphertext law has
not been excluded by these statements. The positive joint-view reduction is
still missing; no hardness of that unresolved view is adopted as a premise.

The primary BGK11 source was rechecked in Sections 4 and 6.1--6.3 using the
existing refs/kdm/BGK11.pdf, together with the IACR proceedings version:
https://www.iacr.org/archive/tcc2011/65970198/65970198.pdf . Its monomial-expanded
ACPS encryption decrypts with the expanded secret and has its own public
Gaussian-combination sampler and prime-square modulus parameters. Its ordinary-
LWE polynomial-KDM theorem is not a theorem for these stored recursive controls.
Applebaum11 Section 5 was also rechecked from the existing local PDF: the
simulatable full-KDM hypothesis used to obtain FHE is stronger than ordinary
bounded-function KDM security. Neither comparison supplies the missing public
conversion preserving the current bootstrap law. No new paper was needed.
The ordinary-LWE-only one-secret-key bootstrappable-FHE goal remains unfinished.

Ordinary-LWE hop for the complete quadratic control comparison, 2026-10-08
------------------------------------------------------------------------
RecursiveFeatureControlHybrid and RecursiveFeatureControlPolynomial connect the
existing quadratic compiler to the actual stored original-bit control format.
This is a positive computational hop for the same complete IND-CPA observer,
while leaving the security of distinct-triple cubic messages unresolved.

The explicit controlPolynomial keeps every constant-feature and linear-feature
control message. For a quadratic feature (j,k), the required message is
w*s_i*s_j*s_k. Binary idempotence makes this quadratic when any two of i,j,k
coincide; those messages are kept as well. Only pairwise distinct triples are
replaced by zero. message_piecePolynomial and controlPolynomial_message_at
prove this classification. stored_control_non_cubic_eq proves equality of each
retained stored scalar ciphertext, with exactly the same masks and errors.
This comparison is a security hybrid, not a new bootstrap-correct generator.

bridge_fresh and quadratic_keyView_evalDist identify the whole comparison law
with the existing compiled quadratic source. They retain the public table,
all stored controls, and the shared secret/error sampling. The source adversary
reconstructs the actual stored layout before invoking the original observer.
blockBits_uniform_evalDist proves that the one-bit block key is exactly a
uniform binary vector; it does not posit binary-secret LWE hardness. The source
terms remain the existing reductions to ordinary uniform-secret narrow-law LWE.

The concrete family_oneTime_abs_signedAdvantage_le checks the bound

  |actual IND-CPA signed advantage|
    <= cubic control replacement advantage
       + explicit quadratic ordinary-LWE reduction loss
       + finite-sampler implementation loss + statistical masking loss.

The replacement advantage compares the real publication to this quadratic
publication under the SAME complete stored-view observer. Its negligibility
is neither assumed nor proved. family_implementationLoss_negligible discharges
the finite-sampler loss using the previously proved polynomial scalar-error
count. The masking loss was already proved negligible. Strict polynomial-time
reduction closure, precise ordinary Gaussian source transfer, and the remaining
cubic replacement are still required for the requested final security theorem.
No independent binary-LWE, KDM, leakage or circular hardness premise was added.

A direct feature-secret reinterpretation does not currently supply a simulation:
applying the quadratic compiler to the expanded feature secret produces products
of those features (degree four in the original bits), while the implemented
refresh ciphertext decrypts in the quadratic feature basis. No public conversion
preserving that bootstrap law was established. This records the missing bridge
for that route, not an impossibility theorem for all feature-source reductions.
Existing related papers in refs were sufficient for this step. The original
ordinary-LWE-only one-secret-key bootstrappable-FHE goal remains unfinished.

Sampled family-level repeatable FHE correctness, 2026-10-08
-----------------------------------------------------------
RecursiveFeatureFHECorrectness closes the correctness composition for the
actual complete same-key sampler and stored evaluator. Earlier circuit theorems
assumed noise bounds on caller-supplied controls and inputs. The new experiment
samples the actual completed key, publicly encrypts every input with independent
selector tapes, runs the existing shared NAND program, and checks its selected
output using the original secret.

sampleDraws_error_bound derives every scalar error bound from the scalar sampler's
support. keygen_noiseBounds then derives both the fresh public-column phase bound
and all original-bit control bounds under the SAME sampled binary secret. Each is
at most H=(n+1)*B. encryptInputs_noiseBounds derives the input bound samples*H
for every supported outcome of the actual public encryption algorithm. No
independence between ciphertext phase errors is assumed in the arithmetic proof.

The generic correctness theorem pays the public encryption budget, complete
refresh output bound, rounding margin and decoding margin. A relation invariant
through the actual shared wire table proves every output's Boolean semantics.
correctExp_supported proves every supported result is true; the total ProbComp
experiment therefore succeeds with probability one. This includes all jointly
sampled controls and inputs, rather than treating their boundedness as external
correctness hypotheses.

The public evaluate wrapper transports only the checked equality
q=factor*lookupModulus, allowing the existing evaluator to consume precisely the
canonical security family's ciphertext type. family_gadget_eq_up verifies that
its gadget and lookup encoding agree across that modulus equality.
familyCorrectExp_eq_scheme checks exact equality with the actual security
family's keygen/encrypt/decrypt API, followed by this public evaluation.

family_correctExp_probability_one discharges every numerical margin using the
existing compatible parameter family and exact interval sampler support.
eventually_family_correctExp_probability_one orders its quantifiers as:

  eventually parameter, exists its capacity proof,
    for EVERY input length, EVERY shared NAND program, and EVERY selected output,
      actual sampled keygen/encrypt/evaluate/decrypt correctness probability = 1.

Thus the parameter threshold is independent of program size and depth. Previously
proved ciphertext types remain fixed at that parameter, the underlying secret is
unchanged throughout, and shared wires are stored rather than expanded into trees.
No new ciphertext generator, bootstrap gate, key schedule or security assumption
was introduced. The wrapper and experiment compose existing algorithms.

This is a correctness composition, not an ordinary-LWE-only security theorem.
The real-to-quadratic replacement of distinct-triple cubic controls still lacks
a computational reduction. Strict bit-operation/runtime classes, closure of the
security reductions in those classes, and the precise ordinary Gaussian-source
transfer also remain unproved. The original goal stays active and unfinished.
The existing reference collection was sufficient; no paper was downloaded.

Composable-FHE source audit and exact fair-bit key generation, 2026-10-08
------------------------------------------------------------------------
The published Micciancio25 paper was rechecked in the existing
refs/surveys-and-boundaries/Micciancio25.pdf and the primary CiC version:
https://cic.iacr.org/p/2/1/1/pdf . Theorem 6 (page 17) assumes psi-circular
IND-CPA security for its security conclusion. Definition 11 and Theorem 7
(page 18) obtain the ordinary-IND-CPA alternative using separate keys for
successive layers. Neither supplies the missing same-key ordinary-LWE reduction.
Definition 6 quantifies over all ciphertexts; our noise-invariant correctness
results do not assert that stronger malformed-ciphertext property. This source
was already in refs and no duplicate was downloaded. No security premise or
key schedule from that construction was adopted.

One remaining efficiency requirement has now been addressed directly.
RecursiveFeatureBitSampling supplies a fair-bit implementation of the actual
complete key sampler. residueSampler maps exactly width fair bits through the
explicit Fin(2^width)/ZMod(2^width) equivalence. Binary secret coordinates are
independently sampled from one fair bit each. challengeEquiv reindexes a stored
vector of independent residues into every mask entry, using the existing public
error-column enumeration. It does not enumerate all possible masks or keys.
The scalar errors use the existing bounded-rejection interval sampler.

Let n be the binary dimension, S the actual scalar error count, and t=lambda+1.
There are n*S independent mask coefficients, each sampled with t^2 bits. The
complete key sampler has the worst-case primitive fair-bit query bound

  n + n*S*t^2 + S*t^2*(lambda+3).

coinBound_sampleIID proves the structural independent-product query bound.
coinBound_keygen applies it to the real completed public-table/control output,
with every query constrained to the primitive fair-bit oracle. The existing
exact polynomial for S yields coinBudgetPolynomial, whose evaluation equals
this entire query budget for every parameter.

The endpoint is distributionally exact: drawsSampler_evalDist identifies the
joint draw law, and keygen_evalDist identifies the completed public key AND
retained binary secret with the canonical security family's actual keygen law.
No context, control, error or shared-key correlation is discarded. Consequently
any downstream continuation can use the same keygen distribution equality
without a statistical replacement loss. The canonical scheme specification was
not changed; this is an exactly equivalent operational sampler.

The structural query predicate measures randomness queries only. It does not
charge the deterministic vector indexing, matrix arithmetic, modular arithmetic,
control materialization, public encryption, or evaluator. Strict machine-level
polynomial costs and PPT reduction closure remain separate requirements. The
cubic-control computational replacement also remains unbounded. These results
introduce no binary-LWE, KDM, leakage or circular-security hardness premise.
The original ordinary-LWE-only, one-secret-key bootstrappable-FHE goal remains
active and unfinished.

Completion audit: unresolved same-key security reduction, 2026-10-08
------------------------------------------------------------------
The original objective is unchanged and has not been achieved. The current
security approach is blocked at a mathematical reduction, rather than a Lean
build error. This same obstruction remains after the last several checked
correctness, complete-view hybrid and sampler developments. No compatible
argument was obtained from the primary-source rechecks below. Additional cost
bookkeeping alone would leave this security obligation unchanged.

The concrete outstanding term is defined at
FormalProof4FHE/LWE/RecursiveFeatureControlPolynomial.lean:

  familyControlReplacementAdvantage parameter adversary.

family_oneTime_abs_signedAdvantage_le includes this term additively alongside
the explicit quadratic ordinary-LWE reductions, the negligible finite-sampler
implementation loss and the negligible masking loss. Searching the project
finds definitions and uses of this term, but no theorem bounding it negligibly.
The comparison retains exactly the constant, linear and repeated-index control
messages; the distinct-triple cubic messages are the outstanding replacement.
All controls and the public table share the same sampled hidden binary key.
Replacing one marginal without preserving this joint law does not discharge
the bound.

Rechecked evidence:

* BJTW26, Theorem 4.1 and Section 1.2: the conclusion is search hardness;
  the theorem uses a prime modulus and quantitative entropy/norm/sample
  requirements. Its M-SIS step can instead use the statistical branch of
  Lemma 4.3, whose loss must then be bounded. The permuted HNF formulation
  also requires attention for distributions without permutation invariance.
  This is not an instantiated decisional theorem for the actual cubic view.
  Source: https://eprint.iacr.org/2025/1472 and the author-hosted full PDF
  already stored in refs/leakage-and-entropy/BJTW26.pdf.
* LSW25: the checked leakage theorem concerns bounded-norm linear leakage
  under its specified timing and source law. No conversion of the present
  nonlinear complete control table into that law has been established.
* MV24 and Micciancio25: the previously recorded linear-circular and
  composability results do not supply the required same-key cubic reduction.
  BGK11's polynomial-KDM ciphertext law has likewise not been transferred
  to the actual stored GSW controls. None of their additional premises or
  distinct-key schedules has been adopted as a solution.

Reference-version correction: the primary BJTW26 ePrint metadata reports a
2026-02-25 revision; the primary PDF still returned HTTP 403 on this recheck.
The Asia mirror supplied a 35-page PDF with that creation date and SHA-256
63d6367243af89b4f495ed9a720c64f424a54221cbb71e6f82d627c8a4a417cb.
Its extracted text is identical, page for page, to the existing author copy.
The previous catalog/index assertion about a longer latest revision was removed.
The primary-host latest PDF was not independently inspected. The retained
author PDF and its checksum are unchanged; no duplicate or new paper was needed.

To resume this security approach, the missing mathematical input is a public
simulation/reduction from the complete real-vs-comparison game to ordinary LWE,
with the actual shared-key correlations and bounded error law preserved, or a
specific alternative construction whose corresponding security and correctness
laws can both be instantiated. Merely assuming this replacement negligible,
assuming polynomial KDM/leakage/circular hardness independently, or changing to
an acyclic sequence of independent keys would not meet the user's objective.
The checked quadratic-approximation obstruction rules out only its specified
fixed-key compiler/phase-error absorption route; it is not a public attack or
a theorem that every possible ordinary-LWE reduction is impossible.

Probability-one sampled shared-circuit correctness, the complete actual
IND-CPA experiment and exact polynomial fair-bit key generation remain checked.
Strict deterministic bit costs, PPT reduction closure and ideal Gaussian-source
transfer also remain unproved, in addition to the central cubic replacement.
This audit does not assert any new security theorem or enlarge the assumptions.

Resumed direct ordinary-GSW route: mixed endpoint randomization, 2026-10-08
------------------------------------------------------------------------
The user resumed the original goal after discussion of alternative approaches.
The current work investigates ordinary GSW's quadratic complete view and the
public-key-only replacement route. The earlier blocked checkpoint is historical;
the objective remains active and unfinished, with no added hardness premise.

GSWMixedRandomization proves a previously missing endpoint law for that route.
The earlier binary-key transformation handled real quadratic transcripts and
fully uniform transcripts. The proposed joint hop instead retains all real
controls while replacing only the public-key bodies. Its randomization must
therefore preserve a mixed transcript, including correlations with the masks.

mixedTranscript selects which bodies are replaced. transformUniform is a public
affine permutation of the independent uniform body tape, conditional on the
original masks. transformTranscript_mixed proves the exact pointwise identity:
real rows retarget to the flipped secret and sign-transformed errors, and
replaced bodies undergo that public permutation. Its mask map is the existing
explicit bijection, so the joint sampler is preserved with zero statistical
loss. transformTranscript_mixedView_evalDist checks the complete per-secret
distribution equality under sign-invariance of the full error tape; it does not
assume independence among those error coordinates. Scalar negation symmetry
suffices for an IID tape by the previously checked product-law theorem.

jointShape, jointWeight and replacePublic instantiate public-key rows followed
by ALL dimension*((dimension+1)*digits) original self-key GSW columns. The
public rows have zero hint; the retained control phases equal the existing
selfKeyLayout phaseMessage exactly, including signed gadget weights. The two
selection lemmas confirm that precisely the public bodies are replaced.
jointMixed_randomization_evalDist transports this whole mixed endpoint to the
flipped key. No control coordinate, mask coordinate or gadget level is dropped.

This closes a sampler-randomization ingredient, not the computational joint
replacement. It starts from a transcript that already has the real controls;
it does not generate them from an ordinary-LWE challenge. A compatible public
challenge embedding, operational stored-key/observer transport and concrete
source-law/parameter checks remain necessary. Secret rerandomization by itself
does not establish search hardness, decision hardness, or a candidate test that
separates correct and incorrect guesses. No completed security theorem is claimed.

Actual stored ordinary-GSW view and observer transport, 2026-10-08
----------------------------------------------------------------
The preceding turn made checked progress on the mixed normalized endpoint.
This continuation connects the direct ordinary-GSW experiment to the actual
materialized sampler and operational single-challenge encryption game.

GSWStoredSecurityView.maskEquiv is an explicit index bijection between one
flattened mask array and the actual separate public-key/per-control mask arrays.
It uses Fin sum/product indexing and does not enumerate possible masks or keys.
seedEquiv keeps the original binary secret. materialFromDraws_eq_storeFresh
proves pointwise equality with the public key and every control produced by
GSWSampling.materialFromDraws. No field of the adversary view is dropped.

normalizeSeedEquiv adds the exact secret-dependent GSW mask offset to private
sampling coins, with an explicit inverse that subtracts it. This is legal as a
keygen-distribution proof because the client already sampled that secret. It
does not define a public ordinary-LWE challenge transformation. The error tape
is unchanged. materialFromDraws_eq_normalizedKey and keygen_normalized_evalDist
retain BOTH the complete stored public view and the client's private binary key.
Consequently the equality remains valid for downstream secret-dependent
continuations, not merely for one public marginal.

jointPhase_eq_signal identifies every weighted phase with the existing quadratic
monomial layout, and keygen_realView_evalDist identifies actual keygen with the
complete normalized quadratic real-view sampler. The proof factors the uniform
private seed and reorders independent sampling; it adds no distributional loss,
noise, independently sampled encryption keys or computational premise.

operationalView_normalized_evalDist then forgets only the client's secret.
uniformView_normalized_evalDist transports the public-key-only replacement
while preserving the real control marginal. operationalGame_normalized_evalDist
uses the existing operationalGame/encryptInputs equivalence, so its observer
runs the actual public encryption code and retains every stored control.
keyReplacementAdvantage_normalized proves exact equality of the operational
computational gap and its normalized quadratic counterpart.

The ideal endpoint in this module is GSWMasking.uniformView normalizedView.
Its explicit identification with the separately proved mixedView sampler and a
public transformation of stored views remain further transport obligations.
Most importantly, none of these equalities supplies the real controls from an
ordinary-LWE challenge. The required compatible public challenge embedding and
negligible computational-gap bound remain open. The full ordinary-LWE-only,
one-secret, repeatably bootstrappable GSW objective remains active and unfinished.

Complete stored mixed endpoint and public bit-flip transport, 2026-10-08
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
GSWMixedStoredView closes the remaining endpoint/materialization obligations
identified by the preceding two resumed goal turns. mixedInputEquiv splits
the full uniform mask/body tape into a uniform public table, every control mask
and unused uniform control bodies. Its explicit inverse reads table entries and
reassembles the original arrays. Marginalization discards only unused uniform
coins, never an adversary-visible control. The resulting public/control input
pair is exactly independently uniform.

realControls_eq_finish proves that materialized controls depend only on their
own retained masks, the one binary secret and the exact control error entries.
mixedTranscript_eq_finish proves the joint materialization factorization.
fixedIdeal_mixed_evalDist identifies the endpoint for EVERY fixed secret, and
operationalUniform_mixed_evalDist identifies the actual stored public-key-only
replacement with the complete mixed quadratic sampler. These are equalities,
not approximations, and add no flooding noise or reduction loss.

readView reads every public and control coefficient without a secret input.
readView_storeTranscript proves that it recovers the entire flattened transcript.
storedFlip then composes this public reader, the checked public quadratic
transformation, and eager rematerialization. Its real, mixed and ideal sampler
transport laws are exact for every fixed key under scalar negation symmetry
of the IID error law. This explicit distribution condition is not a new
computational hardness assumption; no claim is made here that the particular
finite weighted error implementation satisfies it exactly. The encryption,
refresh and circuit algorithms were not changed.

Additional approach audit: functional encryption, 2026-10-08
----------------------------------------------------------
BS23 (Bitansky--Solomon, ITCS 2023) was downloaded from the publisher into
refs/alternative-assumptions/BS23.pdf and registered in the reference catalog.
Its theorem avoids circular-security assumptions using semi-compact public-key
functional encryption and an adversary-running-time-dependent puzzle proof.
The cited FE instantiation uses LPN, bilinear SXDH and shallow PRGs in addition
to the leveled LWE scheme. Section 3.1 explicitly generates an exponentially
long indexed chain of underlying encryption keys from a DPRF seed, and
evaluation changes the underlying key index. It fails BOTH the ordinary-LWE-only
and unchanged-underlying-secret/no-acyclic-chain constraints. It is a comparison
and proof-technique reference, not a solution to the requested goal.

Remaining directions under the original constraints are research proposals:
(1) a new computational proof for the exact full quadratic joint GSW game,
possibly using a different reduction architecture than a public simulator;
(2) a bootstrap-compatible redesign starting from an LWE-proved KDM scheme,
with a public ciphertext conversion, complete joint-view proof and a refresh
noise budget that closes under the same fixed secret and parameters.
Neither direction has a completed construction or ordinary-LWE reduction here.
The checked distribution transports alone do not supply either missing theorem.


KDM-first redesign: a bit-specific native GSW interface, 2026-10-09
----------------------------------------------------------------
The user explicitly asked to try the second remaining direction: redesign the
bootstrap interface around an LWE-proved KDM law. The previous full recursive
quadratic feature format was not simply relabeled: it still produces genuine
cubic self-key controls. This turn constructs a NEW restricted sampling law
and ciphertext interface in LWE/KDMStarGSW.lean.

The underlying one encryption key is (b,u), with b an independently sampled
binary coordinate and u a uniform vector in (ZMod q)^n. No second encryption
key, independent key chain, binary-LWE hardness premise, or KDM/circular-security
assumption is used. The decryption vector is gamma(b,u)=(1,b,u_j,b*u_j),
with exactly 2*n+2 rows. Since b*b=b, multiplication by b stays in this
feature span. In contrast, encrypting an unknown u coordinate does not have
this property. The distinction is part of the construction, not an omitted
bootstrap hypothesis. This is our checked specialization, not a claim that
a referenced paper already proves this GSW law.

For each column, let a,y be an ordinary n-dimensional LWE sample, let r and
t be independent uniform extra masks, and let alpha(b),kappa(b) be the known
affine coefficients of w*b*gamma_row(b,u). Publish all four feature blocks:

  C_const = y - b*r + kappa(b),  C_b = r,
  C_u = -a - b*t + alpha(b),     C_bu = t.

The phase is exactly w*b*gamma_row(b,u)+e. Its error is the one scalar LWE
error e, with no secret-norm or recursive error-dot-secret amplification.
Zero weights produce complete public zero columns; gadget weights on each
row produce an entire GSW control encrypting b. The full batch contains
p+(2*n+2)*levels source rows, covers BOTH the public key and every control
coefficient, and shares the same original key.

The reduction samples b itself and never receives u. Sampling this independent
key coordinate is not guessing an unknown challenge secret. It generates r,t
publicly and shifts the challenge using only b and public column metadata.
The complete tapeEquiv has an explicit public inverse. Therefore the uniform
source produces a fully uniform published view, independent of b. real_eq_source
and advantage_eq_ordinaryLWE prove an exact, lossless finite-game reduction to
zmodBatchProblem with a UNIFORM ordinary LWE secret. The native GSW quadratic
whole-view challenge embedding, which remained open earlier, is not being
assumed or silently used in this new component.

control_noise and published_control_noise connect the fresh law to the actual
existing GSW gadget. encryptInputs is public short-selector encryption from
the zero table, with its exact noise identity. The existing GSW multiply/NAND
functions operate without changing gamma(b,u) or its underlying key.
public_game_abs_advantage_le handles adaptive public message choice while
retaining the whole anchor control, and bounds IND-CPA winning advantage by
one explicit ordinary-LWE advantage plus

  ((2*n+2)*levels) * sqrt(q^(2*n+2)/2^p) / 2.

This is a finite quantitative security bound. A concrete asymptotic parameter
family, strict deterministic bit costs and formal PPT closure have not been
instantiated for this new law. keygen returns eagerly materialized public
zero-table/control vectors and exactly the original private pair (b,u). Its
public marginal is the checked security view; readStoredView_storeView proves
that no published coefficient is lost.

Executable diagnostic: KDMStarSmoke uses n=2, q=4096, radix 2, 12 levels,
p=2, nonbinary u=(173,2981), dense masks, and signed unit errors. For all four
(anchor,plaintext) bit pairs it checks every control phase error, public
encryption decoding, and subsequent same-format GSW NAND decoding after
eager storage and public readback. These tiny values are correctness checks,
not cryptographic parameters, and this diagnostic does NOT run bootstrapping.

Remaining full-key bootstrap obligation
---------------------------------------
This is a positive restricted component, not the requested FHE proof. The
security reduction knows only the independently generated anchor b. The
unknown vector u remains necessary for decryption. Publishing controls for all
of its coordinates/bits changes the message/source law; the current affine
source transformation does not generate them. A separate public conversion
or compatible KDM mechanism must supply those controls in the same format
and jointly with the existing table and anchor control. Moving a successful
one-bit format to every unknown key bit is not justified by multiplying
separate marginal security bounds.

Applying the earlier full recursive quadratic compiler to those missing
messages would again change the decryption features, so the earlier cubic
closure issue must not be hidden under this new format. The original
ordinary-LWE-only, unchanged-secret, repeatable bootstrapping goal remains
active and unfinished after the user's explicit resumption. This fresh attempt
has not established impossibility or exhausted alternative interface designs.

Quadratic-to-star conversion attempt, 2026-10-09
------------------------------------------------
KDMStarConversion checks the next concrete bridge in the KDM-first redesign.
For any fixed anchor b and fixed public output column, its decryption phase is
constant plus a linear function of the unknown vector u. phase_dropQuadratic
computes the exact discrepancy of simply deleting a RecursiveQuadratic matrix:
it adds u^T T u to the original decrypted phase.

A more general, even nonlinear ciphertext-only map cannot universally repair
this. canonical has T_01=-w, other coefficients zero, and decrypts as w*u_0*u_1.
canonical_is_fresh proves this same public ciphertext is an honest zero-error
RecursiveMultiplication encryption of the quadratic KDM polynomial on every
key, using explicitly specified private coins. The four binary key corners have
mixed second difference w. Every fixed star output has mixed second difference
zero. no_exact_public_converter therefore excludes any deterministic
ciphertext-only phase-preserving converter on all keys and input ciphertexts.
For ZMod q, exists_bad_conversion_corner bounds the approximate case: if
4*B < abs(centered(w)), at least one corner has phase discrepancy greater than B.

Scope matters: no probability claim is made about these special private coins.
These are not proofs of an average-case cryptographic attack or impossibility
of distribution-specific conversion. A converter with auxiliary public data
varying with the key is also not covered. The genuine remaining construction
problem is a jointly ordinary-LWE-secure auxiliary interface that supplies all
unknown-key controls while preserving the same key, an executable refresh
circuit and usable noise bounds. That obligation is still unproved.

Literature cross-check: alternative KDM and funcCPA interfaces
-------------------------------------------------------------
Applebaum11, Section 5, Proposition 5.1, obtains circular-secure FHE from
SIMULATABLE FULL KDM security. Its simulator is indistinguishable even with the
secret key available; this is not supplied by a bounded/length-dependent KDM
reduction. Proposition 5.2 uses a KDM oracle for NAND-decryption functions to
amplify to full KDM; that oracle is not a public homomorphic evaluator. These
results do not close the bootstrap circuit/format/noise fixed point here.

DHW23, Security with Functional Re-encryption from CPA, proves a black-box
CPA-to-funcCPA compiler. Its functional re-encryption oracle decrypts input
ciphertexts and encrypts a chosen function of their messages. Security in that
oracle experiment does not supply a public re-encryption algorithm. Its
compiler uses codeword checks and encryptions under multiple keys; no transfer
to the required GSW homomorphic or same-underlying-secret interface is proved.

AGHV25, Achievable CCA2 Relaxation for Homomorphic Encryption, Sections 3.3-3.4,
is especially relevant: its CPA-to-leveled-funcCPA theorem requires independent
level keys, and its unlevelled funcCPA-to-weak-circular theorem requires
oblivious secret-key extraction and the corresponding function family. The
paper presents this implication as a barrier to proving funcCPA for existing
HE schemes. It does not establish the missing same-key funcCPA premise from
ordinary LWE. Both primary publisher PDFs are now in refs/surveys-and-boundaries,
with identity, page count, SHA256 and bibliography/catalog/index entries checked.
The original full bootstrappable-GSW goal remains active and unfinished.

KDM-first completion and auxiliary-interface audit, 2026-10-09
-------------------------------------------------------------
The preceding turn was progress: it added and checked the universal
quadratic-to-star conversion boundary and reviewed two additional primary
papers. The central missing full-key ordinary-LWE reduction nevertheless
persisted through the initial resumed anchor construction, the conversion
attempt, and this auxiliary-interface audit (three consecutive resumed turns).

Authoritative completion requirements and current evidence:
* One unchanged underlying secret: KDMStarGSW.keygen returns exactly (b,u),
  with stored coefficients identified by keygen_public_eq. The restricted
  component meets this requirement; a complete repeatable scheme is absent.
* Security of the whole public-key/evaluation-control view from ordinary LWE:
  KDMStarGSW.advantage_eq_ordinaryLWE and public_game_abs_advantage_le cover
  joinedPlan, whose only nonzero self-key message is weight*b*feature(b,u).
  They do not cover controls for coordinates or digit bits of u. The earlier
  RecursiveFeatureControlPolynomial.family_oneTime_abs_signedAdvantage_le
  still explicitly contains the unbounded familyControlReplacementAdvantage.
* Repeatable public bootstrap: the recursive-feature construction has checked
  same-secret correctness (RecursiveFeatureFHECorrectness), but its security
  gap is open. KDMStarGSW has encryption and GSW operation identities and the
  small executable diagnostic; it has no full-key refresh circuit or proof.
  Security of one format and correctness of another do not compose by merely
  retaining the original secret name.
* No independent circular/KDM/leakage/binary-LWE premise and no acyclic
  independent-key schedule: no such replacement premise has been adopted.
  The reviewed alternative compiler results do not supply an instance that
  simultaneously preserves these constraints and the required GSW interface.
* Compatible asymptotic noise/size parameters and efficient reductions:
  the new finite bound is explicit, but its full bootstrap parameter family
  and formal PPT/sampling cost closure remain uninstantiated.
* Lean verification: the last full build is terminal and successful (9,233
  jobs); the standalone audit is terminal and successful (720 declarations).
  Those checks prove the written restricted theorems, not missing security.

Auxiliary public data can escape the fixed-output conversion obstruction, so
it is not ruled out. However, it must be part of one complete real/ideal
transcript reduced to an ordinary LWE challenge. Separate marginal security
or declaring an extra encrypted-secret hint secure does not provide that
joint source transformation. If the auxiliary law itself is produced by the
existing anchor simulation, its downstream behavior remains covered only
for that simulated view, which contains no unknown-key controls. No checked
public map from that view to correctly refreshing full-key controls has been
found. The earlier fixed affine feature-closure theorem and quadratic/cubic
format boundaries explain why the attempted direct bridges fail; they do
not prove that every possible reduction or redesigned FHE scheme must fail.

No further justified construction or ordinary-LWE reduction is available
from the current worktree and reviewed papers. The agent is at an impasse
on the same mathematical blocker. Resumption requires a new compatible
joint reduction/construction, either developed in a new research attempt or
provided by new evidence. Additional bookkeeping or assuming the unresolved
hop negligible would not move the full objective toward completion. The
original objective remains unfinished; the blocked status is an operational
assessment of this attempt, not a global mathematical impossibility claim.

Rethinking remaining architectures, 2026-10-09
---------------------------------------------
The user asked to rethink the remaining possibilities. The preceding failures
are local to specified sampler, feature and conversion interfaces; they do not
justify declaring all other ordinary-LWE reductions exhausted. Three distinct
research directions remain, with concrete tests before further bookkeeping.

1. Prove the actual challenge game directly with real evaluation data retained.
The objective is secrecy of an adaptively chosen plaintext given (pk,BK), not
necessarily pseudorandomness of BK or a successful uniform-public-key hybrid.
GSWMasking.game already defines that objective with the actual joint sampler.
Its current sufficient bound charges a public-key/context replacement hop.
That hop is one proof architecture, not a necessary property of secure FHE.
A new challenge-specific lossy/source hybrid or an adversary-to-LWE reduction
could target the game directly and keep the true BK on both sides. This must
PROVE weak circular/augmented-view IND-CPA security from ordinary LWE; assuming
that weak property independently would not meet the objective.

First test: identify a source hybrid for the entire challenge transcript and
an explicit efficient reduction, not only a key-conditioned LWE game. A lossy
matrix argument also needs the entropy/error law of the COMPLETE correlated
view. BL14 (Berkoff--Liu), the evaluation-key discussion preceding Section 5,
explicitly explains why its leveled-GSW conditional-entropy proof does not
handle the extra secret-dependent evaluation-key term. Thus the existing
entropy lemma alone is not a missing one-line fix. The bit-flip transports in
this repository may support a new extraction argument, but do not by themselves
produce samples needed to invoke a distinguisher on an ordinary-LWE challenge.

2. Correlate the native controls using short quadratic preimages.
This is a new explicit algebraic candidate, distinct from expanding the
secret-feature vector or converting a previously fixed quadratic ciphertext.
Let the source contain one retained ordinary LWE block

  y = A^T*s + e,          A : n by m,

and one independent same-secret source row (a0,y0) per control column, with

  y0 = a0^T*s + e0.

For public matrices B_j(A) : n by m and public linear/constant coefficients,
publish the complete native-format columns

  a_j = a0_j - linear_j - B_j(A)*y,
  b_j = y0_j + offset_j.

Their exact phases are

  b_j - s^T*a_j
    = linear_j^T*s + offset_j + s^T*B_j(A)*A^T*s
      + e0_j + s^T*B_j(A)*e.

The retained base block and all transformed columns form an explicit public
triangular shear of ONE source transcript. For fixed base data its inverse
adds the same mask correction and subtracts the offset. It is a bijection of
the full tape, so the ideal endpoint remains fully uniform. The builder never
receives s; there is no unknown-secret guessing or separately assumed KDM law.
GSWQuadraticPreimage formalizes this public equivalence, the exact phase/noise
identity, and a lossless finite-view reduction to ordinary uniform-secret
zmodBatchProblem with n dimensions and m+k source samples. Those theorems hold
for ANY builder and do not assert a suitable builder exists or runs in PPT.

The desired builder must satisfy, for every relevant gadget target Q_j,

  s^T*B_j(A)*A^T*s = s^T*Q_j*s.

A public coefficient certificate is equality of diagonal coefficients and of
all off-diagonal pair sums. Equivalently, B_j(A)*A^T-Q_j is alternating: zero
diagonal and opposite off-diagonal entries. This avoids division by two, even
at power-of-two moduli. Native key (1,-s) uses the linear target w*s_i at the
body row, and quadratic target -w*s_i*s_r at mask row r. No cubic target is
introduced because the decryption features remain those of ordinary GSW.

The open question is efficient generation of all these SMALL B_j for a truly
ordinary random A, with negligible failure and a polynomial source length.
Gaussian elimination produces modular preimages but gives no useful centered
norm bound. A standard secret lattice trapdoor cannot be supplied for free:
the joint source/trapdoor experiment would need its own reduction to ordinary
LWE. A public collection of short ordinary linear gadget-basis preimages also
allows an attacker to apply them to the retained y and round secret-coordinate
values when its noise bound is small enough; that is an attack filter, not a
solution. The alternating quadratic freedom is the specific new degree of
freedom to examine, rather than silently giving the source a linear trapdoor.

For source secrets bounded in centered magnitude by S, B entries by H, and
source errors by E, the added phase error is at most n*m*S*H*E, plus e0. This
is a quantitative proposal, not a newly checked noise-bound theorem. A binary
secret gives S=1, but the proved uniform-secret source theorem does not permit
replacing S by 1. A complete construction must separately DERIVE its usable
small-secret LWE law from ordinary LWE and then instantiate the joint reduction
and same-key bootstrap noise budget. No binary-LWE assumption is adopted.

First tests: try to build the alternating quadratic preimages in polynomial
time, determine whether that task implies a prohibited public inversion of
LWE, and quantify H versus the largest gadget weights before choosing final
parameters. Randomized public builders can be analyzed conditional on their
public randomness; such an extension is not yet formalized. Private or leaked
trapdoors, conditioned source matrices and shared shifts cannot be ignored.

An exhaustive two-dimensional experiment, q=17, m=5, seed=417, found B entries
in {-1,0,1} for all 12 selected positive gadget targets (weights 1,2,4,8 and
three quadratic monomials). It checked 11,664 phase/error cases covering every
binary secret and every signed unit-error vector. Negative targets follow by
negating the witnesses. The search covers only 4,335 of 4,913 possible quadratic
coefficient classes for this one matrix, so it does not prove universal
existence. Its enumeration uses 3^(2*m) candidate pairs and is exponential.
This is finite algebraic feasibility, NOT cryptographic security, a scalable
sampler, decryption correctness or a successful bootstrap experiment.
Reproduce with:
  python3 scripts/explore-gsw-quadratic-preimages.py \
    --output docs/GSWQuadraticPreimageExploration.json

3. Redesign the refresh interface rather than publish all standalone controls.
The earlier affine-closure theorem assumes one fixed feature span supports
all original-key-bit/feature products. The conversion theorem assumes a fixed
ciphertext-only output preserves phases for all keys and input coins. Neither
rules out a jointly secure family of formats with auxiliary-dependent public
transitions, or a purpose-built reusable evaluator for the fixed refresh
circuit. All underlying encryption secrets must remain the original secret;
renaming an independent chain as one master key is not sufficient.

A concrete specification would provide public Refresh(aux,ct), output under
the same secret, preserve the plaintext for honest supported ciphertexts,
return to one reusable noise/size bound, and reduce IND-CPA with aux to ordinary
LWE. It need not expose generic GSW controls for every secret bit. Building it
from KDM/garbling is still speculative: bounded KDM gives the key holder an
oracle, not a public evaluator; one-use garbling is not reusable refresh;
function keys that reveal a hardwired decryption secret are unsuitable. LLW21
provides reusable output-homomorphic correlated-source extractors and bounded
KDM constructions, but not this public refresh interface. Its LWE homomorphism
is a transformation for specified linear extractor outputs, not arbitrary
multiplication of GSW ciphertexts.

New-source cross-check and research choice
------------------------------------------
BL14 and LLW21 primary publisher PDFs were downloaded and registered in refs.
A recent claim of circular-security-free QFHE (arXiv:2604.23490v2) was checked
against its actual KeyGen and Section 3.4: it uses L+1 independent classical key
layers and changes the active layer. It does not meet the unchanged-secret or
no-acyclic-scheduling constraints, irrespective of its abstract phrasing.
WWW25 (Waters--Wee--Wu), New Techniques for Preimage Sampling, is a relevant
follow-up tool: its public trapdoor solves shifted multi-preimage sampling for
jointly generated structured matrices. That is not the required ordinary-random
A quadratic builder; shifts, matrix law and joint security must be reconciled
before using it. No transfer is claimed here.

The correlated quadratic-preimage direction is the most concrete new experiment:
the source transformation and phase identity can be checked immediately, while
the genuinely difficult sampler is stated explicitly. Challenge-specific
security is the next independent architecture if a practical short builder
cannot be constructed. All three directions preserve the original objective
as research targets. None is a completed ordinary-LWE-only FHE theorem.

WWW25 compatibility filter after reading the full author version
----------------------------------------------------------------
WWW25's source matrices have form A_i=B-u_i tensor G. Its hardness guarantee
is for ANY ONE chosen A_i with public setup/trapdoor retained (somewhere
programmability), not a joint batch under one identical unknown secret for
all these matrices. This distinction is decisive for the proposed use here.
Suppose public preimages satisfy A_i*pi_i=t_i+c and A_j*pi_j=t_j+c, while
both source batches have the SAME secret s:

  y_i=A_i^T*s+e_i,     y_j=A_j^T*s+e_j.

Then direct algebra gives the PUBLIC quantity

  pi_i^T*y_i - pi_j^T*y_j
    = (t_i-t_j)^T*s + pi_i^T*e_i - pi_j^T*e_j.

The shared shift cancels. If chosen target differences are well-separated
encodings of binary secret coordinates and the two projected errors are small
enough for their decoder, this quantity reveals those coordinates. Thus a
naive simultaneous same-secret use cannot be justified by multiplying the
paper's individual hardness guarantees. The current source-equivalence proof
requires a builder using ordinary random A alone; no public multi-matrix
trapdoor has been substituted for it. WWW25 remains a construction-technique
reference for a more constrained quadratic/alternating interface, not a closed
joint ordinary-LWE proof for this candidate. The 46-page author-hosted full
version is downloaded and registered, bringing the reference collection to 37.

Verification: rethought correlated-control interface, 2026-10-09
----------------------------------------------------------------
Standalone GSWQuadraticPreimage with warningAsError=true exited zero.
Both library roots built successfully in the validated mirror (9,234 jobs).
The standalone warningAsError axiom audit exited zero; all 733 selected
declarations, including 13 new source-transformation definitions/theorems,
have dependencies contained in propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound.
The final module, full-build and standalone-audit logs contain no Lean errors,
warnings or sorryAx. All 458 distinct project Lean/configuration files
byte-match the validated mirror, and git diff --check passes. The reproducible
two-coordinate enumeration found 12/12 selected short witnesses and passed
11,664 phase/error identities. All 37 downloaded PDFs match catalog sizes and
SHA256 hashes. These checks do not construct a scalable short builder, transfer
small-secret hardness from ordinary LWE, instantiate control noise parameters,
or prove reusable bootstrapping security. The full objective is unfinished.


Trying all three remaining routes, 2026-10-09
--------------------------------------------
The user authorized actual attempts on all three architectures. The goal was
resumed; the older blocked KDM-first audit is historical. This is the first
turn of the new resumed audit, not a new completed or blocked goal.

1. Direct challenge game: try a lossy matrix endpoint with narrow real controls.
The explicit test replaces every mask by a rank-zero lossy mask but keeps the
native body-gadget message (q/2)*s_i plus signed unit error. A public nearest
center decoder recovers every secret bit, then decrypts both challenge branches.
The exhaustive q=256, n=4 calculation covers all 16 binary keys, all 81 control
error vectors and both plaintext bits: 2,592 perfect recovery cases. Thus this
particular statistical endpoint cannot hide the challenge. It explains why
simply retaining narrow real key-dependent bodies in a lossy source does not
provide the hoped-for entropy argument. It does NOT refute a direct
computational challenge reduction, other lossy joint laws, or the existing
operational real game. No successful challenge-specific source embedding was
found in this attempt.

2. Correlated native controls: implement a lattice-based public builder.
Vectorize B. For each (i,j), i<=j, require diagonal (BA^T)_ii=Q_ii or
(BA^T)_ij+(BA^T)_ji=Q_ij+Q_ji. These are n(n+1)/2 modular linear constraints
in n*m variables, with the same correlated A in each block. The implemented
builder uses unit-pivot modular elimination to produce a particular solution
and the full integer kernel lattice. It reduces that lattice with fpylll 0.6.4
LLL and subtracts a Babai nearest-plane lattice vector from the particular
solution. Neither the builder nor its lattice computation receives the secret
or source errors. Unit-pivot failure is explicit; there is no conditioning by
resampling A. Every kernel generator and every returned preimage is checked
exactly. This is an actual heuristic implementation, not a proof of a useful
shortness guarantee or a formally verified polynomial-time implementation.

For every returned target the script also builds U and checks exactly

  B*A^T = Q + U - U^T mod q.

This certificate avoids division by two. Lean proves
quadratic_eq_of_certificate over ANY commutative ring, so the certificate
implies s^T*B*A^T*s=s^T*Q*s even at power-of-two moduli. The integer lemmas
residual_abs_le_l1 and total_error_abs_le_l1 prove the actual error bound

  |e0+s^T*B*e| <= E0 + S*E*sum_(r,c) |B_rc|.

A bound for an integer lift also bounds the centered modular representative;
the final centered-noise/bootstrap instantiation is not supplied here.

The eight reproducible matrices and worst returned l1 norms are:
  n=2, m=8,  q=257,   seed=417:  27 targets, l1=12;
  n=3, m=12, q=257,   seed=418:  54 targets, l1=24;
  n=4, m=16, q=257,   seed=419:  90 targets, l1=35;
  n=4, m=16, q=65537, seed=420: 170 targets, l1=136;
  n=6, m=24, q=65537, seed=421: 357 targets, l1=248;
  n=4, m=16, q=256,   seed=422:  80 targets, l1=38;
  n=8, m=8,  q=257,   seed=423: 324 targets, l1=623;
  n=8, m=8,  q=65537, seed=424: 612 targets, l1=14976.
Total: 1,714 exact quadratic targets and alternating certificates. Targets are
all coordinate monomials and powers of two below q. Negative targets use -B.
The JSON retains each A, target norms, and the worst full B/U certificate.

The security filter is essential. Independently apply the same lattice method
to ordinary LINEAR preimages A*pi=(floor(q/2))*e_i. On the first six matrices,
all 23 coordinate witnesses have l1 norm below half the encoded-bit separation.
Consequently pi^T*y recovers every binary key coordinate for EVERY signed
unit-error vector, not only the explicitly sampled keys/errors. This proves
those toy source instances insecure in the tested noise law. On the two denser
matrices that sufficient recovery certificate does not hold; this is neither a
security proof nor a claim that no other key-recovery attack exists.

Noise also changes substantially at density m=n. At n=m=8,q=257, the worst
fresh error bound is 624, exceeding the quarter-modulus margin 64.25. At
q=65537 the analogous bound 14977 is below 16384.25, but gives no post-evaluation
or repeated-refresh noise invariant, and the binary key has only 8 bits of
entropy. No cryptographic claim is made about any of these finite dimensions.

The small-secret source transfer is no longer a wholly missing finite step.
GSWQuadraticPreimageBinary applies the same full-tape shear to BlockBinary's
honest source law. advantage_eq_blockBinary is exact; advantage_le_ordinaryLWE
then composes the EXISTING ordinary-uniform-secret weak-source theorem with
this reduction. Its ordinaryLoss contains both sums of narrow-error ordinary
LWE row advantages, one wide-error ordinary LWE advantage, the explicit
noiseAbsorptionGap, and sqrt(q^d/(b+1)^n)/2. There is no independently assumed
binary-LWE or KDM/circular hardness premise. For b=1 the source is n independent
binary key bits; both blocks and all controls use that one same key. The wide
error law used by this reduction remains present in correctness.

For b=1 a sufficient entropy condition for negligible loss at level lambda is
n >= d*log2(q)+2*lambda. Increasing q solely to make a lattice witness decode
therefore increases the required binary dimension. Every toy case above is
far outside a cryptographically useful instantiation of that bound. The
standard worst-case LLL/Babai approximation guarantee is exponential in the
n*m lattice dimension, and does not establish the desired polynomial l1 bound
in a source/noise parameter family. No average-case shortcut, negligible
failure theorem, or bootstrap-compatible asymptotic norm guarantee is proved.
The constraints are a correlated quadratic coefficient system; no ordinary
SIS hardness theorem for this exact system is asserted.

3. Specialized same-key refresh: attempt a cycle of anchor formats.
Use star_i features consisting of 1, all s_j, and all s_i*s_j. All five formats
in the n=5 diagnostic share the exact same binary key; there are no independent
keys or acyclic schedule. Try the natural GSW-control transition to star_(i+1).
Every missing source feature would need control phases containing its product
with the receiving decryption features. Boolean products reduce duplicate
indices, but the required sets still include degree-three AND degree-four
monomials. The script enumerates all five transitions and their missing
products. Thus this proposed GSW switching compiler does not close with the
proved degree-two KDM law. This does NOT cover scalar-only switches with a new
subsequent conversion, auxiliary-dependent formats, or other refresh interfaces.

A second specialized compiler is a full key-indexed lookup for scalar decryption.
At scalar mask (q/2,...,q/2), body zero and binary key, the noiseless decoded bit
is parity. Integer multilinear Mobius inversion gives top coefficient
(-2)^(n-1), so its degree is n; the full lookup uses 2^n entries. The n=5
calculation gives coefficient 16 and 32 entries. This checks why the direct
low-degree feature lookup does not yield a polynomial construction for this
supported scalar family. It does NOT prove that every reusable circuit
compiler must publish that polynomial or that all specialized refresh is
impossible. Reusable garbling or another computational compiler remains a
separate unresolved construction.

  python3 scripts/try-gsw-remaining-routes.py \
    --output docs/GSWRemainingRoutesExploration.json
In this workspace the diagnostic dependencies are isolated under
/tmp/fhe-preimage-packages; use PYTHONPATH to select them. No dependencies were
added to the Lean project or global Python installation. The tool API was
checked against the primary fpylll documentation:
  https://fpylll.readthedocs.io/en/master/modules.html
CVP.babai returns a lattice vector; GSO.babai returns coefficient coordinates.
The exact modular checks guard this convention explicitly.

Both library roots build successfully in the mirror (9,235 jobs). The two
changed/new Lean modules pass standalone warningAsError=true checks. The
expanded warningAsError axiom audit passes all 741 selected declarations,
including eight new certificate/noise/source declarations, with only the
standard foundational axioms. The full FHE security objective remains unfinished:
there is no secure parameter family proving useful builder norms/failure,
stored native complete control materialization and its adaptive IND-CPA bound,

specialized attempts above fail specific constructions, not their entire
