and is not marked blocked or complete on its first turn.


Native sampler and parameter compatibility continuation, 2026-10-09
-----------------------------------------------------------------
Classification of the preceding goal turn: PROGRESS. It changed authoritative
Lean state, closed the finite small-secret reduction, and produced exact
construction/attack evidence. This continuation advances the same full FHE
goal. It does not replace success with a conditional theorem or a toy experiment.

The current source transformation was not yet an operational GSW key sampler.
GSWQuadraticPreimageGSW now supplies that bridge. For every flattened native
control column, linear(params) encodes the body target w*s_i and quadratic(params)
encodes the mask target -w*s_i*s_r. target_eq_phaseMessage checks their sum
against the repository's existing full self-key gadget layout. All n secret
coordinates have a complete (n+1)*levels GSW matrix; there is no anchor-only
restriction, secret-feature expansion or new key layer.

The source batch has exactly p+n*(n+1)*levels rows under ONE compact block-binary
key. The builder sees only the retained n-by-p mask A. Body-first serialization,
control-block reindexing and the public-key transpose are explicit equivalences
of the full tape, independent of the secret. keygen draws that ordinary source
batch, applies the public shear, and eagerly stores the public key and every
control as finite vectors. readStoredView_storeView and keygen_public_eq connect
this actual materialization to the security distribution. The client retains
the original compact key; the evaluator has only the stored public data.

For an actual sampled base matrix A, the public certificate obligation is

  B_j*A^T = Q_j + U_j - U_j^T.

It is a condition on that sampled matrix, not a false assertion that every
matrix (including A=0) has these preimages. control_noise proves that the native
GSW error is EXACTLY e0_j+s^T*B_j*e, relative to plaintext s_i and the existing
gadget. control_noiseBound and noiseBound_bootstrap allow that same correlated
control tape in the existing reusable refresh algorithm, with the explicit
residual bound. Independence of the errors is unnecessary for correctness.

The finite security theorem now targets the actual operational plaintext game.
storedGame uses keygen's stored public key and entire vector of stored controls;
the adversary chooses its two plaintexts after seeing BOTH. The challenge is
actual GSW public encryption with fresh binary selection coins. A stored-view
adversary lift and exact storedGame_eq identify it with the native matrix game.
stored_game_abs_advantage_le bounds its absolute winning advantage by

  ordinaryLoss(B,linear,0,d,narrow,wide,actual game continuation)
  + ((n+1)*levels) * sqrt(q^(n+1)/2^p)/2.

ordinaryLoss is the already checked ordinary-uniform-secret weak-source
reduction, retaining every wide-error and entropy cost. There is no unbounded
joint-key-replacement term left in this NEW sampler's finite bound, and no
separately assumed circular, KDM or binary-LWE hardness premise. The theorem
holds for any public B, even B=0, which is secure at the transcript level but
fails the mask-target correctness certificates. Thus the bound by itself does
NOT prove a correct FHE instantiation. The stock independently sampled control
law and the earlier recursive-feature cubic gap are unchanged.

Parameter compatibility is a distinct, now checked question.
GSWQuadraticPreimageParameters uses the existing family t=lambda+1,
source dimension d=t^3, binary dimension N=2*t^5, q=2^(t^2), wide source error
radius E=2^t, and base-two gadget with t^2 levels. It reuses the previous
power-of-two lookup of polynomial size (between 8 and 16 times N^2+N+1), but
its CIPHERTEXT decryption rows remain N+1. Quadratic secret features are NOT
introduced by using this public lookup size.

For ANY fixed natural-coefficient polynomial P, set

  control K=(1+P(lambda))*E,
  G=(N+1)*t^2,
  output R=lookup*N*G*K,
  post-NAND M=(G+1)*R,
  p=G+2*lambda+1.

The integer l1 residual estimate motivates K when the actual builder has l1
norm at most P(lambda), binary s and source errors bounded by E. No theorem
here asserts that any efficient builder achieves that P. budget_le_polynomial
bounds lookup*M by an explicit polynomial times E. eventually_budget proves
lookup*M<q eventually for every fixed P, using the existing negligible
polynomial-times-2^(-lambda) theorem. capacity_of_budget, rounding_margin and
output_margin then prove the exact modulus-switch and decoding inequalities.
noiseBound_refreshedNand resets a NAND of two R-bounded ciphertexts to R, and
decode_refreshedNand proves the correct Boolean output. The same controls and
same original binary secret are reused at every step. Repeated reuse follows
by induction on the number of gates once the actual controls meet K.

publicEncryptionBound_le_outputBound places honest public input encryption
(p source errors each bounded by E) inside R. masking_cardinality_margin proves
q^(N+1)*2^(2*lambda)<=2^p, and entropy_cardinality_margin retains
q^d*2^(2*lambda)<=2^N. Thus increasing q and N together in this family can
accommodate wide errors and ANY polynomial residual growth. The toy
noise/entropy tradeoff from the previous turn is NOT a proof that no parameter
family is compatible. The missing item is a sampler/building algorithm with
that shortness and negligible failure in this family, followed by full
asymptotic sampling/reduction/runtime accounting.

Alternative source check: ACPS09 Section 3.1, Lemma 2, gives ordinary-LWE
Hermite-normal-form secret distribution equal to the error distribution,
avoiding this binary weak-source flooding step. The local primary paper
refs/kdm/ACPS09.pdf was inspected. It yields Gaussian/error-distributed key
coordinates rather than binary coordinates. The current public binary
accumulator would require encrypted digits/predicates of those coordinates;
the quadratic builder alone does not provide their native phase products.
No binary-error LWE assumption or unproved Gaussian-digit transfer is adopted.
This is an alternative source architecture, not a completed replacement.

The central algorithmic research target is now explicit: on ordinary uniform
n-by-p A, construct all prescribed B_j with division-free certificates and
polynomial integer l1 norm, efficiently, with negligible failure, in a family
meeting the checked source losses and noise margins. The current monolithic
LLL/Babai heuristic does not have that guarantee. Its generic worst-case
approximation factor is exponential in n*p, much larger than this family's
modulus exponent. A next concrete algorithmic experiment can decouple the
quadratic equations row by row: when constructing row i, prescribe its products
with A_0,...,A_i using the already constructed rows' off-diagonal contributions.
This uses cached prefix linear-preimage lattices of dimension p, instead of
one n*p lattice. It still has no proven useful norm guarantee, and must be
subjected to the same linear-key-recovery filter rather than presented as a
solution in advance.

Row-by-row builders, public inversion filters, and binary diagonal elimination
============================================================================

The proposed prefix method was implemented and tried, including two repairs.
scripts/try-gsw-rowwise-preimages.py reuses the exact public modular kernel
lattice and LLL/Babai machinery of try-gsw-remaining-routes.py. Each prefix
A_0,...,A_i has one cached p-dimensional lattice; no secret, error, private
trapdoor or matrix resampling is supplied. After earlier rows B_j are fixed,
row i solves

  A_j . B_i = Q_ij + Q_ji - B_j . A_i  (j<i),
  A_i . B_i = Q_ii.

This constructs the division-free certificate B*A^T=Q+U-U^T, checked exactly
for every output. Three variants were tried: zero-preserving prefixes; the
same short homogeneous first-prefix vector added for every target; and a
public target-dependent choice of a short homogeneous first-prefix vector.
The last choice uses a deterministic hash solely to select among public basis
vectors; no hash security assumption is made. The homogeneous vectors come
from the reduced prefix kernel itself, avoiding the large two-column seed
used during preliminary debugging. The reusable solver only gained optional
access to its already public reduced kernel basis; its existing callers and
previous results remain unchanged.

The final diagnostics in docs/GSWRowwisePreimageExploration.json cover three
variants on each of seven matrices, with seeds 417 through 423. For n<=8 they
check all i<=j targets and all positive power-of-two weights below the listed
prime modulus. At n=16 they check six targets. These are finite algebraic
experiments, not this project's power-of-two asymptotic parameter family.
All 5,523 counted targets pass their exact certificates. Additional zero,
last-diagonal, last-off-diagonal and linear attack probes are not included in
that count. Worst total integer l1 norms over the counted targets are:

  (n,p,q)          zero       shared       target-dependent
  (2,8,257)          12           14                     15
  (4,16,257)         38           39                     40
  (4,16,65537)      138          134                    136
  (8,8,257)        1248         1274                   1242
  (8,8,65537)    241046       245313                 260458
  (8,32,65537)     438          455                    462
  (16,64,65537)   1987         2088                   2066

A useful binary-specific correction was found during the attack analysis.
GSWQuadraticPreimageGSW now uses s_i^2=s_i: body columns have +w*s_i,
diagonal mask columns have -w*s_i, and only off-diagonal mask columns retain
-w*s_i*s_j. quadratic_eq_zero_of_body and quadratic_eq_zero_of_diagonal
prove those targets are zero. target_eq_phaseMessage and control_noise now
explicitly require coordinate idempotence; noiseBound_bootstrap derives it
from binaryEmbed_idempotent. This is a property of the existing source, not
a new hardness assumption. The complete stored view, public triangular
bijection and finite adaptive game bound still apply to this revised affine
shift. A zero B suffices for body and diagonal columns. Consequently an
attack depending only on unnecessary diagonal preimages would not establish
a defect in this optimized binary sampler.

The remaining off-diagonal targets do expose the prefix defect.
GSWQuadraticPreimageRowSupport proves, over any commutative ring, that a
certificate supported on row i forces

  A*B_i = Q_ii*e_i + sum_(j!=i) (Q_ij+Q_ji)*e_j.

In particular Q=w*E_(j,i), j!=i, gives the ordinary linear preimage w*e_j.
If nonzero-target and zero-target certificates share all rows except i,
subtracting them gives the same result even when neither output is sparse.
The final off-diagonal probe uses Q=(q//2)*E_(0,n-1). The plain and shared
variants share their prefix with the zero target on all seven matrices;
their last-row differences have exact linear preimage certificates. The
public target-dependent variant has a different prefix in all seven cases
and is outside this shared-prefix theorem. This does not prove its security.

projection_real identifies the publicly projected body as w*s_j+pi.e.
projection_noise_bound bounds the centered projected error by H*E for an
l1-H preimage and coordinate error bound E. decode_projection and recover_real
prove exact binary decoding under the usual strict half-distance margin.
verification_phase_of_recovery gives the true error on a fresh independent
verification row. card_verification_window_le and
uniform_verification_acceptance_le bound acceptance for a uniform fresh body
by (2E+1)/q, for every fixed recovered-key shift. These are checked pointwise
and finite probability statements. No full probabilistic builder-failure or
asymptotic ordinary-LWE distinguisher theorem is asserted here.

The independent full-prefix linear solver recovers every coordinate for ALL
binary keys and ALL coordinate errors of magnitude at most one on each of
the five wider matrices: 34 coordinates per variant, 102 repeated coordinate
certificates in total. The target-dependent repair does not prevent this
independent attack on those source matrices. The two square matrices fail
this sufficient linear-decoding margin but produce large control l1 bounds;
those bounds do not meet the toy q/4 noise margin. Failure of a sufficient
noise bound is not an actual correctness-failure probability theorem.

The prefix implementation reduces lattice dimension from n*p to p, but no
useful polynomial norm guarantee for the secure source family was proved.
A generic all-right-hand-side short linear solver would also supply the
ordinary linear preimages used by the attack; such a guarantee cannot be
used as a secure shortcut without addressing that conflict. The conditional
polynomial-parameter compatibility theorem remains valid. Dense quadratic
builders with target-dependent alternating freedom remain unexcluded; the
present selection heuristic is not a proved instance of one. Other source
architectures remain open with the previously recorded joint-view gaps.

Two-target-row and proportional-row builder audit
================================================

Previous goal turn classification: PROGRESS. It changed the actual binary
native sampler, added checked row-supported/shared-prefix inversion lemmas,
and ran reproducible finite diagnostics. The present turn continues the same
ordinary-LWE-only, unchanged-secret goal; no previous blocker is being
relabelled as success.

The next cheap dense repair was tried: let both target rows participate,
with arbitrary alternating splitting of their coefficient. For each pair
(i,j), scripts/try-gsw-two-row-preimages.py solves the exact 2n-1 diagonal
and symmetric-entry equations in 2p variables, rather than an n*p lattice.
The same public kernel elimination and LLL/Babai code is used. The builder
receives ONLY A and the target weight; it never receives the diagnostic
secret or errors, uses no trapdoor, and does not resample failed matrices.
All gadget levels are checked at power-of-two moduli. Every successful output
has an exactly verified certificate B*A^T=Q+U-U^T.

The final docs/GSWTwoRowPreimageExploration.json has:

  (n,p,q), seed        checked targets  failed pairs  worst l1  recovered coordinates
  (2,8,256),517                     8             0        11  {1}
  (4,16,256),518                   48             0        21  {1,2,3}
  (4,16,65536),519                 96             0       101  {0,1,2,3}
  (8,8,256),520                   224             0       902  {}
  (8,8,65536),521                   0            28         -  {}
  (8,32,65536),522                448             0       165  {1,...,7}

All 824 counted successful targets pass exact certificates. The 28 failures
are unit-pivot elimination failures on one square source; there was no
conditioning of its source law. The attack probes use the highest weight
q/2 and exact sufficient rowwise bounds for ALL binary keys and ALL coordinate
errors of magnitude at most one. The four wider matrices have at least one
guaranteed endpoint for every pair. Diagnostic guesses agree with the sampled
secret. Their two completions include that secret and accept a fresh real
verification body. Exhaustively counting all q possible uniform bodies gives
acceptance counts 6, 6, 3 and 6, respectively, each at most 2*(2*1+1).
The successful square source has large l1 norms and no guaranteed endpoint
by this sufficient test. These are finite diagnostics, not a secure source
family or a negligible success/failure theorem.

The algebraic reason is now checked in GSWQuadraticPreimageTwoRows.
Suppose Q=w*E_(i,j), i!=j, and B is zero outside rows i,j. Put

  c=(B*A^T)_ij, d=(B*A^T)_ji.

The alternating certificate forces diagonal entries zero and c+d=w.
Every outside row of B is zero, so the symmetric entry equations also force

  A*B_i = c*e_j, A*B_j = d*e_i.

Thus B_i.y=c*s_j+B_i.e and B_j.y=d*s_i+B_j.e. Modular centered distance
satisfies distance(0,w)<=distance(0,c)+distance(0,d). If each projected error
is bounded by H and 4H<distance(0,w), at least one weight has distance greater
than 2H. readPair publicly selects that weight and recovers its corresponding
bit exactly. It depends only on A, B, y and the public noise bound, not on the
unknown secret or certificate witness. The same proof applies at even moduli;
no division by two or field hypothesis is used.

collect stores a reading for each observed coordinate. collect_sound and
collect_pair_cover prove soundness and pair coverage from the actual readings.
allPairs_two_completions integrates readPair with the actual all-pair
certificates, support and projected-error premises: completing missing
coordinates with either false or true produces a list of two keys containing
the entire original binary secret. At most one coordinate can be missing,
because a pair of distinct missing coordinates would violate pair coverage.
The proof-facing collector uses classical selection among finite readings;
a runtime-certified implementation is not asserted. Finite scanning suffices
as an algorithmic description, but formal PPT accounting remains open.

acceptsCompletions_real accepts an independent real verification row whenever
its error has the advertised radius and those observations are sound and
cover all pairs. uniform_acceptsCompletions_le bounds the probability for a
uniform fresh body by 2*(2E+1)/q. uniformVerification_le retains an ARBITRARY
earlier correlated view that produces the observations and verification mask;
only the fresh verification body must be independently uniform. Thus this
uniform bound is not a row-marginal claim that silently discards earlier
published controls. No full probabilistic ordinary-LWE attack/failure theorem
or asymptotic runtime proof is claimed in this module.

The parameter gap is also narrowed. projected_error_le_keyBound puts a
polynomial centered row norm and radius-bounded source errors inside the SAME
keyBound=(1+P(lambda))*radius used for native reusable refresh.
compatible_budget_pair_margin proves that the existing refresh budget implies
4*keyBound<distance(0,topGadgetWeight). Its proof uses outputBound>=2*keyBound
and the already checked output margin. eventually_budget supplies that budget
eventually for every fixed P. Hence a target-pair-supported builder meeting
the previous polynomial shortness proposal would also meet this public
recovery margin on every successful source input. The retained base (A,y)
is public in the candidate's complete native stored view. At the top gadget
weight in its even power-of-two modulus, the negative native mask target has
the same centered separation. Merely enlarging the modulus within this family
cannot rescue the target-pair-supported construction.

An additional dense boundary is linear_preimage_of_proportional_rows.
If every row of B is a scalar multiple of its target row i, the zero diagonal
forces every entry of column i of B*A^T to vanish. The symmetric-entry
certificate then forces A*B_i=w*e_j directly. This permits nonzero B in many
rows, unlike the earlier sparse-row theorem. Its proportionality premise
must be proved for the actual B; it is not a general rank-one theorem for
every factorization over ZMod(2^k), where nonunits matter. Neither this lemma
nor the two-row result excludes general dense target-dependent alternating
builders. Those remain the constructive gap.

Primary-source scope check during this turn: the already catalogued WHT26
publisher page still defines depth-L setup/evaluation, matching the earlier
local full-paper audit; it does not close the repeated one-secret gap.
https://link.springer.com/article/10.1186/s42400-025-00431-z
The newer QFHE candidate arXiv:2604.23490v2 explicitly uses a layered key
structure in its abstract. That does not satisfy this user's unchanged-secret
and no-acyclic-schedule constraint; no claim about its full correctness is
made from the abstract. https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23490v2
Another search candidate, ePrint 2015/466, claimed circularly secure key
switching; a subsequent primary PDF fetch returned HTTP 403. No uninspected
security theorem from that candidate is used as evidence or an assumption.
No reference files were changed this turn.

Unit-sum-mask circular-security candidate audit
==============================================

Previous goal turn classification: PROGRESS. The target-pair builder was
actually implemented, its certificates and decoding probes were checked,
and the public two-completion attack was connected to the native polynomial
refresh budget in Lean. The existential efficient dense builder and full
ordinary-LWE security obligation remained unproved throughout that turn.

ZYZW15, Zhou--Yang--Zhang--Wu, "Efficient Fully Homomorphic Encryption with
Circularly Secure Key Switching Process", ePrint 2015/466, was downloaded to
../refs/gsw/ZYZW15.pdf. The primary PDF host returned HTTP 403; the previously
used public ePrint mirror served the seven-page manuscript. All pages were
parsed and the first-page title/authors checked against canonical/publisher
metadata. Equivalence to the 2016 journal revision is not asserted. The
catalog, bibliography, index and checksum list now contain 38 papers.
Canonical source: https://eprint.iacr.org/2015/466
Publisher metadata: https://www.inderscience.com/info/e_inarticle.php?artid=80414

Paper-specific findings: its special masks have coordinate sum one.
Theorem 3.3 rewrites a key-dependent row hint g_i(s) by changing the secret
to s+g_i(s)*1. It argues row-by-row indistinguishability; the shift depends
on i and the complete switching table shares the original secret. A joint
reduction with all other real rows retained is required before applying
that argument to our target. The displayed setup takes a maximum depth L,
uses descending moduli, and Theorem 3.1 states L-leveled correctness. The
conclusion leaves application to GSW as further work. This manuscript thus
does not supply a verified same-key repeatable GSW construction here.
These are limitations of the available argument, not a cryptanalysis of
all instantiations or a claim that no alternative joint proof can exist.

The algebra was reconstructed and checked independently in
GSWUnitSumShift. For any commutative ring and mask a with sum(a)=1,

  a.s + e + g = a.(s+g*1) + e.

row_identity proves this exactly. shifted_eq_iff uses a nonempty coordinate
to show that two such shifted secrets are equal exactly when their hints
are equal. all_unitSum_masks_force_shift uses the unit coordinate masks to
prove that an arbitrary proposed target secret satisfying the identity on
ALL unit-sum masks must be s+g*1. common_shift_iff and
common_shift_requires_equal_hints therefore characterize the exact fixed
common-secret interface, without assuming that each individual shifted
key is uniform. This boundary only concerns this direct fixed-shift
compiler; it does not exclude a redesigned joint reduction.

A finite probability example pinpoints why marginal statements cannot
replace a joint proof. Let the original uniform secret be (x,y), choose
valid masks (0,1) and (1,0), and use hints x and y respectively. At zero
noise both published bodies equal x+y. conditional_bodies_eq proves the
identity, and conditionalJoint_eq_unitSum_rows identifies the exact sampler
with those rows. sumEquiv gives a public bijection (x,y)->(x+y,y), proving
each body is exactly uniform. conditionalJoint_fst_uniform and
conditionalJoint_snd_uniform are full distribution equalities. Yet
conditionalJoint_equal_probability shows Pr[body1=body2]=1, whereas
uniform_equal_probability shows it is 1/q for two independent uniform
bodies. These statements hold at arbitrary positive moduli, including odd
moduli. The example conditions on fixed valid masks and uses zero noise;
it is a logical counterexample to the marginal-to-joint inference. Its
conditioning probability and secret/error law must NOT be discarded to
claim an asymptotic attack on the original paper. No such attack is claimed.

Completion requirements were re-audited against authoritative modules:

* Actual native format and full stored public/control material: constructed
  for the correlated candidate by GSWQuadraticPreimageGSW, for an arbitrary
  public builder. The candidate differs from stock independent-control GSW.
* One unchanged binary decryption secret and repeated refresh: the actual
  phase/residual identity and reusable NAND noise invariant are checked
  conditional on the sampled controls satisfying certificates and bounds.
* Ordinary LWE only: the complete candidate view and its finite adaptive
  plaintext game have explicit ordinary weak-source, entropy and masking
  losses; no independently assumed circular/KDM/binary-LWE hop is inserted.
  Negligible source-loss and full reduction/runtime accounting for a concrete
  efficient FHE family are not established merely by that finite bound.
* Successful efficient key generation with usable noise: the missing item
  is an actual dense public quadratic builder, polynomial shortness and
  negligible certificate/failure probability on the secure source family,
  or a different compatible complete-view reduction. Arbitrary-builder Lean
  parameters, small finite certificates, and conditional margin theorems do
  not establish existence. Plain/shared-prefix, target-pair and proportional
  row shortcuts have the recorded public-inversion boundaries.
* A completed stock-GSW alternative: its computational joint self-key hop
  remains unproved; the recursive-feature route retains its cubic-control
  replacement gap and the anchor route lacks unknown-coordinate controls.
  Unit-sum per-row secret shifts do not discharge those complete-view gaps.
* Trusted formal validation: kernel-checked identities and axiom auditing
  can establish their stated conditional results, but cannot certify a
  missing construction or replace a cryptographic reduction theorem.

The same substantive construction/reduction blocker occurs in at least the
native-parameter, rowwise, two-row and present unit-sum continuations. The
previous and present turns supplied verified evidence, rather than merely
restating status. After these construction trials and this additional full
primary-paper audit, no validated next constructive step satisfying the
whole goal is currently available. Further local algebra or toy checks would
not supply the missing efficient secure builder. A new compatible mathematical
construction or complete joint ordinary-LWE proof is required to proceed to
the requested final state. General dense builders and redesigned joint
reductions are not proved impossible; neither is an established solution in
the current workspace/reference evidence.

Both project roots build successfully (9,240 jobs). The standalone

Classical.choice and Quot.sound (or none). All three final logs contain no

All seven pages of the newly added manuscript were parsed. git diff --check
passes. No sub-agents or additional cryptographic hardness assumptions were

The completion audit does not prove the requested final state. The missing
compatible efficient construction/complete joint reduction has recurred for
more than three consecutive goal turns. These turns made verified progress,
but the current methods and reference evidence do not supply a supported next
constructive step for that obligation. The effort is at a mathematical
impasse, rather than waiting on a live process, missing file, permission,

Architecture-level reconsideration, 2026-10-09
---------------------------------------------
The next user request asks for another reconsideration. The previous blocked
assessment applies to the attempted constructions, not to every possible
ordinary-LWE construction. The objective remains repeatable refresh with one
unchanged encryption secret, ordinary LWE only, and polynomial algorithms and
key/ciphertext sizes independent of the evaluated circuit depth.

This review separates limitations of the current proof interfaces from actual
requirements of that objective. The derivations below are mathematical research
notes, NOT new Lean theorems or a completed cryptographic reduction.

Priority 1: a public compiler using the complete retained source block
--------------------------------------------------------------------
The current Builder type takes only A. Its correction has the particular form
linear + B(A)*y, and its body correction is a fixed offset. These restrictions
are not needed for the full-tape uniform-endpoint argument.

Retain x=(A,y), with y=A^T*s+e. Let H_j(x) be ANY efficiently computable public
mask vector and C_j(x) ANY public body correction. For fresh same-secret source
columns (a0_j,y0_j), publish

  a_j = a0_j - H_j(A,y),       b_j = y0_j + C_j(A,y).

For every retained x, the inverse adds H_j(x) and subtracts C_j(x). Thus this
is a public bijection of the ENTIRE source tape. An independent uniform second
block remains independent uniform conditional on x, and a fully uniform source
has an exactly fully uniform transformed endpoint. Public independent builder
coins can also be sampled by the reduction; no secret-dependent coins or
unaccounted private trapdoor are supplied.

The real phase is exactly

  b_j - s^T*a_j = e0_j + C_j(A,y) + s^T*H_j(A,y).

For the desired native GSW phase F_j(s), define

  delta_j = C_j(A,y) + s^T*H_j(A,y) - F_j(s).

The constructive question is to generate H,C in polynomial time, using only
the public source, so that ALL delta_j have usable centered noise bounds with
negligible joint failure under the real source law. Correctness must then reset
the same-key output invariant after each refresh. The security source reduction
still has to include the binary-secret entropy/noise losses and polynomial
runtime/sample accounting; its full-view algebra does not require H to be
linear in y. Body and diagonal columns already have affine implementations;
the unresolved targets are the off-diagonal -w*s_i*s_r columns.

The old builder is the special case H_j=linear_j+B_j(A)*y, C_j=offset_j. A first
strict extension is B_j(A,y), possibly with a body-dependent C_j. More generally
H can be nonlinear in the bodies. This can exploit the sampled LWE relation
without demanding one fixed quadratic coefficient identity for every secret.
No suitable public compiler is constructed in this review. Calling the needed
noise bound an assumption would simply move the original construction gap.

This extension does not erase the sparse-builder attacks. A target-pair-supported
B satisfying the old certificate and usable worst-case projection bounds still
exposes its two linear projections, even if B was selected using y. Those
algebraic attacks are pointwise. Dense or genuinely different corrections need
their own analysis.

Why relaxing only the exact certificate to most binary keys adds little
--------------------------------------------------------------------
Fix A and a builder B(A) independent of y and of the secret. After replacing
s_i^2 by s_i, the phase discrepancy

  D_A(s) = s^T*(B(A)*A^T-Q)*s

is a multilinear polynomial of degree at most two on {0,1}^n, over ZMod q.
If any quadratic coefficient is nonzero, fix all other bits and take the four
choices of its two bits. Their alternating sum is that nonzero coefficient,
so at least one of the four evaluations is nonzero. If all quadratic
coefficients vanish but a linear one does not, at least one of each pair is
nonzero. A nonzero constant is nonzero everywhere. This argument uses addition
and subtraction only and works at even moduli as well.

Consequently, a nonzero degree-two Boolean function fails exact equality with
probability at least 1/4. For an independent uniform binary key,

  Pr_A[D_A is not identically zero] <= 4*Pr_(A,s)[D_A(s) != 0].

Thus an exact, negligible-failure average-case certificate for an A-only builder
already implies a universal Boolean coefficient identity for almost every A.
Binary diagonal/linear cancellations must be combined when defining those
coefficients; identically zero on binary inputs need not mean the unreduced
matrix certificate holds. Approximate centered-error bounds are also a different
question. Crucially, this argument does not apply to B(A,y), because y depends
on the secret and the conditional secret law given y is not independent uniform.
That is why source-body dependence is a substantive extension.

Priority 2: prove the actual plaintext game with the real controls retained
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
For stock independent-control GSW, the desired claim is augmented-view IND-CPA
security given the actual (pk,BK). It need not establish that BK itself looks
like a completely uniform matrix. Existing mixed stored-view and bit-flip
transports identify the complete endpoint but supply no compatible ordinary-LWE
challenge embedding.

The next useful result here would be an explicit PPT map from an ordinary-LWE
challenge to the WHOLE adaptive plaintext experiment, or a new jointly simulated
lossy endpoint preserving the relevant challenge-bit law. A conditional LWE
claim with the real BK supplied as unexplained auxiliary information does not
meet this requirement. Neither does the search-to-decision reduction for
quadratic circular LWE: its search problem already includes the circular view.
This route remains logically open, but there is no new embedding in this review.

Priority 3: prove functional noise cancellation in the refresh computation
-------------------------------------------------------------------------
The present correctness proof bounds each control separately and then takes
triangle bounds. An alternative could construct correlated controls whose
errors cancel after the actual CMux sequence, without requiring every control
to satisfy the old l1 budget. The checked noise_cmux identity gives the exact
increment e_control * digitMatrix(first-second), plus the selected branch error.
The digit matrix depends on previous ciphertexts and hence on source errors.
Any concentration or cancellation proof must account for this dependence and
for all ciphertexts reachable under repeated refresh. A covariance estimate for
independently chosen fixed coefficients is insufficient.

Replacing l1 by l2 alone is a smaller improvement. For a fixed integer matrix
B(A), independent uniform Bernoulli secret bits, and independent centered errors
with variance sigma^2, the integer residual has second moment

  E[(s^T*B*e)^2 | A]
    = (sigma^2/4) * (||B||_F^2 + ||B^T*1||_2^2).

This follows from E[s_i^2]=1/2 and E[s_i*s_r]=1/4 for i!=r. In polynomial
dimensions, ||B||_1 <= sqrt(n*m)*||B||_F, so a polynomial Frobenius bound already
implies a polynomial total l1 bound. This calculation is for lifted integer
errors, not a theorem about modular wraparound or negligible tails. A second
moment alone does not prove a lower tail or a failure probability. It also does
not apply to a body-dependent B correlated with the source errors. A useful
escape must exploit actual correlations, a different parameter budget, or a
different control format, and prove the resulting repeatability invariant.

Priority 4: auxiliary-data conversion or a new joint preimage source law
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
KDMStarConversion excludes a fixed universal ciphertext-only phase converter.
A converter supplied with additional public key-dependent data, or correct only
for actual sampled source ciphertexts, is not excluded. The auxiliary data must
be generated in a complete ordinary-LWE reduction and must provide controls for
every unknown coordinate/digit needed by decryption. A single secure anchor bit
does not close that interface. A conversion cycle between formats must return
to the same underlying key with a refreshed noise bound.

Likewise, the single-row, target-pair, shared-prefix and proportional-row attacks
do not rule out general dense alternating preimages. One can still investigate
a jointly simulated structured matrix/preimage distribution, including shifted
preimage-sampling techniques. The first obligation is a reduction for that
EXACT joint source law, with its published preimages; ordinary-LWE hardness of
the matrix marginal is insufficient. A hidden trapdoor cannot be added to the
challenge for free, and publishing a short linear trapdoor must pass the existing
key-recovery filter. These remain construction ideas rather than proved sources.

Decision and evidence
---------------------
The preferred next attempt is Priority 1, initially a transcript-dependent dense
builder and then, if justified, a general public nonlinear correction. It keeps
the native same-key GSW interface and an explicit complete source simulation,
while relaxing restrictions imposed by the earlier builder definition. Priority
3 can be paired with it if the scalar control-noise bounds remain unsuitable.
Priority 2 remains the main proof route if preserving stock key generation is
essential. Priority 4 requires both a new format/source construction and a joint
security proof and is less concrete at present.

Finite sanity checks performed during this review (not asymptotic experiments):
a nonlinear body-dependent shear at q=4 has 4,096 distinct outputs and passes
16,384 phase identities; all 16,383 nonzero degree-two multilinear polynomials
on three bits over ZMod 4 have support at least 2/8, and all 2,047 such polynomials
on four bits over ZMod 2 have support at least 4/16. An exact rational enumeration
of binary secrets and {-1,0,1} errors matches the second-moment formula (26/3
for the selected 4-by-3 integer matrix). These check the displayed algebra only;
they do not produce a correct secure bootstrapper or strengthen the Lean audit.

Primary literature context was rechecked against MV24, already in refs:
https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-3-031-57728-4_10
Its published abstract explicitly identifies the circular-security problem and
its search-to-decision results; it does not supply the requested ordinary-LWE
reduction. The March 2026 MIT GSW notes also explicitly leave that reduction
open (rough draft, used only as a recent status cross-check):
https://65610.csail.mit.edu/2026/lec/l10-fhe2.pdf
No new paper was adopted as a solution. This review identifies narrower next
research targets, not a proof that those targets can be met or that all remaining
methods have been exhausted. No cryptographic assumption was added.

Trying the reconsidered architectures, 2026-10-09
------------------------------------------------
The user requested trials of the remaining approaches. All four directions
received a concrete construction, source-hybrid, or evaluator test. The full
ordinary-LWE-only same-secret security objective remains unfinished. The new
conditional identities and finite source bounds do not supply the missing
correct efficient construction or the stock GSW joint challenge embedding.

1. Transcript-dependent public compiler
---------------------------------------
GSWTranscriptCompiler.lean now implements arbitrary public corrections
H(A,y),C(A,y), their full-tape inverse, the real phase, and the A-only builder
as a special case. Its binary-source advantage reduces to ordinary LWE with
the existing two narrow row-hybrid sums, one wide ordinary-LWE term, the
noise-absorption gap, and sqrt(q^d/(b+1)^n)/2. Binary-LWE or circular-security
hardness is not separately assumed.

The complete native stored key generator is materialized, with its exact
public marginal and adaptive stored IND-CPA game bound. This retains every
native control, not just one row or one secret-conditioned marginal. The
control_noise identity measures the actual discrepancy

  e0_j + C_j(A,y) + s^T*H_j(A,y) - F_j(s).

noiseBound_bootstrap applies to that actual joint error under one unchanged
binary key. It does not require an A-only certificate. Its error premise is
still a correctness/construction obligation, not a claimed proved guarantee.
The finite source theorem allows even useless compilers; this is why it cannot
be called the completed security proof of a correct bootstrappable scheme.

An executable dense builder was tried in try-gsw-transcript-routes.py. It uses
LLL/Babai on the full quadratic coefficient system and forms a public pool of
the original solution and its additions/subtractions with up to twelve short
kernel vectors. All pool candidates satisfy the same exact public equations.
Two selectors are compared on the same real source and control masks/errors:
minimum total l1, and minimum sum of centered magnitudes of B*y (l1 breaks ties).
The latter genuinely sees the public bodies, not the secret or errors. It is
a public correction-size heuristic, not an asserted residual-noise estimator.

Eight predetermined cases, with no resampling, are recorded in
GSWTranscriptRoutesExploration.json:

  seed   n   m       q    error radius   l1 / body-selected worst l1
   741   4   8     256          1                 51 / 84
   742   4   8   65536          1                334 / 436
   743   4  16   65536          1                130 / 173
   744   4   8   65536         64                303 / 506
   745   8   8   65536          1                unit-pivot elimination failure
   746   8  16   65536          1               2490 / 3376
   747   4  16   65536          1                135 / 189
   748   4  16   65536          1                132 / 177

There are 3,904 checked off-diagonal control targets, including both selectors;
the selected preimages are reused for the symmetric target pair. Full control
phase/error identities are checked on every generated matrix column. The
evaluator uses actual binary gadget decomposition, CMux, reverse control order,
unsigned floor modulus switching (matching GSWModulusSwitch.down), cyclic
indicator accumulation and nearest-codeword lookup. Both branches are tried
through three rounds, with the same controls and secret: 84 refresh calls,
including subsequent NAND followed by refresh.

The l1 selector decodes the selected output bit correctly in 32/42 calls; the
body selector does so in 34/42. The first calls alone give 10/14 and 13/14.
These selected-column results do NOT establish the full output noise invariant.
Some later calls have inputs outside the proved rounding margin; every call's
input decoding, noise and rounding margin are recorded. Among calls whose
inputs satisfy that margin, selected-bit decoding is 21/25 and 21/22.

Only 23/42 and 20/42 outputs, respectively, have every column's noise below
q/4. Only six calls per selector pass the further conservative condition

  2*((growth+1)*actual_output_bound+(n+1)*(factor-1)) < q/2,

needed to guarantee rounding after another NAND with inputs sharing that
output bound. All six belong to seed 742, whose sampled key has Hamming weight
zero. This is a sampled toy accident, not a guarantee over the secret law.
The earlier linear-preimage filter guarantees recovery of every coordinate
in seeds 741,742,743,746,747,748. It guarantees none at radius 64 in seed 744;
that case still has near-half-modulus output noise and no reusable margin.
The elimination failure at seed 745 is not discarded or resampled.

Consequently the transcript-dependent interface survives as a broader research
interface, but the particular public-body objective has not produced a secure
correct family. It can improve a selected-bit sample result while increasing
the norm or losing reusable correctness. No useful asymptotic norm, residual
concentration, negligible joint failure or complete efficient implementation
theorem follows from these tests.

2. Direct plaintext-game source hybrid
--------------------------------------
The attempted lossy embedding first preserves a nonzero rank: in dimension
three, rank-two masks depend on s0,s1 while s2 is invisible to the lossy
projection. A native body-row control has body

  37*s0 + 91*s1 + (q/2)*s2 + e.

Conditioning on the lossy projection (s0,s1) and subtracting its contribution
reveals s2 by nearest-codeword decoding. All 24 binary-key/unit-error cases
at q=256 are checked. Thus this coordinate-aligned extension of the failed
rank-zero entropy endpoint still loses its remaining conditional key entropy.
The test supplies the projection explicitly; it does not claim an attack on
the ordinary real public view or every positive-rank lossy hybrid.

An absorption variant handles the body-row signal by the public mask shift
R*t-w*e_i, whose real body can be u^T*t+e for u=R^T*s. But absorbing a mask-row
signal -w*s_i*s_r by the same mechanism needs the mask shift w*s_i*e_r. That
shift depends on the unknown bit. If it is supplied together with a public
coordinate-aligned lossy factorization, the corresponding mask entry outside
its image also reveals the bit. Hiding the factorization does not supply the
reduction with that missing bit or a simulator for the other correlated rows.

This source-hybrid attempt does not give a public ordinary-LWE embedding of
the complete challenge experiment. The mixed-view transports remain exact,
but do not discharge that computational hop. A different joint lossy sampler
or a direct computational argument remains open; these tests are not a general
direct-game impossibility theorem.

3. Functional correlated-error cancellation
-------------------------------------------
The stronger proposed condition sum_i e_control_i=0 was tested in the actual
native accumulator, rather than only on a fixed linear combination. Take q=256,
two controls, lookup modulus 16, and opposite errors +96,-96 in the top body
gadget column. Their lifted centered error vectors sum to exactly zero for
every one of the four binary keys. Set rotation weights (8,0) and initial body
8*s0, so the ideal decrypted lookup value is zero.

The second control multiplies the zero branch difference, so its cancelling
error never contributes. The first control does contribute. The actual final
phase is 96 rather than the zero-codeword and decodes to one. All four keys
produce the wrong bit. This tests a correctness condition; the diagnostic
control law is not claimed to have an ordinary-LWE security reduction.

Thus global zero-sum errors alone are insufficient. A successful design needs
cancellation against the actual ciphertext-dependent digit matrices and a
bound for every reachable next refresh. The failed trial is consistent with
the checked noise_cmux identity; it does not exclude such a stronger design.

4. Auxiliary conversion and a structured preimage source
-------------------------------------------------------
GSWQuadraticAuxConversion.lean implements a public converter with an explicit
auxiliary native LWE table. For a recursive ciphertext (T,c,b), decompose T
into public weighted coordinate matrices. Auxiliary column a_i has phase
v_i(s)+epsilon_i; public coefficients d_i reconstruct s^T*T*s=sum_i d_i*v_i(s).
The output body subtracts sum_i d_i*body(a_i), and its mask is
-c-sum_i d_i*mask(a_i). Its native phase is exactly

  RecursiveQuadratic.decrypt(s,(T,c,b)) - sum_i d_i*epsilon_i.

Public MATRIX reconstruction implies the phase identity for every key; the
converter does not select coefficients using the secret. Binary power-of-two
digits keep the added error a sum of polynomially many small errors. The
original decryption secret is retained, and there is no ciphertext-only
universal-converter contradiction because the auxiliary table varies with it.

The executable trial converts 800 honest recursive ciphertexts across all
eight three-bit keys at q=4096. The table has 108 weighted-product columns.
Every exact phase/error identity passes, with observed error at most 14 and
the conservative bound 112. This is a successful conditional conversion test.
The honest table generator uses the private key, and its native quadratic
hint law has NO established joint ordinary-LWE reduction here. Supplying that
table as a hardness premise would reintroduce the disallowed circular/KDM
gap. Conversion also carries input noise; it is not by itself a refresh.

The separate structured-source trial specializes the matrix law A_i=B-u_i*G
of WWW25 (already in refs) to two labels differing in one block, while trying
to give BOTH matrices honest zero-encryption batches under the SAME secret.
Then

  y_first-y_second = s^T*G + e_first-e_second.

At the selected top gadget columns this equals (q/2)*s_i plus an error of
magnitude at most 2E. Every bit is publicly recoverable if 4E<q/2.
GSWShiftedSourceDisclosure.lean proves the matrix/body identity and complete
binary recovery at arbitrary selected gadget columns, with no independence
premise on those errors. The trial checks 5,832 key/error cases (17,496 bit
reads) at q=256,E=1, recovering every bit in every case.

This is an attack on the ATTEMPTED same-secret honest-zero specialization,
not WWW25 or its NIZK/vector-commitment applications. Its somewhere-hardness
statement for an individual shifted matrix does not assert hardness of this
different joint source. A law with different correlated phases, or a general
dense preimage builder, is not excluded by this test. The required complete
joint source simulation still cannot be borrowed from matrix marginals.
Primary source: https://eprint.iacr.org/2024/1401

Verification and remaining obligation
------------------------------------
All three new modules, both project roots, and the expanded GSWSelfKeyAudit
pass in the validated container mirror: 9,243 build jobs, 875 axiom-audit
declarations. The only reported axioms are propext, Classical.choice and
Quot.sound. The module/build/audit logs contain no Lean errors, warnings or
sorryAx. All 467 selected authoritative project Lean/configuration files
byte-match the validated mirror; git diff --check passes. Existing work and
all reference files were preserved. No sub-agents or new hardness assumptions
were used.

The final script's coefficient switching is unsigned floor division, matching
Lean. During development, kernel checking caught and corrected the auxiliary
converter's mask sign; both the final checked formula and the executable
converter subtract the auxiliary masks. Final trial records use those corrected
operations. The exploratory output's derived output/NAND margin flags are
computed directly from the recorded full-column error bound and dimensions.

The concrete surviving construction target is an efficient public compiler
with small ACTUAL joint discrepancy and negligible failure under an
ordinary-LWE-derived source family, or an independent complete joint reduction
for stock GSW. An auxiliary converter can consume such a secure native table,
but does not establish its security. No complete solution was obtained, and
the tests do not prove that all possible remaining approaches are impossible.

Private key/source co-design trial, 2026-10-09
===========================================

This follow-up actually implements the private gadget-trapdoor route. It
changes the information available to the builder: the honest key generator
knows a trapdoor, but the public view retains only A,y and the masked native
controls. It does NOT disclose the trapdoor, preimages or decompositions.
Therefore the earlier public-builder source reduction cannot simply be
reused, and public-witness recovery tests do not by themselves attack this
different view.

Construction and exact identities
---------------------------------
Use MP12 Algorithm 1, with identity tag:

  A = [ A0 | G-A0*R ], T = [ R ; I ], A*T = G.

Every entry of R is zero with probability 1/2 and +/-1 with probability 1/4,
the statistical instantiation explicitly given in MP12. The toy runs use
m0=n*levels+80, so the paper's MATRIX-MARGINAL statistical-distance estimate
is (n*levels/2)*2^(-40). This finite estimate concerns A alone; it is not a
security estimate for the full public view. No new reference download was
needed: refs/lwe-foundations/MP12.pdf was re-read.
Primary source: https://eprint.iacr.org/2011/501

For each off-diagonal native target Q=-2^level*E_ij, choose dense alternating
freedom U with entries in {-2^level,0,2^level}. A signed gadget decomposition
D satisfies D*G^T=Q+U-U^T. Set B=D*T^T. Alternatively use

  B=(D+Z*K^T)*T^T, G*K=0,

where each gadget-kernel column is 2*e_level-e_(level+1) and Z has bounded
ternary entries. Both versions satisfy B*A^T=Q+U-U^T exactly. This bounded
kernel randomization is not the basis-independent Gaussian preimage sampler
of MP12; no claim about that sampler's security is borrowed.

The native masked control uses a=a_fresh-linear-B*y and
b=s^T*a_fresh+e_fresh. Binary diagonal products are handled by affine shifts
as before. The complete control error is exactly

  e_fresh + s^T*B*e_base.

GSWPrivateTrapdoor.lean checks the gadget/kernel certificate and projected
body identity over any commutative ring, including even moduli. The shared
residual is retained explicitly; randomization does not magically remove it.
The signed coefficient construction gives a direct triangle bound on total
l1: at most 3*n^2*(m0+1) in the deterministic mode, and at most
3*n^2*levels*(m0+1) in the randomized mode. The script checks these structural
bounds in every case. These particular inequalities are not new Lean norm
theorems. The builder runs in polynomial time and needs no public SIS solver
or matrix resampling. A polynomial norm of this PRIVATE builder does not
discharge the security obligation for a PUBLIC builder in the earlier theorem.

Executable results
------------------
Reproduce with PYTHONPATH=/tmp/fhe-preimage-packages python3
scripts/try-gsw-private-trapdoor.py --output
docs/GSWPrivateTrapdoorExploration.json. The four fixed seeds 811..814 give:

  n, levels, E, key weight | control noise deterministic / randomized
  2, 20, 1, 2             | 30 / 278
  3, 24, 1, 3             | 71 / 571
  3, 24, 8, 1             | 260 / 1809
  4, 24, 8, 3             | 489 / 4909

All 1,232 off-diagonal certificates and 2,352 full control-column phase checks
pass. Each mode refreshes both initial message branches and then repeats NAND
with an initial zero encryption and refresh twice, using the SAME secret and
controls throughout. All 48 calls decode the expected bit, meet the sufficient
input rounding margin and have full-column output noise below q/4. The
conservative margin for a further NAND and rounding passes 24/24 deterministic
calls and 14/24 randomized calls. Randomization therefore worsens the reusable
noise margin in these samples; bit success alone does not prove unlimited
correctness. The keys all have nonzero Hamming weight, no failed source is
resampled, and no asymptotic or cryptographic parameter claim is made for
these dimensions.

As an explicitly DIFFERENT disclosure diagnostic, giving T to an observer
allows top gadget-column projections of y to recover every tested key, with
the worst-case margin passing in every case. The real candidate view does
not contain T. The checked recover_disclosed_factor theorem reconstructs T^T
from a published factor* T^T only when the factor is also published with a
left inverse. It does not assert that arbitrary hidden or randomized factors
can be recovered from masked controls.

Where the ordinary-LWE proof still stops
---------------------------------------
The candidate resolves short-preimage generation for an honest privately
generated matrix. It does not resolve joint security. A reduction receiving
an ordinary unknown-secret challenge (A0,y0=s^T*A0+e0) can sample R and program
the extra matrix block. But its honest zero-source body there would be

  y1 = G^T*s - R^T*y0 + R^T*e0 + e1.

programmed_source_body checks this identity. The reduction does not receive
G^T*s or e0. Replacing y1 by the computable -R^T*y0 changes its phase by the
secret gadget term, so it does not produce the honest zero source used by
the correctness trial. Conversely, receiving the complete random source
matrix does not supply its private trapdoor. Statistical near-uniformity of
A is insufficient to provide either missing joint sampler. These are gaps
in the attempted simulators, not a theorem that every simulator is impossible.

An independent-kernel sampler, a compatible joint source reduction or a direct
plaintext-game proof could still succeed. Assuming the private auxiliary law
secure would reintroduce an unproved KDM/circular premise. This trial produces
a useful private correctness candidate and narrows the required simulation;
it does not complete the requested ordinary-LWE-only security proof.

Verification for this follow-up: both Lean roots build successfully (9,244
jobs); the expanded GSWSelfKeyAudit exits successfully with 880 declarations,
using only propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound. There is no sorryAx or
Lean error/warning in the final build/audit logs. All 468 selected project
Lean/configuration files byte-match the validated container mirror. The final
Python trial is rerun with the structural bounds included, its syntax check
passes, and git diff --check passes. No new hardness assumption was introduced.

Joint-security and completion audit, 2026-10-09
=============================================

The previous goal turn made progress: it implemented the private gadget
candidate, checked its identities and recorded concrete reusable-margin
evidence. Its unproved complete-view security remains the same mathematical
blocker as in the public-compiler/auxiliary attempts, rather than a pending
build process or a Lean elaboration issue.

Rechecking the proposed private-entropy argument
----------------------------------------------
MP12 Algorithm 1 and its statistical instantiation bound the distribution of
A and the trapdoor quality. Its CCA encryption proof in Section 6.3 has a
reduction that chooses the trapdoor itself and constructs an auxiliary body
from the challenge and that trapdoor, followed by an appropriate leftover-hash
argument. It does not provide the missing honest same-secret zero body for
the programmed G block in our private candidate. The checked
programmed_source_body identity still contains G^T*s and the challenge error.
Knowing R does not give either value. Giving a trapdoor for a fully random
challenge matrix instead also requires a new sampler; statistical matrix
near-uniformity alone is not such an algorithm.

BJTW26 was rechecked at its introduction, general-distribution formulation
and explicit limitations. The general result proves search hardness subject
to distribution, entropy, support and sample-count conditions. No verified
specialization here gives the needed decision statement for our entire
correlated private view. In particular, the control masks and shared residual
depend on the matrix-conditioned private trapdoor, and the native gadget
message is secret-dependent. Treating this view as an ordinary independent
matrix/general-error instance without proving those distribution conditions
would skip the required computational hop. This is not a claim that a new
specialization or a separate search-to-decision argument cannot exist.

New candidate checked: LH23
--------------------------
Downloaded and validated the publisher PDF of Lian--Huang, "KDM Security IBE
Based on LWE beyond Affine Functions", Applied Sciences 13(14), 8259 (2023):
refs/kdm/LH23.pdf, 13 pages. Catalog, bibliography, index and SHA256SUMS now
include 39 papers. Primary source: https://doi.org/10.3390/app13148259

Section 4 supplies the affine KDM-IBE base; Section 5 and Theorem 2 propose
randomized-encoding amplification. The full encryption algorithm first
simulates an encoding of g(sk), encrypts its encoded components under the
base scheme, and the full decryption algorithm applies an additional decoder
D to the decrypted encoding. Thus its richer KDM plaintext is not directly
the native linear phase of a GSW control column. The theorem supplies no
same-secret GSW conversion, CMux multiplication identity, reusable refresh
bound or full native control-view ordinary-LWE reduction.

Even granting the paper's KDM claim does not fill our missing theorem. A
compiler would need to turn the encrypted encoding into a native control
for every decryption coordinate, preserve joint security, and retain reusable
noise margins. Computing D on decrypted data is a secret-key operation; it
is not a public homomorphic conversion. Invoking homomorphic decryption to
perform that step would itself require the absent secure control material.
The already recorded Applebaum11 amplification route has the same compatibility
obligation. This audit does not claim an attack on LH23 or prove that all
possible randomized-encoding compilers are impossible.

Requirements checked against authoritative sources
--------------------------------------------------
* Ordinary LWE as the only computational premise: NOT COMPLETE. The recursive
  family security theorem still contains familyControlReplacementAdvantage
  with no negligible bound; the private candidate has no full-view reduction.
* Security of the complete stored bootstrapping view, including all actual
  self-key controls and adaptive public-encryption challenges: NOT COMPLETE.
  The public compiler theorem has the right stored game but no useful public
  compiler with the required joint correctness guarantee. The private builder
  cannot be substituted into that theorem without a new source reduction.
* Repeated native GSW refresh under one unchanged underlying secret, without
  acyclic key scheduling: conditional correctness is checked, and private
  finite trials retain the key, but a complete efficient secure family with
  negligible joint failure is NOT COMPLETE. Forty-eight successful finite
  calls do not establish the asymptotic requirement.
* No independent circular/KDM/hinted premise: no such premise was introduced
  to close the gap. Leaving an unbounded replacement advantage or assuming
  the private auxiliary law secure would fail this requirement.
* Lean proof and required verification: existing conditional identities and
  bounds pass the 9,244-job build and 880-declaration audit. These checks cover
  their stated hypotheses, not the missing construction/reduction theorem.

The same complete-view computational blocker persists across the consecutive
public/auxiliary, private-trapdoor and present audit turns. No live build,
download or verification process is being awaited. The available verified
approaches are exhausted at this point; further aligned progress requires a
new compatible construction or a new joint ordinary-LWE argument, not another
conditional lemma or a broader security assumption. The goal is unfinished
and at an impasse. This is not a general impossibility result for the objective.

Rethinking after the completion audit, 2026-10-10
==============================================

The user explicitly asks for another reconsideration. The previous impasse is
real for the attempted samplers and reductions; it does not establish that all
remaining designs are impossible. This review identifies a concrete extension
that the previous trials did not implement and narrows two other interfaces.
The following derivations are research notes, not new Lean theorems or a
completed cryptographic reduction. The original one-secret, ordinary-LWE-only,
repeatable-refresh and polynomial-complexity requirements remain intact.

First priority: two-sided public correction with exact linear-error cancellation
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Retain TWO same-secret LWE blocks, independently sampled given s:

  y1=A1^T*s+e1,    y2=A2^T*s+e2.

For an efficiently public-computable matrix D of size p1 by p2, define

  C = y1^T*D*y2,
  H = -A1*D*y2 - A2*D^T*y1.

Direct expansion gives the commutative-ring identity

  C+s^T*H = -s^T*A1*D*A2^T*s + e1^T*D*e2.

Both first-order error terms cancel. No division by two is used, so this
identity works at the power-of-two moduli used by native GSW. This is different
from merely minimizing ||B*y|| or randomizing a one-sided B: H may have large
centered coefficients, while its ACTUAL phase discrepancy is controlled by D.

For a native target F_j(s)=s^T*Q_j*s+ell_j^T*s+kappa_j, seek D_j with

  A1*D_j*A2^T = -Q_j+U_j-U_j^T.

Add ell_j to H and kappa_j to C. The alternating part vanishes in a scalar
quadratic form. For a fresh same-secret source column (a0,y0), publish

  a=a0-H_j,   b=y0+C_j.

Its native phase is EXACTLY

  F_j(s) + e0 + e1^T*D_j*e2.

The binary diagonal/body targets can still use the existing affine handling;
the new certificates are needed for the off-diagonal mask targets.

Security interface: concatenate the two retained blocks into the base input
of GSWTranscriptCompiler. Public H,C define the same full-tape triangular
bijection already checked there. All retained bodies, masks and native controls
stay in the observer's view. The source is one ordinary-LWE-derived binary
batch with p1+p2+controlColumns samples, with its existing entropy and
noise-absorption losses retained. No extra KDM or hinted-source assumption is
needed IF a suitable public D builder is actually supplied. A private D would
not have this automatic source-reduction property.

Correctness interface: if |e1|<=E1 and |e2|<=E2 entrywise, then

  |e1^T*D_j*e2| <= E1*E2*||D_j||_1.

Thus the needed norm is that of the TWO-SIDED preimage, not of A1*D_j or
A2*D_j^T. A polynomial norm would give a polynomial residual amplification;
the parameter/noise proof must account for E1*E2 and the enlarged source
sample count. Reusing the old parameter theorem verbatim would skip this
changed error budget. A fixed secret-independent D built from A1,A2 also has
zero mean residual and second moment sigma1^2*sigma2^2*||D||_F^2 for independent
centered errors. This identity is not a negligible-tail theorem, and it does
not apply to a D selected using the bodies without further dependence analysis.

The remaining builder problem is a linear modular system in p1*p2 unknown
entries, with bilinear source coefficients. Its full matrix entries are
structured products of A1,A2; hardness or good short-vector bounds for a
generic uniformly random SIS matrix cannot be transferred to it for free.
One can solve the symmetric Boolean coefficient constraints and retain an
alternating witness rather than unnecessarily imposing every matrix entry.
The next concrete trial is a dense two-sided lattice builder, with exact
certificate, norm, failure, key-recovery and repeated-refresh checks. A
factorization from two already short LINEAR coordinate preimages is only a
baseline: those same public preimages may permit key recovery and must be
tested. Prior one-row and target-pair attacks do not automatically apply to
a dense D whose contracted one-sided rows are large.

Evidence so far is algebraic only: 1,000 exact phase/triangle-bound checks with
random dense A1,A2,D at moduli 2,4,8,16,97; and all 81 pairs of ternary error
vectors for D=[[1,2],[-1,0]], whose exact residual mean is zero and second moment
is 8/3=(2/3)^2*||D||_F^2. These checks do not construct short certified D_j,
validate an asymptotic source family, or constitute a Lean proof. No candidate
failure is being hidden by calling these identity checks bootstrap trials.

Second priority: prove the actual plaintext game with retained real controls
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
The direct proof target remains the stored IND-CPA experiment, rather than
indistinguishability of every control from a uniform matrix. In particular,
GSWMixedStoredView already describes replacement of the PUBLIC-KEY block
while retaining the real controls. A successful reduction could target that
specific computational hop, or embed the challenge directly into public
encryption. It must generate the entire correlated view at the unknown
challenge secret; supplying a real BK as an unexplained oracle is insufficient.

Public evaluation and repeated refresh add no secret oracle to the security
experiment: given the stored key and challenge ciphertext, they are public
postprocessing. This observation reduces the security work to the augmented
encryption game, but does not prove it. Correctness and reusable noise margins
remain independent construction obligations.

Refinement of the private route: simulate the published marginal, not the trapdoor
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Failure to obtain a private trapdoor for a challenge matrix is a failure of
that attempted simulator, not a necessary condition for every reduction.
Only the public masked controls need to be simulated. A distributional proof
could integrate out B and R instead of reconstructing them. The exact marginal
shows where the hidden trapdoor still matters.

Condition on the key, base transcript, private preimage B_j and source errors.
The fresh control mask a0 is independent uniform; translating it by ell_j+B_j*y
makes the published mask a uniform, independent of that conditioned data.
The published body then has the conventional native law

  b = s^T*a + F_j(s) + delta_j + e0_j,
  delta_j = s^T*B_j*e_base.

Therefore, for this sampler, the remaining influence of the private trapdoor
in the control marginal is the JOINT residual law. This normalization uses
the latent secret in the argument; it is not itself a public challenge map.

If independent interval noise of radius F washes out every residual, the
existing UniformInterval.shift_intCast_le bound gives, after conditioning and
mixing, a complete-view TV bound of at most

  epsilon_matrix + epsilon_bad + sum_j Dbound_j/(2F+1)

to conventional GSW with the same narrow public-key noise and independent
wide control noise, provided |delta_j|<=Dbound_j except with probability
epsilon_bad and the matrix marginal is epsilon_matrix-close to uniform.
The product bound does not require independent residuals; it conditions on
their entire vector and then uses independent fresh flooding noise. Matrix
near-uniformity can be used AFTER the controls have lost their R dependence.
The newly stated full sampler comparison is a mathematical derivation, not
an already checked Lean theorem. Forty-nine two-coordinate interval shift
cases at radius 5 verify the sum bound exactly by rational enumeration.

Consequently, flooding all correlations does not establish ordinary-LWE
security of native self-key controls: the comparison still contains F_j(s).
It returns the conventional full native-control security obligation, with
the stated wider noise law. A potentially distinct private approach needs a
joint marginal simulator that uses the helpful correlations while preserving
correctness, or a direct plaintext-game argument. Mere additional trapdoor
randomization followed by complete flooding is not an independent resolution.

Lower priority: a cycle of distribution-restricted ciphertext formats
-------------------------------------------------------------------
The existing conversion obstructions concern universal ciphertext-only phase
conversion. A converter that is correct only on reachable sampled ciphertexts,
or uses jointly secure auxiliary data, remains logically possible. The useful
target is a COMPLETE cycle, under the same original secret:

  native GSW -> alternate format -> refreshed native GSW.

Each leg must have a public executable algorithm, security for all published
auxiliary material, and a closed reusable noise/reachability invariant.
Randomized-encoding decryption alone does not supply these legs. Likewise,
supporting one bit-dependent operation at degree two does not support the
next operation if the output requires degree-three control phases. Varying
ciphertext representation is distinct from changing the underlying key, but
it does not remove those algebraic and simulation obligations.

The primary composability paper already in refs was rechecked:
https://cic.iacr.org/p/2/1/1/pdf (Micciancio25). Its bootstrapping transformation
uses circular security, and its contributions here clarify correctness and
composition rather than providing the missing ordinary-LWE construction.
Relabeling the target as composability is not an independent security proof.

Decision
--------
The preferred next construction attempt is the two-source, two-sided public
compiler, starting with its phase/certificate formalization and an honest
dense-preimage trial. It changes the required short object and cancels the
previous linear residual exactly, while retaining an available public source
simulation. The direct plaintext-game proof remains the best route for stock
key generation. The private route now has the narrower task of simulating
the published correlated marginal, and a format cycle is exploratory until
both conversions are supplied. None of these possibilities is currently a
proved complete solution or a theorem that one must exist.

Trying the two-sided public compiler, 2026-10-10
==============================================

This continuation makes the proposed route concrete, rather than renaming
the missing full-view security term. The previous turn made progress by
deriving the new public two-source correction and identifying its distinct
short-preimage obligation. The full objective is still unfinished.

Checked Lean implementation
---------------------------
GSWTwoSidedCompiler.lean implements an actual public compiler on the retained
concatenation of two source blocks. Its Builder receives only A1,A2 and the
column index. The module checks:

* split/join identities and bilinear cancellation over any commutative ring;
* the complete actual correction phase, including both sampled error vectors;
* the negative-target alternating certificate, without division by two;
* the native self-key phase/discrepancy identity for every original coordinate;
* full native control noise, exactly e0+e1^T*D*e2;
* the integer bound E1*E2*||D||_1;
* complete stored adaptive plaintext-game bounds for both compiler variants,
  with the ordinary-LWE terms, binary-source entropy, noise absorption and
  public-encryption masking loss still explicit.

The second variant removes an unnecessary restriction for binary keys. Let
P_j=A1*D_j*A2^T. Its builder solves only the off-diagonal coefficient equations,
with certificate

  P_j = -Q_j + diag(P_j) + U_j-U_j^T.

The compiler adds diag(P_j) to its affine mask correction. The checked
binary_diagonal identity s_i^2=s_i cancels the otherwise unwanted diagonal
phase. Both full and diagonal-relaxed variants thus have the SAME actual
bilinear residual. All added correction coefficients are computed publicly
from A1,A2,D_j; no unknown secret coordinate is supplied to the reduction.

The existing GSWTranscriptCompiler.noiseBound_bootstrap applies to these
concrete compilers once the stated actual error vector is bounded. This is
same-secret conditional native correctness, not a claim that the executable
LLL builder below satisfies a useful asymptotic bound. The new stored-game
theorems do not assume a circular/KDM replacement advantage.

Public builder and exact trials
--------------------------------
scripts/try-gsw-two-sided-compiler.py constructs the full modular coefficient
system in the p1*p2 entries of D. Diagonal rows have coefficients
A1[i,c]*A2[i,d]; an off-diagonal row (i,j) has coefficients
A1[i,c]*A2[j,d]+A1[j,c]*A2[i,d]. The relaxed mode omits the diagonal rows.
An LLL-reduced full kernel lattice and Babai nearest plane give candidates.
Every returned matrix is checked against an explicit alternating witness.
All matrices are independently sampled, no failures are resampled, and the
builder never receives the key or source errors.

Reproduce with PYTHONPATH=/tmp/fhe-preimage-packages python3
scripts/try-gsw-two-sided-compiler.py --output
docs/GSWTwoSidedCompilerExploration.json. The eight fixed seeds are compared
in both modes on the same source draws:

  seed  n,p1,p2,levels,E | full / free-diagonal: max ||D||_1, control noise
  10011 2,2,2,8,1       | failure / failure
  10012 3,3,3,12,1      | failure / 43, 1
  10013 4,4,4,16,1      | failure / 312, 144
  10014 4,8,8,16,1      | 162, 42 / 67, 18
  10015 4,4,4,24,1      | failure / 2567, 819
  10016 4,8,8,24,1      | 375, 80 / 134, 29
  10017 4,8,8,24,64     | failure / 132, 53740
  10018 8,8,8,24,1      | failure / failure

Failure means the chosen full-row-rank unit-pivot solver cannot invert that
coefficient system. It is recorded, not a proof that the particular target
has no preimage under any solver. The relaxed variant reduces failed cases
from six of eight to two of eight. The completed full variant has 480 control
target checks and 800 full-column phase checks; the completed relaxed variant
has 1,320 target checks and 2,224 full-column checks.

The cancellation is visible beyond small coefficient norms. For example,
seed 10016 in the relaxed mode has ||D||_1<=134, while the largest centered
total norm of A1*D is 169,999,416. Its maximum bilinear residual is 28,
whereas the first-order term removed by the correction can reach 8,335,681.
Thus the new residual accounting is a substantive change from bounding the
one-sided contracted matrix. No independence between the residual and the
secret-dependent public bodies is silently assumed in the phase identities.

Every completed case uses actual native digit matrices, unsigned floor modulus
switching, CMux accumulation and three refresh cycles on two branches, including
two NAND-and-refresh continuations with unchanged controls and secret. Among
the baseline trials, the full variant decodes 12/12 selected outputs, and the
relaxed variant 33/36. Sufficient input rounding margins pass 12/12 and 30/36;
full output decoding margins pass 12/12 and 33/36. The conservative sufficient
margin for another NAND and rounding passes 6/12 and 9/36, respectively.
All eight source keys have nonzero Hamming weight, including failed cases.

Security diagnostics and noise stress
-------------------------------------
Every COMPLETED baseline source admits public linear coordinate preimages
whose worst-case margins recover the entire binary key from A1,A2,y1,y2.
That includes the E=64 case. These are attacks on the recorded toy sources,
not claims about all large-dimensional tensor builders. Passing the finite
refresh tests does not make those instances secure.

Two additional diagnostics keep seed 10017's public matrix geometry and key
and raise the source/fresh noise radius to 32,768 and 262,144. The relaxed
builder still has ||D||_1<=132 and all exact certificates and phase checks
pass. The previously sufficient public linear recovery margins now all fail.
The observed public filter still gets 4/4 bits at the first radius, but only
2/4 at the larger radius. Failed margins or incorrect guesses by this one
filter do not establish security against other algorithms.

Both stress cases have control noise almost q/2: 8,387,603 and 8,386,573 at
q=16,777,216. Each initially has two valid input rounding margins but NO full
output decoding margin and NO sufficient next-NAND margin. Selected-bit
outcomes are 5/6 and 2/6, respectively; these successes do not restore the
lost full-column invariant. The complete experiment records 18 attempts,
eight solver failures, 2,376 target certificates, 3,984 full-column phase
checks and 60 refresh calls. Of the selected outputs, 52/60 happen to decode
the expected bit, but the full invariant and source security do not follow.

Remaining obligation
--------------------
The new compiler supplies a public reduction interface and checked conditional
native correctness. The construction still lacks an efficient public tensor
preimage algorithm with polynomial useful norm, negligible joint failure,
and a proved compatible asymptotic parameter/implementation family. The
