Rethinking after the blocked audit: control/digit co-design, 2026-10-10
====================================================================

The user requested a fresh rethink. The goal tool still reports BLOCKED;
this review does not claim completion or an automatic change to ACTIVE.
The required ordinary-LWE-only security, unchanged secret and repeatable
public refresh remain the objective. No additional assumption is adopted.

The direct plaintext-game route and integration of private trapdoors out of
the published marginal were already explicit at lines 4569 and 4585 before
this review. Repeating them is not a new construction. The fresh question
here is whether a restricted digit/evaluation space could absorb large
structured control errors. This exposes two precise filters and suggests
co-designing the public decomposition with the sampler rather than merely
changing the errors while fixing every evaluation operation.

Filter 1: strict annihilation fails at the current initial gadget
----------------------------------------------------------------
For q=2^L with exactly L unsigned binary levels, D(G)=I. This follows
coordinatewise from the binary digits of 2^ell. GSWAccumulator.initial has
G at the known body and zero elsewhere. For a nonzero rotation weight,
one first-step slot has shifted branch G and unshifted branch zero.
That slot computes C_control*D(G)=C_control. Its error is exactly the
control error. A nonzero error vector cannot be annihilated on every digit
factor used by this initial evaluator, even before considering later steps.
This is specific to these parameters/decomposition and this initial format.
It does not prove that correlated nonzero errors cannot be tolerated.

Filter 2: an unrestricted linear error quotient exposes a public key list
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every binary N-by-N matrix B is a native digit factor: columnwise binary
recomposition gives D(G*B)=B, since the reconstructed entries lie between
0 and q-1. In particular, every matrix unit occurs. Suppose an additive
error subgroup E of (Z/qZ)^N is stable under ALL native digit factors.
Projection by matrix units shows that E=I^N for a single ideal I of Z/qZ:
project each coordinate of an element of E, then move that coordinate to
any other position. Additive closure supplies multiplication by ring
scalars and sums of these projected coordinates. For q=2^L the ideals are
2^k*(Z/qZ), including the whole ring and zero.

An exact public quotient by a proper such ideal makes every control error
zero modulo two. This is enough to recover a polynomial-size binary-key
list from the CONTROLS THEMSELVES; no parity condition on public-key errors
is needed. Take the level-zero column in every gadget block of control C_i
and reduce modulo two, giving a public (n+1)-square matrix M_i. For the
unchanged binary secret, v=(1,s_1,...,s_n) then satisfies

  v*M_i = s_i*v  over F_2.

The following public algorithm receives only the M_i. Start with V=F_2^(n+1)
and an empty bit label. At stage i split each current V into

  V_0={x in V: x*M_i=0},  V_1={x in V: x*M_i=x},

append the corresponding bit to each label, and discard zero spaces.
Both spaces and their bases are computed by Gaussian elimination.
V_0 intersects V_1 only at zero, so their dimensions sum to at most dim V.
Inductively the sum of live dimensions is at most n+1, and there are at
most n+1 live spaces at every stage. Commutativity of the M_i is unnecessary.
There are n stages and polynomially many polynomial-size linear systems.
After the last stage, retain a label b only if (1,b) satisfies all its
labeled equations. This gives exactly the compatible binary keys and
includes the real one. Every final label has at most one body-first vector
(1,b). Thus there are at most n+1 compatible keys, efficiently enumerable.

For a correctly encrypting public scheme this list breaks augmented
plaintext security: encrypt fresh random known bits publicly and compare
the candidate decoders. The true decoder passes with the correctness
probability. Polynomially many examples distinguish candidates with a
constant prediction error; a surviving high-accuracy decoder predicts a
fresh random-bit challenge with a constant advantage. This is a mathematical
attack argument, not a completed Lean probability/PPT reduction. As a
separate simpler diagnostic, even PUBLIC-KEY source errors give ordinary
noiseless equations modulo two and recover a binary key from full-rank masks.

Neither argument applies to usual native controls with unrestricted noisy
parities, to a small residual added outside the proposed ideal, to nonlinear
error states, or to stability required only on a smaller reachable family.
The whole-ring ideal does not provide a useful exact decryption quotient.
These filters therefore do not assert general impossibility of the objective.

Executable evidence
-------------------
scripts/check-gsw-digit-invariants.py is independent of numerical lattice
packages and uses exact Python integer arithmetic. It verifies:
* D(G)=I at 12 choices of q=4,8,16,32 and rows=1,2,3;
* 756 individual matrix-unit reconstruction identities;
* 476 actual initial-CMux phase/error identities, with every binary key of
  the configured dimensions and each unit error coordinate;
* all 15 additive subgroups of (Z/4Z)^2 against all 16 binary digit factors;
  the invariant subgroup sizes are precisely 1,4,16, the three ideal powers;
* 1,008 independently sampled native control families, at n=1,...,6 and
  errors in {-2,0,2}. The public eigenspace algorithm's entire key list equals
  exhaustive compatible-key enumeration in every case and always contains
  the real secret. The largest observed list has two keys, and the largest
  observed live-space count is three;
* 96 even-public-source-error, full-rank-mask key-recovery cases.
docs/GSWDigitInvariantExploration.json records the totals and limitations.
These are finite correctness checks of the filters/attack, not secure
parameters, negligible-failure claims, or Lean theorems.

Revised research directions and first decisive gates
---------------------------------------------------
1. Joint control AND short-digit design on restricted reachable states.
   Permit a public randomized/redundant decomposition D'(X) with G*D'(X)=X.
   Its exact control-error contribution remains e_i*D'(X), but D'(G) need
   not be I. Short kernel corrections D'(X)=D(X)+K(X), G*K(X)=0, or an encoded
   accumulator could change the noise evolution. Ordinary randomized digit
   bounds alone are not such a construction. The first object to supply is
   an actual public rule for K/encoding and an actual correlated control law
   which together preserve a fixed-size invariant through a complete NAND,
   refresh and next refresh. It must retain useful message separation and
   avoid the modulo-two key-list attack above. Public transcript compilation
   is attractive here because its source simulation already exists, but no
   compatible residual/decomposition pair has been constructed in this turn.
   This is a new concrete search interface, not a ready proof or sampler.

2. A private joint sampler with a useful correlated published residual law.
   Retain private witnesses and simulate only the public view, as previously
   identified. The first gate is an explicit map from an ordinary challenge
   to that complete public law, including bodies, before a new trapdoor
   correctness implementation. The programmed MP12 source and its rotated
   variant do not supply this map. Flooding back to conventional self-key
   controls restores the original gap. No new simulator is available.

3. A genuinely body-dependent dense public compiler.
   Work beyond the attacked short-certificate and pair-supported families.
   Supply a polynomial public H(A,y),C(A,y) whose actual discrepancy is useful
   at all necessary gadget weights with negligible joint failure. The first
   gate is its real discrepancy law and public key-recovery diagnostics;
   a target-dependent coefficient choice or another successful low-noise toy
   solve is insufficient. The existing complete source reduction can then
   be reused with the actual sampler's runtime and parameters. No such
   sampler has been constructed in this review.

For stock key generation, a direct ordinary-LWE proof of the retained-control
plaintext game remains the exact missing theorem, but there is no new
challenge embedding to try. Feature/KDM format cycles likewise lack their
executable return conversion and complete joint security. They are not
promoted to additional ready approaches.

Fresh searches again returned the already reviewed WHT26 and layered QFHE
claims, without a verified theorem meeting the same-key requirements.
The author repository https://github.com/vbuterin/crazy_gsw illustrates
matrix-message invariant subspaces and points back to Hiromasa--Abe--Okamoto
matrix GSW (already represented by HAO15 in refs). Its public-key matrix-LWE
discussion does not give the missing same-key bootstrapping joint reduction;
no security premise is imported from that prototype. No additional paper
was needed or downloaded.

Decision
--------
The most distinct next experiment would co-design a short decomposition and
the control error law, with the full reachable refresh cycle as its first
test. The canonical annihilator and universal ideal proposals fail the gates
above. The other two directions remain unresolved construction problems.
The current result is a more precise search space and a checked public-list
filter, not a solution or evidence that a solution must exist. The goal
remains BLOCKED and the full proof remains unfinished. No Lean sources or
axiom-audit entries changed, so the prior passing Lean build does not need
to be repeated for this Python/documentation review.


Trying joint short-digit/control design after resumption, 2026-10-10
===================================================================

Authoritative get_goal now returns ACTIVE: the user resumed the goal. This
starts a fresh blocked audit. The preceding rethink was progress, because
it changed the research state and supplied a checked public-list filter that
changes the next construction gate. The full objective remains unchanged.
This continuation makes the proposed digit/control interface concrete.

Formal constraint on fixed public factors
----------------------------------------
GSWRedundantDigits.lean defines a constant affine control at a two-bit key s:

  C(s) = ciphertextMatrix(raw-H, s^T*raw+c).

For ANY fixed public factor D and target T, its product error, relative to
plaintext s_0 and gadget target T, has the exact selected-column form

  (c^T*D)_j
    + s_0*((H*D)_(0,j)-T_(0,j)+T_(1,j))
    + s_1*(H*D)_(1,j) + s_0*s_1*T_(2,j).

The raw mask cancels. Its four-corner alternating difference is T_(2,j).
Thus reconstruction G*D=T fixes the quadratic coefficient regardless of
redundancy, signs, digit norms or kernel corrections. If G*D=G and j is the
native second-mask-coordinate gadget column at weight w, one of the four
keys has centered error greater than B whenever 4B<|center(w)|. At w=q/2,
errors uniformly below q/8 are impossible in this particular fixed-factor
sampler family. For independent uniform two-bit keys the bad-key probability
is at least 1/4; that probability statement is a mathematical consequence,
not an additional Lean probability theorem.

The six checked theorems cover the affine phase, exact residual expression,
four-corner identity, bad reconstructed corner, actual native initial-gadget
column and preservation of reconstruction by a kernel addition. These are
scoped construction filters, not an ordinary-LWE security theorem. Fresh
key-independent additive errors can be absorbed into c when conditioning
on their draw. Factors chosen using only independent stored masks can be
conditioned on those masks, but factors chosen using key-dependent bodies
are not fixed across the four keys. The theorem does not exclude the latter,
private corrections, arbitrary alternative formats or weaker reachable-state
invariants which tolerate large errors. It also does not apply to the earlier
body-dependent compiler merely by declaring its H,c constant.

Actual public signed minimax decomposition
----------------------------------------
scripts/try-gsw-redundant-digits.py supplies an input-only public rule. For
each scalar x in Z/(2^L), choose a,b in {-1,0,1} and H satisfying

  x = a + 2b + 4H mod q,

by examining the two lifts x and x-q. Minimize

  max(|H|,|-H-b|,|H+a|),

the four midpoint-corner residuals, breaking ties first by |H|+|a|+|b| and
then by the fixed enumeration order. Encode H using signed-magnitude binary
digits at levels 2,...,L-1. Every digit is in {-1,0,1}; the rule has no secret,
source error or plaintext input. This changes D(G) while reconstructing all
inputs exactly. It is a concrete choice within the suggested co-design route,
not an unimplemented existence premise.

At q=8,...,512, 1,016 scalar reconstruction/shortness checks and 4,064 scalar
corner checks verify the lower bound in the formal constraint. The previous
56 midpoint cases, with identical key/source/control draws and both diagonal
variants, are then run through the actual NAND/refresh/next-refresh evaluator
using this rule. Every signed digit call checks exact reconstruction and
shortness, including intermediate states. There are 26,336 such calls and
11,652,096 reconstructed scalar entries. The paired results are:

  policy              correct/336  output margins  next margins  all-six cases
  unsigned                 168          34              18             0
  signed quarter minimax   236          96              16             8

Both policies check 14,592 control phases. The eight all-six successes occur
only at n=2. None of the signed n=3 or n=4 cases passes all six selected-output
checks. Correct selected output alone does not establish a reusable margin.
No key/source/control draw is resampled or discarded for correctness.
docs/GSWRedundantDigitExploration.json records the complete paired cases.

Dimension-scaling gate
---------------------
The small signed improvement justified a broader check of the native product
law. scripts/check-gsw-signed-digit-scaling.py draws uniform binary secrets
at n=2,4,8,16,32,64, with q=2^24 and fresh-error radii 0,1. It draws a uniform
second-control mask. The first raw mask is normalized to zero for diagnostics:
the exact identity e_product=e_first*D(second)+s_i*e_second eliminates that
mask, and e_first=delta(s)+fresh is independent of it. The resulting phase
law equals the law with a uniform first raw mask; this is an algebraic
normalization, not a claim that zero masks occur typically or meet source
hardness. These tests do not run a larger full refresh evaluator.

Each of 12 cases uses 256 samples; both digit policies check exact
reconstruction and the actual matrix product/phase identity. There are
6,144 product identities and 6,144 short reconstruction checks. Signed
selected products are correct in the following sample counts:

  n        E=0       E=1       (each out of 256)
  2        256       256
  4        235       233
  8        200       187
  16       162       149
  32       140       136
  64       126       132

The larger dimensions lose the small-case benefit even at zero fresh noise.
Zero-noise tests here isolate the algebra; they are not adopted as hard LWE
sources. docs/GSWSignedDigitScalingExploration.json retains unsigned counts,
counts by plaintext product bit, seeds and normalization details.

Scoped distributional explanation and exact counting bound
----------------------------------------------------------
For the full signed-outer-product midpoint variant, mask-block errors at
levels 0,1 are s_i*s_k and -(s_i XOR s_k). At higher levels they are
(1-2s_i)*(1-2s_k)*2^(ell-2). Thus that block contributes

  (1-2s_i)*(1-2s_k)*H_k
    + s_i*s_k*a_k - (s_i XOR s_k)*b_k

to zero-fresh-noise product error. The second stored mask scalars are
independent uniform conditional on the key. Write x_k=4u_k+r_k, Q=q/4,
and let z_k be the centered representative of u_k modulo Q. Outside the
single exceptional quarter u_k=Q/2, direct comparison of the 18 candidates
in the implemented minimizer gives this table:

  r    a                         b                         H
  0    0                         0                         z
  1    1 if z>=0 else -1          0 if z>=0 else -1          z if z>=0 else z+1
  2    0                         1 if z>=0 else -1          z if z>=0 else z+1
  3    -1                        0                         z+1

The comparison uses integer absolute values and the stated tie order.
Consequently |H_k-z_k|<=1 outside an event of probability 1/Q per block.
The script check-gsw-signed-quarter-law.py checks every entry of this explicit
table at q=8,...,4096: 8,144 regular scalar cases and 40 exceptional cases.
These finite checks corroborate the case derivation; the generic minimizer
table/coupling argument is not yet a Lean theorem.

With fresh control errors bounded by E, the remaining error after coupling
to sum_k (1-2s_i)*(1-2s_k)*z_k is bounded, outside that event, by

  R = 3n + ((n+1)*L+1)*E.

Here n accounts for H-z, 2n for the low digits, and the final term for all
first-control fresh errors and the second-control error. Dependence of the
body digits on the masks does not require an independence assumption: their
fresh-error contribution is bounded pointwise. The coupled z_k are independent
uniform consecutive-quarter variables. Multiplying them by fixed signs only
translates the convolution law. Any correctly decoded product therefore lies
in a shifted window of at most q/2+1+2R residues in that convolution, with an
additional exceptional probability at most n/Q. This is a mathematical
probability argument for this sampler/decomposition, not a Lean theorem or
a claim about all correlated controls and decompositions.

Exact integer circular convolution counts at q=65536 supply these resulting
upper bounds for selected product correctness (largest window mass+n/Q):

  n          E=0             E=1
  16      0.621085284      0.629349394
  32      0.527003826      0.543108815
  64      0.510548412      0.542311979

All six bounds use exact rational arithmetic in
docs/GSWSignedQuarterLawExploration.json. This explains the observed loss of
the signed improvement and rules out this concrete full-midpoint/signed rule
as a negligible-failure native multiplication construction at these parameters.
The bound is not transplanted to the exact-diagonal variant or arbitrary
body-adaptive decompositions. It also is not a full asymptotic impossibility
theorem for every parameter family.

Remaining construction requirements and verification
---------------------------------------------------
The signed candidate changes actual public evaluation and improves tiny
examples, but fails the scaling and repeatability gates. Fixed input-only
decompositions do not remove the quadratic initial-gadget coefficient of
constant affine controls. A substantive next design would have to exploit
body-dependent correlations or a different sampler/encoded state while
preserving complete ordinary-LWE source simulation. Merely re-randomizing
fixed digits or treating a successful n=2 case as a secure family cannot
complete the objective. A factor adapted to published source/control bodies
is not excluded by the fixed-factor theorem, but there is no useful efficient
rule for it yet. Private full-view simulation and nonlinear dense public
compilation likewise remain unresolved.

The full 9,250-job build of FormalProof4FHE and FormalProof4FHETest passes.
The extended 933-declaration GSWSelfKeyAudit contains all six new results;
its axiom output uses only propext, Classical.choice and Quot.sound, with
no sorryAx. Changed Lean/root/audit files are copied to the validated mirror.
The three new executables complete, their reported counts agree with the
JSON artifacts, Python syntax validation and git diff --check pass. No
additional paper or sub-agent was needed.

This resumed goal turn is progress: a concrete decomposition was tested,
a new native filter was proved in Lean, and scaling evidence determines the
next construction gate. The requested complete same-key security reduction
still has no compatible control sampler. The goal remains ACTIVE; this first
resumed continuation does not satisfy the three-turn blocked-audit threshold.
No completion claim, new security assumption or scope relaxation is made.


Body-adaptive factors and renewed remaining-route review, 2026-10-12
=================================================================

The previous signed-digit turn was progress: its formal fixed-factor
constraint and scaling experiments changed the next action. This continuation
implements the suggested body-adaptive action, then responds to the user's
fresh rethink/continue requests by strengthening the mixed-control test.
The unchanged-secret, ordinary-LWE-only full objective is preserved.

Concrete public single-control repair
------------------------------------
Given the stored native GSW control C_i, compute P_i using its ENTIRE public
matrix, including bodies, subject to

  G*P_i=C_i,       C_i*P_i=C_i.

The builder solves [G;C_i]*P_i=[C_i;C_i] by public unit-pivot elimination,
LLL kernel reduction and Babai nearest-plane coefficients. Nearby vectors
are formed as exact integer combinations of the verified reduced kernel
basis. An initial direct CVP.babai call returned a non-kernel vector on
the structured q=65536 test; exact certificate checks caught it. The new
coefficient reconstruction fixes that tooling failure. No invalid candidate
was accepted, no source was resampled, and the shared historical solver
and its previous research artifacts were not changed.

If elimination fails, the total experimental evaluator records the failure
and uses canonical signed digits for that control. No such builder failure
occurs in the final 56-factor test, but this finite observation is not a
negligible-failure or asymptotic shortness proof. GSO coefficients affect
shortness, while exact basis combinations and modular checks certify all
returned constraints independently of floating-point approximation quality.
Neither keys nor source errors are arguments to the builder.

Apply the control from the right during the public accumulator step:

  CMux_P(F,S)=S+(F-S)*P_i.

Exact reconstruction retains the original message law. GSWBodyAdaptiveProjection
checks these useful relations in Lean:

  (a*G+b*C_i)*P_i=(a+b)*C_i;
  CMux_P(a*G+b*C_i,c*G+d*C_i)=c*G+(a+b-c)*C_i;
  e_i*P_i=(1-s_i)*e_i, where e_i=v*C_i-s_i*v*G;
  G*P_i^k=C_i for every k>=1.

The last notation describes the executable iterateRight implementation; it
does not assert P_i^2=P_i on the whole ambient space. None of the sampled
factors is globally idempotent. The invariant controls repeated use of ONE
encrypted key bit on the span of G,C_i. It neither recovers all unknown-key
controls nor bounds the interaction of different controls.

scripts/try-gsw-body-adaptive-projection.py makes both the public builder and
right-acting evaluator executable. It uses 20 fixed source/control draws at
n=2,3,4; q=256,65536; unit source/fresh noise; and both midpoint diagonal
variants. Signed NAND and three refresh cycles are evaluated under one
unchanged secret. The paired totals are:

  policy                correct/120   output margins   next margins
  left signed                76            29               10
  right canonical            75            43               18
  right self-stable           84            44               18

All 56 public repairs satisfy 25,472 scalar certificate equations, using
3,008 preimage columns. Max digit magnitudes range from 4 to 413 and maximum
column l1 norms from 36 to 8,380. These are measured toy norms, not a useful
polynomial parameter guarantee. Seventeen uses of each repaired control
match the exact two-branch indicator formula in 203,776 scalar checks.
There are also 3,008 control-phase checks and 3,008 actual error-eigen checks.

Different controls satisfy the ordinary exact law

  error(C_i*P_j)=e_i*P_j+s_i*e_j.

All 6,720 cross-error scalar checks pass, but only 79 of 112 selected cross
products decode correctly. All 112 public cross commutators C_i*P_j-C_j*P_i
are nonzero. Three of 20 repaired cases pass all six selected-output checks;
that does not establish the invariant needed after another circuit gate.
docs/GSWBodyAdaptiveProjectionExploration.json includes failures/fallback
policy, norms, seeds, full trials and the cross diagnostics.

Strengthening the actual mixed-control construction
--------------------------------------------------
The user's additional rethink/continue request prompted a stronger public
joint builder, rather than another promise to search for a body-adaptive rule.
Solve for ALL P_i together with the previous reconstruction/self-stability
constraints and, for every i<j,

  C_i*P_j=C_j*P_i.

This is a linear modular system for each output column. Each variable block
holds one P_i column, and the pair rows use the publicly stored C_i,C_j as
coefficients. The same checked public short-preimage method is used. No
secret-dependent target or unknown noise vector is supplied to the solver.
The constraint is commutation ON G:

  G*(P_i*P_j-P_j*P_i)=C_i*P_j-C_j*P_i=0,

not a global commuting-operator statement. Two additional Lean results check
this identity and the resulting equality of cross errors:

  e_i*P_j+s_i*e_j=e_j*P_i+s_j*e_i.

That is an equality, not a small-noise bound. Higher interactions and powers
outside the single-control span remain unconstrained.

scripts/try-gsw-joint-projection.py tests eight matching source/control draws
at n=2,3 and q=256,65536, with unit noise and both diagonal variants. All
eight joint builders succeed. There are 336 preimage columns, 9,072 scalar
certificate equations, 50,688 repeated-single-control scalar checks, and 32
selected cross products (24 decode correctly). Digit maxima range from 7 to
637; measured max column l1 norms reach 12,195. Complete public refresh cycles
give 39 correct selected outputs in 48 calls, with 23 output margins and
seven conservative next-NAND/rounding margins. The identical eight-draw subset
of the independent-repair experiment gives:

  left signed       32/48 correct, 2 next margins;
  right canonical   31/48 correct, 6 next margins;
  independent repair 35/48 correct, 7 next margins;
  joint repair      39/48 correct, 7 next margins.

Three joint cases pass all six selected checks. The finite improvement does
not provide a negligible-error construction or a useful complete invariant.
docs/GSWJointProjectionExploration.json records the full joint trial and its
limitations. Increasing algebraic agreement while leaving error magnitude
uncontrolled does not fulfill the bootstrapping objective.

Fresh primary-paper check
-------------------------
Downloaded HJLM26, SNARGs for NP from Unprovability of Mathematical Theorems,
from https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/098/download/ to
refs/formalization/HJLM26.pdf. Its Chapter 8 formalizes GSW in bounded
arithmetic. Algorithm 2 on PDF page 68 generates d+1 independent keys and
encrypts each under the next; Theorem 8.10 is explicitly leveled. It does
not establish the requested unchanged-secret reusable theorem. The verified
135-page report is catalogued, cited and checksummed; refs now has 41 catalogued
papers. No additional security assumption is imported from its ambient theory.

Rethinking the remaining possible ways
------------------------------------
The first priority is now an ACTUAL MIXED-CONTROL NOISE INVARIANT for a public
compiler/operator construction. There is a concrete body-adaptive algorithm
to improve, and it avoids the fixed-factor four-corner proof's scope. Its
public source compilation has an existing ordinary-LWE reduction interface;
published factors are public postprocessing of that view. This observation
does not establish PPT bounds/parameters for a new implementation or correct
refresh, but it identifies the construction's present bottleneck accurately.
The missing object is a sampler/operator law with useful bounds for e_i*P_j
and every reachable accumulator error, reset after a full refresh and the
next gate, under hard source parameters. Single-control fixed points and
pairwise error equality do not supply it. No new useful law is constructed
in this review, and no independent KDM/SIS/leakage assumption may replace it.

The other substantive remaining targets are still:
* A private coupled-marginal simulator: generate the COMPLETE public-key and
  useful correlated-control law at an ordinary-LWE challenge. The MP12
  gadget-source body terms remain unknown; hiding the trapdoor or showing
  near-uniformity of its matrix marginal does not simulate those bodies.
* A direct ordinary-LWE reduction for stock GSW's augmented plaintext game,
  retaining real controls. The existing exact transports identify the game,
  but do not embed its complete unknown-secret view in an ordinary challenge.

Dense nonlinear body-dependent compilation belongs to the first construction
target. Feature/KDM/encoding cycles remain possible in principle, but no
fixed-size executable return conversion with complete same-key security is
available; they are not an additional ready approach. Exact joint operator
relations alone are not promoted as the next solution: the measured joint
trial has already exposed its missing noise bound. No global impossibility
claim follows from these limited failures, and a concrete verified theorem
or new mathematical construction could change the priority.

2026-10-15 rethink: matrix actions and the public refresh-token interface
======================================================================

The user requested another reconsideration of the remaining possibilities.
The unchanged objective is ordinary-LWE-only security, one unchanged encryption
secret, and repeated public refresh. This review does not resume or replace
the BLOCKED goal, and does not claim a new sampler or a completed reduction.
It examines two interfaces outside the tested scalar-control repairs and
adds precise filters for their simplest implementations.

1. Matrix-valued plaintext actions: a scoped square-zero obstruction
------------------------------------------------------------------
The scalar feature-span lower bound does not automatically exclude every
matrix-message encoding. Consider a different decryption matrix

  S = [ X | I_k ],       T_i(b) = I_k + b N_i,

where X is the secret-dependent block and N_i are fixed public k-by-k
matrices over the ciphertext ring. Ignoring noise only to examine the
target phase, S C_i = T_i(s_i) S G would require

  S G + s_i [ N_i X | N_i ] G.

One tempting way to remove the quadratic key dependence is to arrange a
public row submodule U with U X = 0 and every row of every N_i in U. Then
N_i X = 0. However, for u in U, the row u S = [0 | u] is public, so applying
it to C_i exposes the phase

  [0 | u] G + s_i [0 | u N_i] G.

In an exact or sufficiently accurate instance, a readable nonzero second
term can disclose s_i. A natural sufficient structural condition to avoid
that public-row signal is U N_i = 0 for every i. Under THESE two conditions,
every row of N_i annihilates N_j, hence

  N_i N_j = 0,
  T_i(a) T_j(b) = I_k + a N_i + b N_j.

The product has no ab term. Arbitrarily long products of these actions have
the same additive form. Inserting public transports B that preserve U does
not help: rows(N_i B) are still in U, so N_i B N_j = 0.

For a concrete orientation check, let k=2, X=(x,0)^T, U=span((0,1)),
N=E_01. Then N X=0, U N=0 and N^2=0. Adding the reverse shear E_10 makes
the product nonzero, but the public row (0,1) now sees a coefficient b in
the identity block of T(b) S. This is a noise-free algebraic example, not a
security theorem for noisy matrix ciphertexts.

Scope: U N_i=0 is an imposed sufficient hiding condition, not a necessary
condition for every secure encoding. This argument does not exclude
nonlinear encodings, different secret-dependent bases, noisy public-row
hiding, non-U-preserving transports with a new proof, or other matrix
actions. It also does not prove that threshold decoding of additive states
cannot implement any Boolean gate. It specifically defeats the proposed
noncommutative-product escape under this common-annihilator ansatz. A useful
matrix proposal must supply its actual nonzero mixed action, explain why
public rows do not reveal key bits, and give a secure return to a reusable
native format. Merely changing scalar messages to matrices supplies none
of these objects.

2. Reusable garbling: inspect who can encode the next input
---------------------------------------------------------
Downloaded GKP13 (STOC 2013, ten-page primary Microsoft-hosted copy) and
Applebaum17 (ECCC TR17-067, forty-page author survey) into
refs/randomized-encoding/. Both identities and page counts were checked.

GKP13 Section 4, PDF page 9, generates an input token by FE-encrypting
(sk,x); sk is part of the private garbling key. This gives reusable circuit
privacy with private token generation. Section 1.1.1, PDF page 3, separates
the public-encoding variant, which supplies input privacy. It does not
promise hiding a hardcoded decryption secret. Corollary 1.4 also uses
subexponential LWE, rather than directly the requested ordinary-LWE premise.
Thus its circuit-private theorem does not furnish a public refresh token.
These are interface and assumption mismatches in this paper, not an
impossibility claim about the restricted refresh-function family.

Applebaum17 Definition 4.5, PDF page 21, is explicitly static: later inputs
are chosen independently of prior encodings. The online encoder uses the
shared encoding randomness. Theorem 4.7 controls online complexity without
turning that encoder into a public algorithm for adaptive inputs. Repeated
refresh feeds earlier outputs into later calls; neither public adaptive
encoding nor its security follows from that theorem statement.

Primary sources:
  https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/2013-reusable-garbled.pdf
  https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2017/067/download/

3. An additional gate: the output encryption coins cannot all be known
---------------------------------------------------------------------
A naive public token for the function

  R_s(c;r) = Enc_pk(Dec_s(c);r)

immediately breaks plaintext privacy if the caller knows r and r specifies
ALL encryption randomness. On a challenge c*, evaluate R_s(c*;r), compute
Enc_pk(0;r) and Enc_pk(1;r), and compare. Correct deterministic encryption
with its full coins fixed gives distinct outputs for 0 and 1, so this reads
the challenge bit. With negligible correctness failure the same comparison
still succeeds except for that failure. This uses an honest challenge and
does not depend on malformed ciphertexts or on extracting secret-key bits.

Consequently, a special-purpose public refresh-token construction must
retain randomness hidden from the evaluator, or return a different native
ciphertext distribution with a separate security proof. Public caller coins
can be an input to such a mechanism, but cannot simply be the complete
known coins of a fresh output encryption. Native GSW evaluation is not
excluded: its public computation can retain hidden randomness from the
published ciphertexts. Protecting a hardcoded PRF seed might address this
particular gate, but publishing a program that uses that seed securely is
itself a missing construction, not something supplied by ordinary PRF
security or by the cited garbling theorem.

Revised priorities and honest stopping point
--------------------------------------------
The most economical remaining construction search is still a joint public
control sampler and body-dependent decomposition with a bound for actual
reachable refresh states. The existing ordinary-source compiler already
provides its security interface. The first useful new result would be a
concrete sampler and a closed distributional mixed-error law for repeated
refresh, with negligible total failure. Universal additive invariants,
independent projection repairs, and the tested joint gadget-commutation
constraint have not provided that law. No such new sampler was found here.

A more substantial change is a matrix encoding with useful noncommuting
actions outside the scoped annihilator ansatz. It must pass the public-row
filter before spending effort on a full bootstrapping implementation.

A specialized public refresh token also remains logically open, but now
has sharper requirements: public adaptive inputs, hidden output randomness,
same-secret reusable output, and a complete ordinary-LWE joint reduction.
Generic reusable garbling is not an off-the-shelf solution. Proving only
static RE privacy, public input privacy, or known-coin reencryption would
not meet these requirements.

The stock-GSW retained-control plaintext-game reduction remains another
unresolved mathematical theorem, without a new challenge embedding in this
review. It should not be advertised as a ready algorithm. Likewise, the
private trapdoor route still needs an actual full-view simulator.

Artifacts and verification
--------------------------
The reference catalog now has 43 entries; the two additions have matching
PDF metadata, bibliography entries, index notes and SHA-256 records. The
complete 43-paper catalog/hash/bibliography/index consistency check passes.
An exact-integer check of the displayed two-dimensional matrix example
passes 120 cases over moduli 2, 4, 8 and 16, checking the action product and
the reverse-shear public coefficient. This verifies orientations in the
example and is not evidence for an asymptotic cryptographic claim.
The repository diff whitespace check passes. The
algebra and known-coin comparison above are mathematical derivations, not
new Lean theorems or asymptotic security proofs. No Lean source changed,
so the previously validated 9,251-job build and 941-declaration audit are
not rerun or presented as evidence for the new derivations. The full
objective remains unfinished and the goal remains BLOCKED. This rethink
narrows the missing interfaces; it does not assert all constructions are
impossible or manufacture an implementation-ready candidate.


2026-10-15 resumed goal: globally commuting factors and their public spectrum
===========================================================================

The explicit goal continuation resumed the original objective. get_goal
returned ACTIVE. This starts a fresh resumed blocked audit; the earlier
BLOCKED decision is historical, not the current operational status. The
preceding rethink counts as progress: it added two verified primary papers
and changed the candidate filters with explicit matrix-action and known-coin
arguments. It was not a wait on any process. The full objective remains the
unchanged-secret ordinary-LWE-only bootstrappable GSW theorem.

Next concrete gate
------------------
The existing joint builder imposed commutation only on the gadget row space.
A stronger proposed repair is to demand globally commuting, idempotent
public factors P_i with G P_i = C_i. This makes each C_i self-stable and
provides an exact finite algebra for all their products. The present
continuation tests whether that stronger algebra closes mixed correctness.
It does not. There are two distinct results, with different proof status.

A. Public spectral list: mathematical derivation and finite verification
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
Suppose P_1,...,P_n are pairwise commuting idempotent N-by-N matrices over
Z/(2^L), L>=1. For a binary pattern b define

  E_b = product_i (P_i if b_i=1, I-P_i if b_i=0).

These matrices are pairwise orthogonal idempotents and sum to I. Any nonzero
idempotent remains nonzero modulo two: if E=2K, then E=E^(2^t) is divisible
by 2^(2^t), and eventually vanishes modulo 2^L. The nonzero reductions of
the E_b have nonzero, pairwise independent images over F_2, so at most N
patterns have E_b nonzero. Let S(P) be this public set of patterns.

This is efficiently enumerable without visiting 2^n labels. Start with
(empty label,I), and split every nonzero prefix projector E into E-E P_i
and E P_i. At every depth the surviving projectors are pairwise orthogonal
and nonzero modulo two, so there are at most N. Matrix arithmetic therefore
gives a polynomial algorithm in n,N,L. It uses only the public factors;
no secret, source error or hidden diagonalizing basis enters the algorithm.

Construct a public decision trie for the Boolean function

  f_P(b) = 1 if b is NOT in S(P), and 0 otherwise.

There are at most 2*n*N+1 nodes. Evaluate its leaves as G or zero, and a
node querying bit i by rightCMux P_i first second. Because the spectral
patterns exhaust the identity, this exact matrix evaluation returns zero.
This is true for every G, independent of any control-noise magnitude.

Consequently, IF an intended invariant guarantees semantic correctness on
this very public-factor-dependent, polynomial-size, key-selected decision
tree, the actual binary secret must lie in S(P), except on its correctness
failure event. Otherwise f_P(s)=1 but the ciphertext is the literal zero
matrix, which decodes as zero. This implication does not assume even errors,
an approximately common eigenvector, or a bound on the norms of all E_b.

The correctness premise must be kept explicit. Correctness only on the
particular decryption/refresh computations does not automatically include
this adaptively chosen trie. This is NOT an unconditional attack on the
current bootstrap algorithm, ordinary GSW, arbitrary decompositions, or
operand-dependent factors. It filters a proposed strong universal invariant
for fixed globally commuting idempotent factors.

Under a hypothetical public compiler with that correctness property, a
standard source distinguisher can check the public list with t additional
independent validation columns withheld from the compiler. If the real
source validation errors are bounded by B with failure at most eta, and
the builder/correctness failure is at most epsilon, then, provided
2B+1<q, its advantage is at least

  1 - epsilon - eta - N*((2B+1)/q)^t.

In the uniform source, condition on the compiler's entire input and list;
the withheld uniform bodies remain independent, giving the displayed union
bound even though the list depends on the original transcript. The source
must allow those extra same-secret samples, and any block-binary source
transfer must account for them in its existing ordinary-LWE loss. No binary
LWE hardness, leakage premise, SIS premise or circular premise is inserted.
This reduction sketch and the spectral cardinality argument are not yet
Lean computational-security theorems.

Executable evidence:
  python3 scripts/check-gsw-commuting-projections.py \
    --output docs/GSWCommutingProjectionExploration.json

The script generates 24 conjugated diagonal families over moduli 4,16,256,
widths 3,6 and key-label lengths 2,4,6,64. Every public enumeration agrees
with its independently retained construction labels. For n<=6, 504 exhaustive
pattern products independently check the support. All 24 constructed
complement tries evaluate to zero and satisfy the polynomial size bound.
The 64-bit cases use the public prefix algorithm without exhaustive key
enumeration. These are exact finite checks, not asymptotic hardness evidence.

B. Native-gadget failure despite the stronger identities: Lean witness
---------------------------------------------------------------------
For q=16, use the existing binary native GSW gadget with three rows and
twelve columns, and the unchanged key s=(1,1). Write v=(1,-1,-1)G, and let
e_j denote the j-th standard coordinate row (zero-based indexing). Set

  v_1 = v+e_1,        a_1 = 5e_0^T-e_2^T,
  v_2 = v+e_2,        a_2 = 3e_0^T-e_1^T,
  P_i = a_i v_i,     C_i = G P_i.

The pairings satisfy v_i a_j = 1 when i=j and 0 otherwise. Also v a_i=1.
It follows that P_i^2=P_i and P_1 P_2=P_2 P_1=0. Thus all the desired
global commutation, idempotence, reconstruction and self-stability equations
hold. Nevertheless, (1,-1,-1)C_i=v+e_i, so both controls decode as ONE with
noise at most ONE, while C_1 P_2=0 decodes as ZERO. The corresponding NAND
returns G, which decodes as ONE rather than the expected ZERO.

The actual cross error is not small: e_1 P_2 + e_2 = -v. Its selected body
coordinate has magnitude q/2. Commuting idempotents therefore cannot by
themselves replace the missing mixed-error bound, even with initially
correct, small-error controls. A shortness/correctness hypothesis would
exclude this witness and must itself be established for a real sampler.

The new module GSWCommutingProjection proves this q=16 witness in Lean,
including its actual native noise bounds and decryption results. Its 15
theorems are registered in GSWSelfKeyAudit. The script also checks the same
construction at q=256 and q=65536; all three have error one, wrong mixed
product/NAND bits and the public pattern list {00,01,10}, excluding the
actual key 11. Their seven-node complement tries also return zero.

Limitations: these are deliberately algebraic counterexamples, not draws
from a secure control distribution. The factors are not canonical digits
and are not short. The explicit controls do not claim to hide their key.
The counterexample refutes using the stronger identities ALONE as the
proposed correctness invariant, and not every possible commuting-factor
construction with a carefully restricted reachable-state law.

Effect on the remaining construction search
------------------------------------------
Global commutation and idempotence are not the missing repair to implement
next. They leave mixed errors uncontrolled, and a sufficiently strong
universal correctness claim for their fixed algebra admits the public-list
filter above. A useful new invariant must concern the actual reachable
refresh states, or use a different/noncommuting/operand-dependent algebra,
and still come with a concrete ordinary-source-compatible sampler. This
continuation does not find that sampler or a full-view private simulator.
The whole requested security theorem remains unfinished.

This resumed turn makes concrete progress by checking the stronger proposal
and formalizing its native failure, rather than repeating a passing old
trial or introducing a new security assumption. The goal remains ACTIVE;
no completion or new BLOCKED claim is made during this first resumed turn.
Final validation results for this continuation follow below.

2026-10-15 resumed continuation: operand-dependent public dual factors
====================================================================

The previous goal turn is classified as progress: it added the checked
GSWCommutingProjection native counterexample and the public-spectrum filter,
with a passing full build and focused axiom audit. All associated processes
were terminal. get_goal still returns ACTIVE. This is the second turn of
the current resumed audit, with the original objective intact.

A concrete candidate outside the fixed commuting-factor model
------------------------------------------------------------
The remaining reachable-state proposal is made executable here, rather
than adding another conditional correctness wrapper. For public native
gadget G and control C, solve for an N-by-r right inverse H satisfying

  G H = I_r,       C H = I_r,       P = H C.

Then G P=C, C P=C and P^2=P. Different P need not commute. More importantly,
H can be recomputed for each actual CMux branch difference Delta, so this
is outside the previous fixed globally commuting factor filter.

The first public builder finds a short base H by modular elimination and
LLL/Babai, with exact kernel-basis and affine-equation certificates. For
the operand-dependent variant, let R select the lowest gadget column of
each row, so G R=I_r. For column j of H, choose a representative h in the
same affine solution coset minimizing the heuristic public objective

  J_Delta,j(h) = ||h||_2^2 +
                ||centered(Delta h - (Delta R)_j)||_2^2.

The optimization is approximate lattice nearest-plane search. Its embedded
lattice consists of [v,Delta v-q z] for v in the exact modular kernel of
the stacked [G;C] system. Reduced basis vectors and final solutions are
certified with integer arithmetic. The zero correction remains available;
a candidate with a worse objective than the base column is rejected using
only public data. Full reconstruction, stabilization and global idempotence
of each returned P are checked exactly. No secret, source error, diagnostic
plaintext or selected correctness result enters this builder or its metric.

This objective tries to retain the actual branch's action on a public
compression R while preserving the self-stability constraints. It is a
heuristic, not a claimed bound on the hidden-key phase. The experiment
tests that distinction directly.

Reproduction and actual candidate trials
---------------------------------------
  PYTHONPATH=/tmp/fhe-preimage-packages \
    python3 scripts/try-gsw-operand-adaptive-dual.py \
      --output docs/GSWOperandAdaptiveDualExploration.json

The six fixed source draws use (n,levels,seed)=(2,8,15000),
(2,16,15010),(3,8,15020), each with exact-diagonal and full signed-product
midpoint variants. Fresh source/control noise radius is one. Source draws,
keys and controls are never resampled. These match six cases in the earlier
independent body-adaptive projection artifact. No cryptographically hard
asymptotic family is claimed for these small instances.

All 14 base right-inverse builders succeed, with zero fallbacks. Every one
of the ten distinct base-factor pairs fails to commute, so the candidate
actually leaves the previously filtered globally commuting model. Across
the complete three-cycle refresh/NAND runs, the script certifies 73
idempotent factors; 59 differ from their corresponding base factor because
of the actual operand. There are 89 distinct cached operands. All 215
optimized columns improve the public objective, and none is rejected:
total squared objective falls from 37,288,009,744 to 29,292.

Actual plaintext/noise evidence does not follow that metric improvement:

  Method                    Selected correct  Output margins  Next margins
  Fixed public dual              19/36            14              5
  Operand-adaptive public dual   20/36            11              3
  Earlier matched self-stable    25/36             --              7

The earlier matched comparison is read from the saved independent-factor
artifact, checking n,levels,seed,variant and secret diagnostics against
each new case; no more favorable draws are selected. Neither new method
has a case with all six selected outputs correct.

The diagnostic separately computes the actual rounded input plaintext.
For BOTH methods all 12 first-cycle rounded input bits equal their intended
plaintexts, but only eight first-cycle outputs decode correctly. Thus four
first refreshes fail even before any invalid earlier output is reused.
Across all 36 calls, fixed-dual evaluation agrees with the actual rounded
input in 22 cases and operand-adaptive evaluation in 23 cases. This avoids
attributing every later intended-plaintext mismatch to matrix evaluation
when the input has already left the refresh margin.

The dual entries are small in the finite runs (case maxima 4 through 8),
but the centered factor entries reach q/2 in EVERY case: 128 at q=256 and
32768 at q=65536. Actual control errors range from 32 to 33 at q=256 and
8191 to 8193 at q=65536. A short H is not a short P=H C, and the matrix
agreement objective is not a certificate of controlled native phase error.

Why the missing bound is still missing
-------------------------------------
Let u be the actual decryption row, C have intended control bit b, S have
message a and Delta have message d. Write

  e_C = u C - b u G,      e_Delta = u Delta - d u G,
  e_S = u S - a u G.

For the actual operand-dependent H, G H=I still gives the exact identity

  u(S+Delta H C) - (a+d*b)uG =
    e_S + d*e_C + (e_Delta H)C.

The public objective controls Delta H-Delta R, not e_Delta H and not its
product with C. Even the extra identity C H=I only implies

  e_C H = (1-b)u.

It does not annihilate cross errors or bound their action on another full
control. There is no valid replacement here for the real mixed-error
transfer law. The finite first-cycle failures show that supplying the
missing bound for this heuristic would be false on the recorded draws.
These identities are mathematical consequences of the existing matrix
noise equations, not newly added Lean security theorems.

accepted without the integer affine and reconstruction certificates.

not rerun or used as proof of this new heuristic. The reference collection

assumption. The same single key is retained for every refresh in every case.

2026-10-16 rethink: certified refresh and privately randomized certificates
========================================================================

The user requested another reconsideration after the blocked audit. The
original same-secret ordinary-LWE objective and the prior BLOCKED decision
remain intact. get_goal still returns BLOCKED; this review does not create
a replacement goal or claim that an unimplemented research route is a
completed construction. The new work examines two possible escapes from
the previous filters and revises priorities accordingly.

A different refresh interface: validity certificates and a hidden helper
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Instead of supplying generic secret-bit controls, consider a public helper
which accepts a ciphertext together with a proof of a permitted encryption/
evaluation history, then returns a refreshed ciphertext under the original
secret. Correctness could be restricted to this certified language, avoiding
the unsupported inference from correctness on a particular refresh family
to correctness on every arbitrary key-selected decision trie.

This restriction does not alone implement the helper or prove its privacy.
In particular, the ciphertext must remain public-key encryptable: an honest
user must efficiently construct an accepted initial ciphertext/proof using
the public data. The output must also retain hidden randomness; the earlier
known-output-coins comparison attack still applies if a helper merely uses
the caller's complete encryption coins.

Checked WZ17, Obfuscating Compute-and-Compare Programs under LWE, in the
official twelve-page FOCS proceedings PDF, now saved in refs. Its pure-LWE
guarantee requires conditional target pseudo-entropy given the function and
auxiliary data (PDF pages 2,8). The unpredictability-only variant adds
assumptions. Its multi-bit form releases a fixed payload. These conditions
must be checked before treating this primitive as a hidden refresh helper.

Primary source:
  https://ieee-focs.org/FOCS-2017-Papers/3464a600.pdf
Canonical full-version record:
  https://eprint.iacr.org/2017/276
The downloaded file is the proceedings version, not the full ePrint.

Our accepting-input argument for the proposed application
-------------------------------------------------------
Write a compute-and-compare program as CC[f,y](x), accepting precisely when
f(x)=y. Suppose the public data aux alone permits a PPT algorithm Gen to
create an accepted input with probability epsilon. Then the predictor

  Predict(f,aux;r) = f(Gen(aux;r))

predicts y with exactly that same probability epsilon. The security
condition deliberately gives this predictor the unobfuscated function f;
it does not matter whether a real evaluator knows the secret inside f.
Thus, if honest public input generation succeeds with overwhelming
probability, the target is not computationally unpredictable given
(f,aux). Nor can it have superlogarithmic conditional HILL pseudo-entropy:
an efficient equality test using Predict separates that distribution from
one whose conditional guessing probability is at most 2^(-h).

This gives a quantitative first gate. If the HILL comparison has error
delta, an efficiently generated accepting input must satisfy

  epsilon <= 2^(-h) + delta.

It cannot at the same time have epsilon=1-negligible and superlogarithmic h.
Adding a secret tag to the plaintext does not solve this if a public tagged
encryption can still generate an accepted input: the predictor evaluates f
on that generated ciphertext and obtains the lock value.

Scope: this concerns the proposed direct application of this particular
entropy/evasiveness guarantee, when the public accepted-input generator
uses precisely the auxiliary data in that guarantee. It is not a theorem
excluding every certified refresh helper, every obfuscator, or a different
proof game. A construction requiring the target obfuscation itself to
generate accepting inputs needs a separate dependency/security analysis;
it cannot silently treat that obfuscation-dependent information as free
auxiliary input satisfying the same entropy premise.

There is also a separate fixed-payload problem. If a naive helper returns
one fixed encryption Z_0 on plaintext zero and one fixed encryption Z_1 on
plaintext one, a public user can produce valid encryptions/proofs of zero
and one and query the helper to learn both output fingerprints. Querying
the helper on the honest challenge and comparing the output reveals its
bit. Correctness makes Z_0 and Z_1 distinct. A reusable helper needs a
different output mechanism with input-dependent hidden randomness; fixed
secret payloads alone are insufficient. This is our elementary comparison
argument, not a new general impossibility assertion about certified refresh.

A second possible escape: keep the short certificate's coins private
-------------------------------------------------------------------
The earlier signed-source trace attack should not be overextended to every
private witness. Conversely, hiding independent coins does not automatically
escape it. Consider a public randomized sampler S(X;r) whose input X is
exactly a source instance covered by GSWPublicPreimageBarrier, and whose
output is a family of certificates satisfying its coefficient equations,
norm budget and decoding margin. These equations and norms are public.

An adversary need not recover the generator's particular r or its particular
certificate. It runs S again on the same public X with fresh independent
coins. The already proved pointwise recover_lifted_sources theorem works
for EVERY certificate satisfying those hypotheses. If the sampler's good
certificate event has probability epsilon and the requisite source-error
event fails with probability eta, fresh sampling recovers the key with
probability at least epsilon-eta. A separate withheld validation block can
test the candidate in the existing source distinguishing experiment. There
is no requirement to find the original private sampler coins.

This observation only applies when the sampler is efficiently executable
from the public X with publicly sampleable fresh coins, and its actual
input law is the signed/lifted source law of the attack. It does not move
the certificate equations or success promise to an unrelated matrix law.
It also does not cover a sampler that requires the private key, a private
trapdoor, or coins correlated with key generation that cannot be sampled
conditionally from X. Those cases return to the genuine missing joint
simulation question. Nor does this argument exclude arbitrary nonlinear
body-dependent compilers outside the stated certificate class. It is a
precise filter on the private-independent-coins proposal.

Revised ordering of the remaining research work
----------------------------------------------
1. Prioritize the actual plaintext-security hop of the already correct
   recursive-feature family. The authoritative
   RecursiveFeatureFHECorrectness.eventually_family_correctExp_probability_one
   already handles arbitrary finite shared NAND programs under one sampled
   key. RecursiveFeatureControlPolynomial.family_oneTime_abs_signedAdvantage_le
   leaves familyControlReplacementAdvantage for the cubic controls, with
   the actual familyObserver. A reduction for that observer is enough;
   a stronger generic full-KDM theorem is not required by this interface.
   The first missing object is a concrete ordinary-LWE challenge embedding
   or hybrid for that exact correlated game, with efficient reductions and
   negligible losses. This is a revised priority, not a new proof: no such
   embedding has been constructed in this review.

2. If changing key generation, use a genuinely private coupled sampler
   whose COMPLETE public law can be simulated in the relevant plaintext
   game from ordinary LWE. Merely hiding fresh public-sampler coins is not
   enough in the certificate class above. A useful candidate must explain
   how the simulator produces all public bodies and retains correlations
   despite its unknown source key. A uniform matrix marginal or a correct
   private-preimage toy does not supply that simulator. This first gate
   precedes more correctness experiments for another private sampler.

3. A certified refresh interface remains a different construction route,
   because its permitted input language may avoid the universal-invariant
   filters. It needs a function-private public helper with publicly
   generable accepted inputs, input-dependent hidden output randomness,
   same-secret reusable output and a complete ordinary-LWE game proof.
   The checked compute-and-compare theorem does not implement that interface.
   No approval to introduce iO, random oracles, ELFs, circular security,
   private online tokens or an acyclic key chain is inferred or requested.

The failed factor metrics are not promoted to another implementation-ready
candidate. These three items describe different changes to a proof, key
generation, and the refresh interface. They do not assert that a complete
construction is ready, that the remaining space is exhaustive, or that all
other constructions are impossible.

and both authors were checked. Its catalog, bibliography, index and SHA256

The full security theorem remains unfinished and the goal remains BLOCKED.

Known-bit conditioning of the actual cubic-control hop, 2026-10-17
----------------------------------------------------------------
The goal has resumed. This continuation implements the first algebraic
component of a proposed reduction for the actual cubic-control game, then
checks whether conditioning/guessing can supply a complete simulator.

The candidate
-------------
For a set K of known key coordinates with fixed values a, a control message
has the form w*s_i*s_j*s_k. If i is in K, it equals

  (w*a_i) * s_j * s_k.

The same construction works with j or k as the known factor. Thus a single
cubic control has an exact quadratic representation after knowing one of
its bits. The new RecursiveFeatureConditioning.conditionedCubic chooses
such a quadratic polynomial. message_conditionedCubic proves the identity
under agreement on K; conditioned_control_message connects it directly to
RecursiveFeatureGSW.controlMessage for an original-bit control and a
quadratic feature. This is a positive local simulation component rather
than merely an abstract degree argument.

The actual retained control table includes every ordered triple of three
distinct coordinates, at every gadget level. The per-level count is
n*(n-1)*(n-2); the six permutations of each unordered triple do not change
the known-coordinate covering condition. For this construction to cover
every genuine cubic column, K must intersect every three-element subset.

The checked coversCubics_iff_unknown_card_le_two states exactly that this
condition is equivalent to |K-complement| <= 2. known_card_lower_bound gives
n <= |K|+2. The necessity here concerns covering by this explicit known-factor
degree-reduction template, not all conceivable simulations of the table.

Why the two direct reduction variants stop
-----------------------------------------
1. Guess fixed values without the key. compatible_key_count_le_four proves
   that at most four binary keys agree on a covering K. The checked uniform
   sampling statement uniform_guess_probability_le_four_div bounds the
   agreement probability by 4/2^n. Thus a naive full-prefix guessing simulator
   has an exponential success loss. This bound concerns a fixed guess
   independent of the uniformly sampled key. It is not a bound on guesses
   correlated with a public LWE view, which need separate analysis.

2. Condition the source on a known prefix. There are then at most four key
   candidates. The source-entropy estimate used by RecursiveQuadraticParameters
   has expression sqrt((q^d-1)/2^u)/2 when u uniform bits remain. The checked
   low_entropy_source_gap_ge_quarter shows that expression is at least 1/4
   whenever q>=2, d>=1 and u<=2. It therefore cannot become negligible by
   substituting this conditioned key size into the existing estimate.
   This lower bound on a proof's UPPER-BOUND LOSS TERM does not lower-bound
   actual distinguishing advantage and does not prove insecurity. Nor does
   this calculation itself provide a conditional-source reduction.

At the actual parameter family t=lambda+1, q=2^(t^2), d=t^3 and the original
binary-key dimension n=2*t^5. The existing source theorem obtains its entropy
margin from the entire binary key. A simulator cannot condition away n-2
bits and retain that old bound merely by citing the unconditioned theorem.

Why a one-bit/one-column hybrid is still a separate missing construction
---------------------------------------------------------------------
Guessing one factor of one selected cubic costs only a constant. This does
not yet simulate an intermediate experiment: the observer receives the
complete public table and all stored original-bit control columns. The
remaining columns are correlated with the same unknown key. They are not
independent auxiliary samples the ordinary-LWE challenger supplies.

In particular, the complete-view interface from
RecursiveFeatureControlPolynomial.familyObserver allows the adversary's
message choice and stored state to depend on the entire published view.
There is no restriction to reading one column or to the columns explicitly
used by a particular honest refresh. Replacing one column at a time still
requires generating the other real cubic columns with their joint law.
Knowing only one coordinate leaves all triples on its complement uncovered.

This identifies the missing simulator object; it is not a theorem that all
single-column hybrids fail. A reduction with different source transport,
an efficient coupled sampler, or a direct analysis of the plaintext-game
observer could avoid this exact-template obstacle. No such computational
simulator, conditional-source theorem, or negligible cubic-hop bound is
constructed here. In particular, the stronger claim that all key conditioning
is impossible is not asserted.

Remaining concrete proof targets
-------------------------------
The first target remains a complete ordinary-LWE reduction for
familyControlReplacementAdvantage with the actual familyObserver. The local
conditioned polynomial now exists, but its whole-table completion has failed
the entropy/guessing gate. Another proposed hybrid must explain explicitly
where every other real cubic column comes from. Calling those columns
arbitrary auxiliary input would add an unproved security premise.

The second target is a private coupled key/control sampler with a simulator
for the ENTIRE public law. A private preimage witness or a uniform public
matrix marginal alone remains insufficient. The specialized certified-helper
route remains open with the accepted-input and hidden-output-randomness
requirements recorded in the preceding rethink. These are research targets,
not claims of ready constructions or an exhaustive list of possibilities.

The existing same-key correctness theorem is preserved. No independent
circular, KDM, binary-LWE, leakage, obfuscation, or source-conditioning
assumption is introduced. No extra paper is needed for the finite algebra,
counting, probability and loss-expression proofs in this continuation; the
reference catalog remains at 44 papers. The original full security goal
remains ACTIVE and unfinished after this first resumed continuation.

Affine processing of a complete LWE source, 2026-10-17
----------------------------------------------------
The previous goal turn made progress: it added the exact conditioned cubic
polynomial, proved its full-table covering/entropy costs, and validated seven
new results. This continuation tests a different simulator family, allowing
public affine mixing of every source mask and noisy output across the entire
batch. It does not repeat a known-bit guessing trial or assume that other
cubic columns are freely available as auxiliary input.

The source and proposed output law
--------------------------------
Let s be a uniform n-bit OUTPUT key, n=3+k. Let the input source key be
t=t(s) in (Z/qZ)^d, for ANY fixed function t and any source dimension d.
The two keys need not be identical. Uniform source masks A are independent
of s, and source outputs are y=t(s)^T*A+e. Fix independent errors e and
simulator coins. An arbitrary public affine simulator for one output
recursive ciphertext is

  Cipher = F(A,y) + C,

where F is a Z/qZ-linear map of the COMPLETE input transcript and C is a
fixed ciphertext. It may mix masks, outputs, and all columns. The current
quadratic publicShift/project construction is a special case; shifting only
one predetermined matrix coefficient is not the scope of this test.

The native recursive phase is linear in Cipher for fixed s. Therefore

  phase_s(F(A,t(s)^T*A+e)+C) = L_s(A) + Q_e(s),

where L_s is a linear map of A and

  Q_e(s) = phase_s(F(0,e)+C)

is a FIXED quadratic polynomial of the output key. The new
offsetPolynomial/message_offsetPolynomial and affine_source_phase prove
these facts directly for the actual RecursiveQuadratic.Ciphertext and its
decryption function. The source-key map t can be nonlinear in s; the
linearity being used is in A after the key is fixed.

The quantitative obstruction
----------------------------
Set q=2^ell and w=q/2, and let H satisfy 8*H < |w|_q. The actual targeted
control message is w*s_0*s_1*s_2. The earlier checked cube-difference count
shows that for at least 1/8 of uniform binary output keys,

  |Q_e(s)-w*s_0*s_1*s_2|_q > H.

For each such key, either L_s=0, in which case every mask fails, or its image
is a nonzero subgroup of Z/(2^ell)Z. Every such subgroup contains q/2. The
existing checked exists_half_turn theorem supplies a source shift h with
L_s(h)=q/2. Translation A -> A+h is injective and sends every good mask to
a bad mask: if both phases were within H of the target, the modular
triangle inequality would give |q/2|_q <= 2*H, a contradiction.

Thus at least half the masks fail for each of those bad-offset keys. The
new affine_bad_source_count proves this pairing, cubic_affine_failure_sum
performs the key/mask count, and source_simulator_failure_sum instantiates
the complete source map. The checked probability result is

  source_simulator_failure_probability:
    Pr_{s,A}[ |phase_s(F(A,t(s)^T*A+e)+C)-w*s_0*s_1*s_2|_q > H ] >= 1/16.

This holds for EVERY fixed e and independent fixed simulator coins, so
averaging over any independent error/coin distribution preserves the bound.
That last averaging observation is a mathematical consequence of the
uniform pointwise bound, not a newly formalized sampler theorem. The Lean
probability theorem itself uses a uniform pair (s,A) and fixed e,F,C.
The current recursive-family top weight is q/2, and the existing eventual
fresh-control margin is stronger than 8*H<q/2, so the gate is relevant to
the current correctly refreshing family. No computational hardness premise
is used by this phase/counting theorem.

A sharp diagnostic, and why public mask rejection is a separate route
--------------------------------------------------------------------
The executable uses the native quadratic phase, with n=3 and a single LWE
row y=s_0*a_0+s_1*a_1+s_2*a_2+e. Set only the output matrix entry

  T[0,1] = w*(a_0+a_1-y),

and all other matrix/vector/body entries to zero. This is a public affine
map. Its exact phase on binary keys is

  w*s_0*s_1*s_2*a_2 + w*s_0*s_1*e.

When e=0, all keys other than 111 have the target phase; key 111 fails on
exactly half the masks. This attains failure 1/16 and shows that the older
fixed-quadratic 1/8 approximation bound cannot simply be asserted for this
larger source-dependent affine class. For odd e the same map fails with
probability 3/16. These witnesses are executable exact finite calculations,
not additional Lean sharpness or security theorems. They have degenerate
output masks and no claimed fresh recursive-ciphertext distribution.

Now publicly retain only rows with a_2 odd. This changes the input-mask law,
so the affine theorem's uniform-mask premise no longer applies. The same
diagnostic becomes exact for e=0, but for odd e the remaining error is
w*s_0*s_1, and exactly 1/4 of keys fail. The executable confirms this on
the native phase. Thus public rejection is not ruled out in general; this
particular proposal has not removed the amplified source error, even before
the full fresh output distribution is addressed.

Requiring all input errors to be even would make binary-source equations
noiseless modulo two; a full-rank parity mask block would reveal that binary
key. That is not a justified replacement source-hardness assumption. A
different rejection/noise-handling proposal must supply its actual joint
source law and ordinary-LWE reduction rather than assume access to source
error parity. Also, this three-bit diagnostic does not establish a useful
large-dimension source-hardness family.

Exact scope and revised next actions
-----------------------------------
The filter covers residue-linear affine processing of a complete source,
with uniform masks independent of the uniform binary output key, a fixed
source-key map t(s), and coefficients/errors independent of that output key
and masks. It also covers the same-key special case and independent public
randomized choices of such affine maps. Allowing a different but fixed
mask-independent hidden-key transport does not by itself escape the proof.

It does NOT cover nonlinear transcript processing, public mask rejection,
mask-dependent hidden-key coupling, or simulator coefficients/coins that are
privately correlated with the output key. Nor does it give a distinguisher
for the public scheme: its event uses the hidden output key. A public-only
game simulation need not preserve this particular hidden-key joint law.
Those distinctions matter for the actual familyObserver security target.

The next direct-reduction candidate must therefore use genuinely nonlinear
source processing, a different public-only joint-game argument, or another
mechanism outside this affine coupling. Another affine shift or a fixed
key-coordinate change is no longer a useful next implementation under these
premises. A private coupled sampler remains a separate route if its complete
public output can be simulated; key-dependent private coefficients are not
excluded by this result. The certified-helper interface remains open with
the earlier accepted-input and hidden-output-randomness requirements.

None of these remaining routes currently has a concrete complete simulator
or an ordinary-LWE proof of the genuine cubic-control replacement. The
existing family_oneTime_abs_signedAdvantage_le still retains
familyControlReplacementAdvantage. No missing term is declared negligible,
and no circular, KDM, leakage, binary-LWE or acyclic-key premise is added.
The full requested theorem is unfinished. This second resumed continuation
makes substantive progress on the simulator gate and leaves the goal ACTIVE.

executable scripts/check-gsw-affine-source.py counts exact scalar image
cosets and separately checks complete raw-source evaluations. Its saved
GSWAffineSourceExploration.json records 192 same-key affine maps with 576
fixed-error cases, 104 transported-key maps with 312 fixed-error cases,
55,296 exhaustive raw-source draw checks, fifteen sharpness cases, and
three public-rejection cases. Transport tests include a bijection packing
four uniform output bits into a uniform Z/16Z source secret. These finite
cases test phase/counting identities, not a secure fresh sampler or strict
polynomial-time asymptotic implementation. No references were added; the
catalog remains at 44 papers.
