2026-10-18 -- IMPLEMENTED PUBLIC-RERANDOMIZATION FEASIBILITY PLAN

The user allowed ciphertext/refresh-format changes while preserving ordinary
LWE, one unchanged secret and repeatable GSW bootstrapping, and selected the
feasibility gate before further formalization. This continuation implements
that gate. The full goal was resumed and is ACTIVE, not completed. A failed
candidate is not substituted for the requested security theorem.

Implementation:
  scripts/check-gsw-public-rerandomization.py
  docs/GSWPublicRerandomizationExploration.json

The existing scripts/check-gsw-signed-source-cancellation.py now additionally
exports local_affine_stencil(q,weight). The previous cancellation diagnostic
still reproduces its saved artifact exactly. No production Lean construction,
parameter definition, theorem, or assumption has been changed.

Local signed construction, all levels
------------------------------------
For a cubic on bits (s0,s1,s2), sample an anchor r from the eight possible
triples, draw a in (Z/q)^3 uniformly, and set
c=weight*r0*r1*r2-a.r. This affine polynomial is correct at r and has
uniform value at every other binary triple. Hence the signed local law
8*Pplus-7*U represents the exact conditional cubic message channel. It
uses constant branch weight 15 rather than a full-key exponential anchor
sum, but is not a positive sampler by itself.

On coefficients (c,a0,a1,a2), its exact denominator-q^4 mass numerator is
q*#{r:c+a.r=weight*r0*r1*r2}-7. The shared helper verifies every conditional
message marginal. The new direct oracle separately constructs these counts
from the anchor sampler and the subtracted uniform law.

The simulator shifts the actual recursive ciphertext coordinates
(T row-major, c-vector, b-body). The local affine kernel changes c-vector
and body; every one of the nine matrix coordinates is retained. Public
zero columns are privately generated by the exact existing law

  c_j = sum_i s_i*T_ij - inner_j + error_j,
  b = sum_i s_i*inner_i + body_error.

Their phase b+s.c-s.T.s is exactly body_error+s.error. The SAME key is used
for every retained public column and the control. No latent key change,
public-table regeneration, missing body coordinate or conditional table
resampling is used. Hidden keys/errors are used only to generate/check the
real diagnostic instances, never by the positive sampler or Gaussian attack.

For a fixed retained table B, the candidate is H_B=K*(R_B convolved L times),
where R_B is a uniform binary subset sum of the entire public table. All
signed masses are computed on the complete 13-coordinate ciphertext group.
Translating by the source control ciphertext does not alter the negative
mass, so computing the shift kernel at zero is a full pointwise-kernel
positivity test, not a test only of a favorable source tape.

Completed finite grid
---------------------
All eight three-bit keys, error radii 0 and 1, and seeds 0 through 7:

* q=2, public sample counts 4,8,12,16, one round;
* q=4, public sample counts 1,2,4, one and two rounds;
* q=8, public sample counts 1,2, one and two rounds;
* every nonzero base-two gadget level at each modulus.

There are 1,152 retained tables and 3,584 complete-law cases. Exactly 203
are positive, all at q=2. The remaining 3,381 have negative mass, including
ALL 3,072 q=4/q=8 cases. Every negative case records a complete ciphertext
atom, its exact signed numerator and denominator, and its phase. These
are finite-parameter witnesses; they are not by themselves an asymptotic
obstruction for a larger-table nonbinary family.

At every table, the full signed law's decryption marginal is checked to
be exactly the target cubic plus the actual public subset-error law.
This illustrates why checking that phase marginal alone would incorrectly
accept kernels which still have negative full-ciphertext atoms.

A stronger algebraic certificate
--------------------------------
Reduce the retained ciphertext table modulo two. Let V be its binary
span, and L0 the four-coordinate subspace of affine coefficient shifts
(three c entries and the body). For each anchor r, test whether the
character (r0,r1,r2,1) on L0 extends to a full ciphertext character
annihilating V. Let A_B be the set of such anchors. Each extension is
found by public Gaussian elimination on the nine matrix coordinates;
no secret is supplied to this computation.

Modulo two, the signed kernel has Fourier coefficient
(-1)^(weight*r0*r1*r2) at that anchor character, and coefficient zero at
every nontrivial character with body frequency zero. Binary subset-sum
rerandomization preserves every character annihilating V, for ANY L>=1.
If two distinct anchors r,t survive, their characters have unit signed
expectations, while their product has zero expectation. The event where
BOTH characters have the opposite target signs therefore has exact mass

  (1 - 1 - 1 + 0)/4 = -1/4.

This proves negative mass at least 1/4 whenever |A_B|>=2, at every
power-of-two modulus (by projection modulo two), at every gadget weight,
and for every positive number of rerandomization rounds. Adding more
rounds to such a fixed table cannot fix the candidate. Projection is used
here only to certify negativity; positivity is never inferred from it.

Every one of the 3,381 negative grid cases has this certificate. Eleven
representative full-law event sums additionally check the exact -1/4
value directly in the unprojected ciphertext law. The certificate's
scope is explicit: it applies to tables with |A_B|>=2. It does not exclude
larger tables satisfying |A_B|<=1 or alternative source-dependent kernels.
The old conservative gate of a singleton negative atom is retained too.

Constructive positive sampler and why it does not qualify
--------------------------------------------------------
Over q=2, R_B is exactly uniform on V. Public elimination computes V and
reduces the fixed 16 affine coefficient atoms to quotient cosets. If all
coset masses are nonnegative, choose among 16 integer tickets using four
random bits, then add a uniform span element using rank(B) basis bits.
The implemented binary_coset_sample uses no key, no ciphertext-output
lookup table, and no enumeration of public selector strings. Its coin
bound is 4+rank(B). The diagnostic independently enumerates its entire
output law and compares every atom to the dense convolution for all 203
positive cases. A saved-artifact verifier also executes this public sampler.

For q=2, positivity is equivalent to |A_B|<=1. If |A_B|=0 the quotient
kernel is uniform; if |A_B|=1 only one sign mode remains, also positive.
The grid checks this equivalence on all 512 binary cases.

All 203 positive cases fail a security/correctness gate:

* In 87 cases every public column's actual phase is zero modulo two.
  The true key is then in A_B, and positivity forces A_B to be precisely
  that key. The eight public Gaussian extension tests recover all three
  secret bits. All 87 cases pass the diagnostic zero-noise decoding margin,
  but expose the actual key. The older rank-12 normal-vector attack catches
  83 of them; the new anchor test also catches four lower-rank tables.
* In the other 116 cases at least one public zero column has phase one.
  Its independent selector bit makes the rerandomization error parity
  EXACTLY uniform. The control bit is erased with error probability 1/2,
  regardless of the independent original control error. This is an actual
  decoding obstruction, not merely a failed worst-case noise estimate.

These binary conclusions cover arbitrary table size and every L>=1 for
this exact local affine smoothing construction. The finite diagnostic
checks the corresponding public attack or uniform parity error in every
positive instance. The modulus-two baseline consequently supplies an
executable sampler but no acceptable cryptographic construction.

Noise gate beyond the tiny instances
------------------------------------
A public zero column has phase magnitude <=(n+1)*E. L rounds using p
columns add at most L*p*(n+1)*E, so the fresh control's supported bound
is (1+L*p)*(n+1)*E. Each grid case computes its exact unwrapped subset
noise distribution and verifies this bound.

The artifact also substitutes this enlarged control bound into the
existing recursive bootstrap budget at parameters 16,32,64,128 and L=1,2.
The strict scaled post-NAND inequality fails at 16 and holds at 32,64,128.
All these are exact integer computations, not a new eventual Lean theorem.
The extra multiplier is polynomial for the intended p and L, so noise
alone is not an asymptotic rejection of public rerandomization. These
budget evaluations do not certify the unconstructed nonbinary sampler
or replace the remaining correctness/efficiency proofs.

Decision at the agreed gate
--------------------------
No tested candidate has an efficient positive sampler, retained secrecy,
and correct controls together. The plan therefore stops before modifying
the production construction or adding a new conditional Lean simulator
interface. Larger-table nonbinary kernels and approximate positive
simulation are not ruled out by the grid or the modulo-two certificate,
but neither has a compatible constructive sampler in this implementation.
The full requested ordinary-LWE security theorem remains unfinished.

    --output docs/GSWPublicRerandomizationExploration.json
  python3 scripts/check-gsw-public-rerandomization.py \
    --verify-existing docs/GSWPublicRerandomizationExploration.json

replays all 3,381 singleton negative atoms using separately enumerated

are checked. All numerators, denominators, support counts and noise
checks are exact integers/rationals. No reference or Lean input changed.

Final verification: the final-code quick run exits zero and reproduces all
144 seed-zero tables (448 complete-law cases) from the full artifact exactly.
The original cancellation diagnostic reproduces its earlier JSON artifact
without changes. Independent artifact checks verify the complete accepted
grid, every negative case's parity-certificate lower bound, the 87/116
positive-case split, and all direct-oracle counts. Python AST parsing and

2026-10-18 -- LARGE-TABLE PHASE OBSTRUCTION AND APPROXIMATE SMOOTHING

This continuation separates two questions left open by the implemented
public-rerandomization gate: exact positivity beyond the parity obstruction,
and statistical approximation to a positive law. Neither result below is
a completed ordinary-LWE reduction. No production Lean input changes.

Implementation and reproducible output:
  scripts/check-gsw-rerandomization-phase-obstruction.py
  docs/GSWRerandomizationPhaseObstructionExploration.json

Exact two-phase identity
------------------------
Let K be the local affine signed cubic kernel from the preceding entry.
For distinct binary triples r,t, let F(u)=weight*u0*u1*u2. Every three
distinct Boolean triples are affinely independent modulo two, so the
corresponding affine-evaluation map has a unit minor over Z/(2^k).
Consequently the joint phase projection of K at r and t is exactly

  delta_F(r) x U + U x delta_F(t) - U x U.

Indeed the anchor r contributes delta_F(r) x U; anchor t contributes
U x delta_F(t); each of the other six anchors contributes U x U; the
subtracted part contributes -7*(U x U). No independence of the public
walk's two phases is assumed. If rho_r,rho_t are its marginal phase laws,
the smoothed kernel has projected mass

  H_r,t(x,y) = rho_r(x-F(r))/q + rho_t(y-F(t))/q - 1/q^2.

Suppose the actual key is r and rho_r has a missing residue. At a missing
residue x, positivity for every y would require rho_t(y)>=1/q for every
y, hence EXACT uniformity of rho_t. Thus a real phase gap and ANY
nonuniform wrong-key phase distribution certify a negative full-law
event. Projection only certifies negativity, never full-law positivity.

For a fixed wrong key t, the frequency-one Fourier coefficient of L
binary-subset walks is

  product_j ((1+exp(2*pi*i*phase_t(B_j)/q))/2)^L.

It is nonzero if no public column has wrong-key phase q/2. Therefore
the wrong-key subset law is nonuniform for every finite L in that case.
For independently generated genuine zero columns at a fixed true key,
each wrong-key phase is uniform: a differing binary secret coordinate
gives a unit coefficient on an independent uniform inner coordinate.
This also holds conditional on the actual column errors. The probability
that any of p columns has phase q/2 is at most p/q. Meanwhile, the true
subset-error support has at most 1+L*sum_j |epsilon_j| residues. If that
quantity is below q, the required true phase gap exists.

Hence the fixed translation-kernel candidate fails exact positivity with
probability at least 1-p/q whenever this noise gap remains. This includes
polynomial p,L and supported errors against the existing exponential
modulus eventually; the asymptotic statement is a mathematical argument,
not a new Lean theorem. It is not an impossibility theorem for approximate
positive simulation, arbitrary source-dependent simulation, or the full
user objective. Searching the residues by dynamic programming takes time
depending on q, so these certificates are not asserted to give a PPT
security attack at the production modulus.

Completed larger-table diagnostic
---------------------------------
The new grid uses q=512,2048,8192,32768, keys 000,101,111, seeds 0,1,
error radius one, and p=13*log2(q)+17 public columns. Every column retains
all nine matrix entries, three vector entries and its body; all use the
same unchanged key. Every full retained table and its actual unwrapped
column errors are saved. Both one and two rounds and every gadget level
are checked. The saved error information is for verification only.

All 24 tables pass the necessary positivity condition from the old
modulo-two certificate: that certificate no longer rejects these tables.
Nevertheless all 48 round cases have exact negative two-phase events.
Twenty-four also pass the strict supported control decoding margin.
Forty-six have no wrong-key half-turn column. The other two are negative
by direct exact subset counts too; the no-half-turn argument is sufficient,
not necessary. Every saved negative numerator and denominator is exact.

The script checks 6,384 unsmoothed pair atoms for all distinct binary
triple pairs, all levels, q=2,4,8. A separate direct selector/anchor
convolution checks 80 smoothed pair atoms at q=4,8. The saved-artifact
verifier replays the actual public-column generator and checks the full
table, errors, two projected phases and all event shifts. It uses a
separate pure-Python modular recurrence rather than the NumPy recurrence
which generated the records.

Approximate positivity is a different question
---------------------------------------------
An arbitrarily small negative mass defeats exact sampling but need not
defeat a negligible-error simulator. In fact noise-aware leftover hashing
provides a statistical approximation to a positive, secret-defined law.

Fix the secret s and all actual column phases epsilon_j. A genuine zero
column is uniform on its phase hyperplane: its (T,c) coordinates are
uniform and independent of these errors; its body is then determined.
Let G=ker(phase_s), so |G|=q^(D-1), with D=n^2+n+1 complete ciphertext
coordinates. Restrict uniform binary selectors to each fixed subset-error
fiber. The random uniform hyperplane coordinates define a two-universal
hash on every fiber: two distinct binary selectors differ by a unit in
some coordinate. If K_error is the number of nonempty error fibers and
N=2^p, the expected chi-squared divergence from an independent uniform
G-coordinate, retaining both the public table and the subset error, is

  (|G|-1)*K_error/N.

For a fiber of size m, the expected sum of squared hash counts is
m+(m^2-m)/|G|. Summing its Pearson term over fibers proves the displayed
identity; Cauchy--Schwarz bounds squared total variation by one quarter
of chi-squared divergence. Let P_s^0 be uniform on G and R_B be one
public subset walk. Averaging over the retained table therefore gives

  E_B TV(R_B, P_s^0 * R_B)
      <= (1/2)*sqrt(q^(D-1)*K_error/2^p).

The same bound holds for any additional finite rounds using the SAME
table by conditional convolution contraction. It is not legitimate to
treat repeated copies of a table as fresh independent hash columns.
The phase/error side information is retained in this argument; it is not
replaced by an independent error distribution.

The local signed kernel has l1 norm at most 15 and its s-phase is exactly
F(s). Thus K*P_s^0 is the positive uniform law on phase F(s), and

  E_B (1/2)*||K*R_B - (K*P_s^0)*R_B||_1 <= 15*delta,

where delta is the preceding leftover-hash bound. Taking
p=D*log2(q)+2*lambda+1 and using K_error<=q gives
delta^2<=2^(-2*lambda-3). This is a single-local-kernel statistical
approximation, with the full public table retained. It does not construct
a public sampler for the positive comparison law, which depends on the
unknown s. The public signed expression can be enumerated at tiny
parameters, but no polynomial-time way to sample its clipped/normalized
approximation is supplied. No hardness of doing so is asserted either.

Five exhaustive finite configurations check the noise-aware calculation
over Z/2, (Z/2)^2, Z/4 and Z/8, retaining public table and subset-error
side information. There are 8,768 complete tables and 339,968 selector
choices. The exact averaged chi-squared identity, the pointwise TV bound,
and both averaged TV inequalities hold in every configuration. Family
envelope records substitute lambda=16,32,64,128 and L=1,2 into the noise
gap and the conservative delta bound. These are mathematical/finite
checks, not new Lean theorems or an instantiated efficient simulator.

The per-local bound must not be silently promoted to a complete control
table theorem. For M local signed kernels, a naive composition may incur
15^M. More public columns could offset this in the statistical estimate,
but neither such a joint theorem nor a polynomial-time sampling/evaluation
algorithm has been constructed here. An expectation bound for a retained
table also must not be asserted pointwise for every fixed table.

Decision and reproduction
-------------------------
The broader exact-positive fixed-kernel route now fails beyond the old
parity-obstructed tables as well. Approximate smoothing survives the
statistical gate, but has not passed the constructive efficient-sampler
gate. The implementation therefore does not promote an uninstantiated
simulator interface or change the correctly bootstrapping construction.
The requested full ordinary-LWE-only security theorem is still unfinished;
the goal remains active after this continuation.

  python3 scripts/check-gsw-rerandomization-phase-obstruction.py \
    --output docs/GSWRerandomizationPhaseObstructionExploration.json
  python3 scripts/check-gsw-rerandomization-phase-obstruction.py \
    --verify-existing docs/GSWRerandomizationPhaseObstructionExploration.json

Validation: artifact generation and the independent replay both exit zero.
All 24 full retained tables and all 48 negative events are replayed with
separate modular counting, including every recorded gadget-level shift.
All five exhaustive noise-aware leftover-hash configurations pass their
exact rational identities and bounds. Python AST parsing and git diff
--check pass. All 481 Lean/config/dependency inputs still byte-match the
validated mirror, so the preceding 9,257-job build and 1,017-declaration
standard-axiom audit continue to cover exactly the same Lean code; no
additional Lean theorem or new build is claimed. No reference PDF changed.


2026-10-18 -- PUBLIC REJECTION CHECK AND RESUMED COMPLETION AUDIT

The preceding goal turn was progress: it changed the authoritative script
and saved artifact, supplied negative-event certificates beyond the parity
obstruction, and established a separate finite noise-aware smoothing check.
It was not a wait or completion. This turn checks whether that smoothing
can close the remaining efficiency gap through direct signed tests or a
public rejection sampler. The original objective is unchanged.

Direct signed-branch evaluation
-------------------------------
One local expansion has eight positive and seven negative tickets. For M
independent local expansions, the direct branch estimator's absolute weight
is 15^M. Its product sign has expectation 15^(-M). Even for an independent
fair observer bit B, the unbiased signed score Z=15^M*sign*B has

  E[Z]=1/2,    Var(Z)=225^M/2-1/4.

Public rerandomization does not change these internal branch weights.
The new exact checks cover M=1,2,4,8,16,32. This rejects this direct
estimator as an efficient generic evaluation method with fixed mean-square
accuracy. It is not a query lower bound for all possible estimators:
an evaluator exploiting structure or cancellation could behave differently,
and the independent fair observer itself has a trivial known expectation.
The missing step is precisely such an efficiently evaluable bounded
complete-game score. Statistical closeness to a positive law does not by
itself provide one.

Why ordinary public rejection of affine proposals reveals the key
-----------------------------------------------------------------
Consider the natural rejection implementation of the positive envelope
Pplus*R_B. On a chosen triple I of secret coordinates, each proposal
draws an anchor r and uniform affine coefficients a independently of
its public walk. Its shift has true phase

  F(r) + a.(s_I-r) + phase_s(walk).

If r=s_I, the intended message phase is obtained with the walk's actual
error. If r differs in any coordinate, one coefficient is a unit and the
true phase is EXACTLY uniform modulo q, even conditional on B, the
anchor, the walk and all earlier proposals. This fact holds for adaptive
choices of later anchors provided their fresh affine coefficients remain
uniform and independent of the corresponding walk.

Let A_s be the allowed phase set for a correct output, with
kappa=|A_s|/q. For any rejection filter which returns one of at most T
such proposals, a union bound gives

  Pr[returned anchor is wrong AND output is correct] <= T*kappa.

If its output/failure correctness error is at most epsilon, instrumenting
the public sampler to retain the accepted proposal's anchor therefore
recovers s_I with probability at least 1-epsilon-T*kappa. No acceptance
oracle, hidden-error test, or actual secret is used by that instrumented
algorithm: it merely returns the anchor already sampled internally.

For a narrow control interval, kappa<=min(1,(2*controlBound+1)/q), which
is negligible in the supported production regime even after polynomial T.
The saved family envelope now records this exact ratio. Repeating the
same construction on triples covering the key recovers the full unchanged
secret with a polynomial union-bound loss. An independent held-out zero
column then tests that key: its true phase is in the supported interval,
whereas its body makes the phase under any independently chosen candidate
key uniform in the uniform-table branch. The existing quadratic/base-table
ordinary-LWE reduction is the relevant security argument for that branch.
The key-recovery consequence is a mathematical deduction for this sampler
class, not a new full Lean reduction theorem.

This excludes a successful public PPT sampler built solely by rejecting
and returning these affine-plus-walk proposals under the base-table
security goal. It does NOT exclude output transformations, different
proposals, coupled non-affine compilation, source-dependent simulation,
or efficient bounded tests which avoid this rejection construction.
In particular, correctness of arbitrary public encryptions does not
imply key recovery; the retained random affine anchor is essential here.

The executable now retains that latent anchor in its finite phase checks.
All 107,008 labeled proposals at q=2,4,8, every gadget level, every true
three-bit key and every anchor pass 2,176 exact phase-atom equalities.
All 336 wrong-anchor cases are exactly uniform. The fixed walk shift in
the check can be arbitrary; independence permits averaging over any
actual public-walk law. These are correctness/anchor checks, not a claim
that a phase projection certifies full-ciphertext positivity.

Current-state completion audit
-------------------------------
* Same unchanged secret and repeatable refresh correctness: proved for
  the actual recursive-feature family. The current
  RecursiveFeatureFHECorrectness.eventually_family_correctExp_probability_one
  states that a sufficiently large parameter works for every finite shared
  NAND program and output. familyCorrectExp_eq_scheme identifies its actual
  keygen/encrypt/evaluate/decrypt experiment. This proves correctness only.
* Full ordinary-LWE security: incomplete. The current
  RecursiveFeatureLowLevelHybrid.family_oneTime_abs_signedAdvantage_le
  still contains deletedReplacementAdvantage. Its definition compares the
  surviving higher-weight complete control view with the quadratic view.
  There is no proved negligible bound for that comparison in this worktree.

  GSWCorrelatedMasking.ordinary_source_simulator_game_abs_advantage_le
  requires an actual positive ProbComp simulator and a uniform-source
  complete-table marginal. Matching the real view and proving efficiency

  GSWTranscriptCompiler.noiseBound_bootstrap still takes herror, bounding

  by these partial results. Introducing either would change the objective

2026-10-19 -- IMPLEMENTED NONLINEAR COMPILER SYNTHESIS PLAN

The user selected constructive synthesis and authorized implementation of
the proposed bounded feasibility plan. The full original objective has
resumed and is ACTIVE. No narrower security endpoint, extra assumption or
changed encryption secret is substituted for it.

Implementation:
  scripts/search-gsw-nonlinear-compiler.py
  docs/GSWNonlinearCompilerExploration.json

Source, output and the uniform branch
-------------------------------------
The compiler receives only public coordinates, target indices and gadget
weight. Its public input consists of a guarded ordinary LWE block (A,y),
A^{-T}, the full-residue vector u=A^{-T}y, and thirteen recursive ciphertexts:
zero and the six linear/quadratic monomials on the target triple at weights
one and w. All source rows/ciphertexts use the SAME original binary secret.
The input includes neither that secret nor the source errors.

The quadratic inputs are generated by the actual public ordinary-source
transformation T=A'-M, c=y'-a'+linear, b=z+constant. Their raw masks,
outputs and errors are retained separately for independent source-law
verification. Their real phase is the intended degree-at-most-two message
plus f+s.e. This does not assume a new polynomial-KDM hardness statement;
it matches the existing fixed quadratic-source construction. Its full
joint ordinary-LWE parameter theorem would be reused only after a viable
compiler has been obtained.

The required cubic output is evaluated with the actual original-secret
phase b+s.c-s.T.s. The vector u=s+A^{-T}e is explicitly checked as that
NOISY full-residue estimate, never used as a replacement decryption key.
The verifier's oracle and the source compiler are separate data objects.
Secret-dependent reference encryption is used only to validate the checker
and is never included among the public candidates.

The experimental compile_view retains every full public-table coordinate
and changes only the control context. The search circuit itself does not
read the retained encryption table; this is an explicit restriction of the
executed grammar. The current correlated-masking theorem permits arbitrary
context correlations with a uniform table, so uniformity of the compiled
controls is not required. Retaining the table is a pointwise data-flow fact,
not an assertion that its REAL narrow-phase distribution is uniform.

Guard and nonlinear family
--------------------------
The public guard requires A mod 2 invertible. A^{-T} then exists modulo
every tested power-of-two modulus. For uniform square A the guard
probability is product_{i=1}^n(1-2^{-i}), at least 1/4. For n>=2 the
first two factors give 3/8, and the remaining product is at least
1-sum_{i>=3}2^{-i}=3/4; n=1 is immediate. Thus fixed R retries would
exhaust with probability at most (3/4)^R. The code checks exact guard
probabilities at eight dimensions. This counting argument is not a new
Lean theorem, and the diagnostic conditional-source generator is not
being claimed as the completed bounded-retry production implementation.

The typed arithmetic DAG has scalar/vector/matrix registers. Its thirty
operators cover modular addition/subtraction/multiplication, dot and outer
products, matrix/vector and matrix/matrix multiplication, transpose, trace,
public-index selection, parity, highest-bit extraction and centered rounding.
Rounding grids are q/2,q/4,q/16; constants are 0,1,-1,w,q/2 plus zero,
identity and public basis vectors. The output is a full (T,c,b) correction
added to the zero source ciphertext. All operations are positive,
deterministic public processing; there is no signed measure or anchor
rejection sampler in this experiment.

The same formula is used at every key, dimension, modulus and target triple.
The searched node caps are 12 and 24. The solver constrains types and
backward-only register references, and optimizes finite-example success.
Seed formulas include the zero/quadratic baselines and genuine nonlinear
body-estimate/matrix-gated parity and highest-bit formulas. The seeds are
explicit controls of the experiment, not solver discoveries.

Counterexample-guided MaxSMT uses at most thirty iterations per cap, sixty
seconds per solve, thirty minutes per node cap and two hours of synthesis
wall time. It starts with 32 examples and adds up to sixteen failing pool
examples per usable model. An unchanged timed-out query with no usable
model is not rerun. Unknown/incomplete models are rejected unless every
register coordinate agrees with the independent integer interpreter.
Timeout is recorded as inconclusive, never as UNSAT or an impossibility
result. Up to eight distinct concrete formulas are ranked for validation.

The system Python lacks pip and ensurepip. The pinned Z3 4.13.3.0 wheel
was downloaded from PyPI into /tmp/fhe-nonlinear-synthesis-deps and its
published SHA256 checked before extraction:
  z3_solver-4.13.3.0-py3-none-manylinux_2_17_x86_64.manylinux2014_x86_64.whl
  794843e4946ade1561e40a75ffc1163b45d36b493fd6cc269ad1d6a65bddb8e5
No system package or repository dependency was changed.

Screen, validation and the production gate
------------------------------------------
The 512 seeded training cases cover n=3,4, q=2^12,2^16, E=0,1,3,
all binary training keys, and quarter/half-modulus target weights. Masks
are drawn independently conditional on the PUBLIC guard; errors are
drawn from the uniform integer interval for this diagnostic. The screen
counts errors against min(q/32,8*(n+1)*(E+1)^2), separately by dimension,
modulus, radius and gadget level. The 99-percent rule is only a candidate
selection heuristic. It supplies no statistical or computational theorem.

Independent held-out source draws use n=3,4,6,8, q through 2^24, all gadget
levels and ordered triples, with 4,096 draws in each dimension/modulus/error
stratum. Each draw retains all source/output ciphertext coordinates and is
tested under its ORIGINAL key. Paired source-error changes cover every raw
and quadratic scalar error at both signs on four fixed fixtures. Complete
view checks materialize every ordered genuine cubic-control slot at every
level under four fixed same-secret worlds, with the encryption table intact.

Before trusting any synthesis model, a fixed all-operator circuit is solved
at four dimension/modulus combinations and its 1,868 register coordinates
checked against separate integer semantics. A further exact finite check
compares centered rounding with bit-vector semantics, including ties.
Every training source has independent raw-law and true-phase checks; an
oracle-only correctly encrypted reference validates all 512 target checks.
These controls prevent an encoding error, omitted body, changed secret or
zero/even-error specialization from being counted as construction progress.

Production integration remains conditional on an explicit asymptotic
derivation: polynomial noise H(n,log q,E), negligible compiler failure,
polynomial total source/runtime bounds, and the actual reusable refresh
budget. A small-parameter pass would not suffice. If these gates passed,
real key generation could check/freshly replace failed compiled controls
using its actual secret; the public reduction would charge the proved
negligible mismatch without performing that hidden check. No such fallback
security bound or new production sampler is assumed in this implementation.

An asymptotic obstruction for the six seed formulas
--------------------------------------------------
The inverse-based seed formulas are explicitly analyzed, rather than only
rejected at toy parameters. This argument concerns those six formulas,
not the full synthesis grammar.

Write d=A^{-T}e, so parity(u)=s XOR parity(d). Each row of A^{-T} is
nonzero modulo two. For independent uniform integer errors in [-E,E],
the parity of a row supported on t>=1 coordinates is odd with probability

  beta_t = (1-(1-2*p)^t)/2,

where p is the scalar odd-error probability. If E is even,
beta_t>=E/(2E+1). If E is odd,
beta_t>=(1-(2E+1)^(-2))/2. Both bounds are at least 1/3 for E>=1.
These statements hold for EVERY accepted matrix, so the public matrix
guard does not remove this parity error.

The matrix-gated parity seed has message w*s_i*s_j*parity(u_k);
it differs from w*s_i*s_j*s_k with probability at least beta_t/4.
The body-parity seed substitutes parity(u) into the three-bit product.
For any nonzero three-bit delta, a uniform three-bit key's product differs
from the delta-shifted product on exactly two of eight keys. Thus this
seed also has mismatch probability at least beta_t/4.

Highest-bit extraction has a separate obstruction. The map
s -> highestBit(s+d) agrees with the binary identity at both s=0,1
only when d=q/2-1. Since some coefficient of the inverse row is odd,
conditioning on the other errors makes that d value attainable by at
most one error in [-E,E], provided 2E+1<=q. Its probability is therefore
at most 1/(2E+1). The matrix-highest-bit seed then mismatches on at least
(1-1/(2E+1))/8 of keys/errors. For the body-highest-bit seed, unless all
three maps are identities, its three-bit truth table differs from the
target product on at least one of eight inputs. The same lower bound
applies. The zero/quadratic baselines each have an explicit 1/8 message
mismatch event on a uniform three-bit target.

For a top or quarter gadget weight, these mismatches remain actual noise
failures whenever H+(n+1)*E<q/4. Consequently all six seeds have failure
probability at least 1/12 under this margin and E>=1, 2E+1<=q.
Transferring from uniform interval errors to the existing scalar sampler
subtracts at most n times its scalar TV approximation loss. That loss is
negligible in the current family, so increasing parameters cannot make
these seed failures negligible. The zero-noise success of the parity
seeds does not survive the required noisy ordinary-LWE source.

The executable --verify-seed-obstructions checks 224 exact parity-weight
identities, 64 XOR/product truth assignments, 37,376 highest-bit product
truth assignments, and 1,624 odd-coefficient/error trials. The general
probability deductions above are mathematical derivations, not new Lean
theorems. A constant seed failure blocks the planned negligible-fallback
coupling argument. It is not an attack on a repaired real key generator,
or a lower bound on arbitrary nonlinear compilation.

---------------------------------
The actual 12-node and 24-node MaxSMT queries each timed out at the
60-second solver limit. Both returned UNKNOWN, with no model passing
independent type/register semantics. The known seed was supplied only as
an initial-value hint, not as a restriction of the grammar. No new formula
was accepted from the solver. Identical timed-out queries were not repeated.
The result is INCONCLUSIVE for the bounded synthesis grammar, not UNSAT,
not exhaustion of that family, and not a proof that nonlinear compilation
is impossible.

All six explicit seed formulas fail the finite screen. Training and held-out

  formula                    training        held-out
  matrix-gated parity         463/512        187476/196608
  matrix-gated highest bit    450/512        182318/196608
  quadratic-message baseline  449/512        182184/196608
  zero-shift baseline         447/512        182336/196608
  body-estimate parity        430/512        180548/196608
  body-estimate highest bit   406/512        175703/196608

There are 196,608 seeded held-out cases and 1,179,648 formula/case
evaluations. Paired checks cover 496 scalar-error assignments per formula
(2,976 total), and complete cubic-control contexts cover 912 controls per
formula (5,472 total). The latter use all-ones and alternating original
keys at n=3,4; the shared key is unchanged within every world. Every formula
has failures in these complete cubic-control contexts. All 24 formula/world

An explicit coverage audit caught a held-out scheduling bug: the original

was intentionally interrupted; its partial result is not validation evidence.

No candidate reaches the mathematical noise/failure/reusability gate.
Consequently no production sampler, Lean source, proof interface or security
assumption is changed. The useful concrete results are the reproducible

validated full-coordinate counterexamples for the seeds, and the

security theorem remains unfinished and the resumed goal remains ACTIVE.

Reproduction using the isolated pinned solver:

2026-10-19 -- THREE-BLOCK CUBIC COMPILER WITH THIRD-ORDER ERROR
============================================================

Scope and concrete implementation
---------------------------------
The nonlinear synthesis queries in the preceding entry were inconclusive.
This continuation implements a concrete positive nonlinear candidate rather
than repeating those queries: a three-block inclusion-exclusion compiler.
The public input consists of three independent ordinary same-secret LWE
blocks A_r,y_r, with y_r=A_r^T*S+e_r. It uses the ORIGINAL binary S, not a
transformed secret. Neither S nor any source error is a compiler input.
All matrix, vector and body coordinates of the resulting recursive
quadratic ciphertext are materialized and checked.

Files:
  scripts/search-gsw-cubic-tensor-compiler.py
  docs/GSWCubicTensorCompilerExploration.json

This is an executable candidate and exact finite certification study. It
is not a new Lean security theorem, production key generator, complete
published-view instantiation, or proof of an asymptotic source assumption.
The existing public encryption table is not replaced by a table under a
new secret. No production Lean code or security assumption was changed.

Algebra and actual public ciphertext
-----------------------------------
Write x_r=A_r^T*S. For a public coefficient tensor D, trilinearity gives

 P_D(S) = D[y1,y2,y3]
          -D[x1,y2,y3]-D[y1,x2,y3]-D[y1,y2,x3]
          +D[x1,x2,y3]+D[x1,y2,x3]+D[y1,x2,x3]
        = D[x1,x2,x3]+D[e1,e2,e3].

P_D has degree at most two in S. Thus it can be represented by the actual
recursive ciphertext phase b+S.c-S^T*T*S. Its public b is D[y1,y2,y3].
Its c contains the three negative one-x terms. Its T contains the
negatives of the three two-x terms, matching the phase's minus sign.

Boolean-reduce the cubic D[(A1^T*S),(A2^T*S),(A3^T*S)]. Public modular
linear constraints on D set its coefficient at the distinct target
triple I to w, and every other distinct cubic coefficient to zero.
Repeated-index terms form a public degree-one/two polynomial L_A(S).
Subtract L_A in the actual c and T coordinates. The exact resulting law is

 phase_S(compile(A,y,D)) = w*product_{i in I} S_i + D[e1,e2,e3]  (mod q).

There are no first- or second-order source-error terms, and no remaining
S factor in the integer residual. For |e_rj|<=E, the supported bound is
E^3*||D||_1. This is an improvement in error ORDER, not automatically an
improvement in error size: tensor coefficients can be large.

Conditional on A, independent centered errors of common variance sigma^2
also give mean zero and second moment sigma^6*||D||_F^2: all off-diagonal
terms vanish by independence and zero means. The script checks this
exactly for its exhaustive ternary-error fixtures. This observation does
not supply the missing joint negligible-tail or tensor-shortness theorem.

Public coefficient builder, including even pivots
------------------------------------------------
The builder sees only masks A, target indices I, public weight w and q.
It constructs the Boolean cubic coefficient matrix, then finds an integer
preimage of the target modulo q=2^bits. It first tries unit-pivot
elimination. When that fails, it diagonalizes over Z/(2^bits), retaining
even pivots and the row/column transformations. Divisibility of each
transformed target determines membership in the image.

For a soluble target, the builder constructs the complete modular kernel
lattice, runs public LLL, and applies MPFR GSO Babai. The nearby vector is
reconstructed as an EXACT integer combination of the reduced kernel
basis. Every basis vector and final target equation is checked modulo q.
The nonunit branch includes qI among its generators so reducing the
column transformation modulo q does not lose part of the integer lattice.
No unchecked floating-point closest-vector output is used as a certificate.

For an insoluble target, it saves a row-combination witness u and a divisor
d of q: all coordinates of u*A_cubic are divisible by d, but u*target is
not. Saved-artifact replay verifies this directly, without lattice code.
Such a witness applies only to the sampled masks and that target weight.
Failure at weight one does not reject even gadget weights. In particular,
the initial unit-only search's eight rank failures are not treated as
infeasibility: all eight were revisited with the nonunit solver.

The standalone solver audit compares both solver branches with exhaustive
image enumeration: all 2-by-2 matrices at q=2,4 and 64 seeded 3-by-2
matrices at q=8. All 336 matrices and 73,856 target/solver trials pass;
62,452 trials produce independently checked nonmembership certificates.
These are finite solver checks, not a general formal proof of its runtime.

Finite experiment and exact outcomes
-----------------------------------
The grid is (n,m)=(3,2),(4,3),(6,4),(8,5),(8,6), with three n-by-m ordinary
blocks, at bit lengths 12,24,64,128. Each case tries the first and last
three distinct secret indices, deduplicated at n=3, at weights 1,q/4,q/2.
This samples two target triples, NOT every triple of the larger dimensions.
There is no mask resampling after an inconvenient rank or noise outcome.
Each case has a 90-second wall-clock cap.

The final version-2 artifact records:
  20 parameter/mask cases;
  13 cases with all requested finite targets solved;
  5 cases with some targets solved and some certified infeasible;
  2 timed-out cases, at (n,m,bits)=(8,6,64),(8,6,128);
  90 full tensor/control certificates;
  6 target nonmembership certificates;
  4,336 sampled source/key/error phase identities;
  538 sampled decoding-margin failures (|centered residual| >= q/16).

The two timeouts supply no control certificate, infeasibility conclusion,
or shortness evidence. The initial unit-only run's twelve other case
outcomes, including those timeouts, were preserved while extending its
eight rank-failed cases. Identical timed-out LLL queries were not repeated.

All binary keys are used at n=3,4. At n=6,8 each target/weight uses the
basis keys and 24 seeded random binary keys. Each key receives sampled
independent errors of radii one and three. Every sampled phase identity
passes, including trials whose noise exceeds the decoding margin. Error
identities and successful decryption are different checks.

Separately, exhaustive algebra checks cover all eight binary keys and
all 729 ternary error assignments at q=2,8,97, for 17,496 complete
ciphertext checks. Mean and exact second moment are checked on each
fixture: second moments are 128/27,128/27,16/3, agreeing with
(2/3)^3*||D||_F^2. These include the original secret throughout.

Compatible-budget and source-input checks
-----------------------------------------
For a finite bound H=E^3*||D||_1, the diagnostic substitutes
B=max(H,(n+1)*E) into the existing refresh budget shape. With
Drows=n^2+n+1, growth=Drows*bits and lookup the least power of two at least
8*Drows, its strict inequality is

 lookup^2*Drows*growth*(growth+1)^2*B < q.

The chosen finite public-sample count is growth+2*bits+17; its fresh
supported encryption error is also checked below the output budget.
This is a per-control arithmetic substitution. It is NOT a correctness
proof for a joint full control table, a proof that every required tensor
shares that bound, or an instantiation of a negligible-failure family.

Twenty-three of the 90 control bounds satisfy this radius-one inequality;
17 satisfy the radius-three inequality. The one-control supported
q/16 margins hold in 73 radius-one and 56 radius-three certificates.
The sampled lower-modulus failures remain in the artifact.

A separate public ordinary-input check solves linear preimages for
(q/2)*e_i in the concatenated [A1|A2|A3]. It saves exact pi_i such that
[A1|A2|A3]*pi_i=(q/2)*e_i mod q. If 4*E*||pi_i||_1<q for every i,
nearest-half-modulus decoding of y.pi_i recovers all original key bits
for every supported input error. This check uses no secret oracle.

All eighteen completed cases have such radius-one key-recovery
certificates; seventeen also meet the radius-three bound. Every case
with a passing refresh budget has recovery certificates at its matching
error radius. The two timed-out cases have no saved source-filter result.
This is a certificate concerning the ordinary PUBLIC INPUT instance. It
is a direct view attack when that raw source prefix is published; it is
not an asserted attack on a protocol that hides that prefix, or on the
production asymptotic family. It establishes that the finite margin
successes are not examples of hard ordinary-LWE input parameters.
Conversely, failure of this particular filter would not prove hardness.

Remaining gate and relation to the full goal
--------------------------------------------
The constructor is positive and public, and cancels lower-order errors.
Those concrete properties avoid the signed-simulator total-variation
weight problem. Given a correct suitable family, deterministic compilation
could be combined with the existing full-view ordinary-source/correlated
masking argument. That joint reduction has NOT been instantiated here.
An auxiliary ordinary-source prefix and the separate published encryption
table must both be accounted for; no marginal or dropped-coordinate
security statement is substituted for that requirement.

The missing prerequisite is a proved, efficiently achieved tensor bound
for all required weighted cubic controls at the intended binary-secret
ordinary-LWE parameter family, with compatible errors and negligible
joint failure. In the current family n=2*(parameter+1)^5,
q=2^((parameter+1)^2), E=2^(parameter+1). The finite small-n experiments
do not prove an adequate bound there. LLL's exact modular certificates
prove the equations, not the necessary asymptotic noise estimate.
A polynomial norm bound would suffice eventually, but a suitable smaller-
than-q bound can also suffice; polynomial norm is not claimed necessary.
An argument using the Frobenius norm would still require a joint tail
bound and compatible coefficient construction.

No independent SIS, binary-LWE, circular/KDM, trapdoor or leakage assumption
was introduced. No theorem is concluded from the six infeasible target
weights, the toy source-recovery filter, or two timeouts. These outcomes
do not rule out other nonlinear compilers or an asymptotic tensor builder.
The production sampler/Lean integration gate remains unmet. The requested
ordinary-LWE-only same-secret repeatable FHE proof remains unfinished;
the resumed goal remains ACTIVE.

2026-10-19 -- CUBIC ERROR TAIL CHECK AND FULL-GOAL BLOCKED AUDIT
==============================================================

Previous-turn classification and next safe action
-------------------------------------------------
The preceding goal turn made progress: it implemented a public three-block
compiler with exact full-coordinate phase/error identities, nonunit target
certificates and saved parameter outcomes. It did not prove the missing
same-secret ordinary-LWE security hop. No lattice or synthesis job from
that turn is live. Repeating its two identical timed-out cases would not
be a verified wait or a new result.

This turn tests a remaining concrete shortcut: replace the tensor's
supported L1 error estimate with a joint high-probability Frobenius-norm
estimate. It then audits completion against the full objective. The
objective is not reduced to correctness, a conditional security inequality,
finite diagnostic success or an alternative assumption.

A concrete conditional-Hoeffding derivation
-------------------------------------------
Fix public masks and their tensor D, chosen without seeing source errors.
Let all three error blocks have independent, centered coordinates in
[-E,E]. This describes the finite interval-error fixtures. Write

 W_ab = sum_c D_abc*e3_c,
 U_a  = sum_b W_ab*e2_b,
 Z    = sum_a U_a*e1_a.

For each (a,b), the bounded centered scalar MGF estimate gives

 Pr[|W_ab| > E*sqrt(2t*sum_c D_abc^2)] <= 2*exp(-t).

Union over m^2 pairs, then condition on the third error block. A second
bounded-sum estimate, unioned over the m values of a, gives

 |U_a| <= (2t)*E^2*sqrt(sum_bc D_abc^2)

except for the first-stage failures and at most 2*m*exp(-t) more failure.
Condition on the second and third blocks, then apply the same estimate
once to the first block. Consequently

 Pr[|Z| > (2t)^(3/2)*E^3*||D||_F]
     <= 2*(m^2+m+1)*exp(-t).

The bound is for the actual integer residual; it is sufficient for a
centered modular decoding margin when its threshold is small enough.
No converse inference from a large integer residual to modular failure
is made. Conditioning on masks is legitimate because the builder uses
only masks and public target indices/weight, not bodies or errors.

Union over K controls needs no independence between their residuals.
Using exp(-t)<=2^-t, the integer choice

 t = securityBits + ceil(log2(2*K*(m^2+m+1)))

makes the joint failure bound at most 2^-securityBits. The script computes
ceil(sqrt((2t)^3*E^6*||D||_F^2)) with exact integer arithmetic and compares
it with the supported E^3*||D||_1 threshold. K=n*(n^2+n+1)*bits is the
conservative full scalar-control count from RecursiveFeatureSecurityView,
not the number of sampled target triples. This substitutes a bound for
sampled tensors; it does not construct the other required control tensors.

Files and checked outcome
--------------------------
  scripts/check-gsw-cubic-tensor-noise.py
  docs/GSWCubicTensorNoiseExploration.json

The artifact records the SHA-256 of the preceding tensor artifact, all
90 control certificates, error radii 1,3 and security-bit targets 8,32,64,128:
720 exact noise/budget substitutions. None improves the supported bound,
none supplies an additional passing refresh budget, and none gives a
passing budget without an ordinary-input recovery certificate.

This finite failure is also explained by a deterministic inequality:
||D||_1 <= m^(3/2)*||D||_F. If 2t>=m, the displayed probabilistic threshold
is at least the supported threshold for every D. All 720 records are in
that regime. In a larger-dimension family, this concentration estimate can
save a polynomial dimension factor; it still requires a useful proved
coefficient bound. It does not eliminate that construction obligation.

Thirty independent exact tail fixtures check the stated numerical bounds
for all-one tensors at m=2,8,16,32,64, with Rademacher errors and t in
1,2,4,8,16,32. Their residual is the product of three Rademacher sums.
Integer binomial multiplicities compute the exact tails, without Monte
Carlo or enumerating individual tapes. At least one fixture has both a
positive tail and an upper bound strictly below one. These finite checks
are not a formal proof of the general concentration derivation above.

Commands, both exiting zero:
  python3 scripts/check-gsw-cubic-tensor-noise.py --output docs/GSWCubicTensorNoiseExploration.json
  python3 scripts/check-gsw-cubic-tensor-noise.py --verify-existing docs/GSWCubicTensorNoiseExploration.json

2026-10-19 -- IMPLEMENTED APPROXIMATE CUBIC COMPILER PLAN
=======================================================

Authorized scope and unchanged goal
-----------------------------------
The user approved implementing the approximate-compilation plan after the
preceding blocked audit. This is a new bounded research attempt at the same
full objective: ordinary-LWE-only security, an unchanged underlying secret,
no independent circular/KDM or other hardness premise, and no acyclic key
schedule. The plan explicitly gates production integration on a proved
compatible efficient parameter-family construction. Finite experiments do
not replace that theorem. Earlier blocked audits remain historical evidence
of the full security gap, not a restriction on the authorized new work.

Files:
  scripts/search-gsw-approximate-tensor-compiler.py
  docs/GSWApproximateTensorCompilerExploration.json

The script is separate from the production Lean sampler. Generation uses
the existing isolated fpylll 0.6.4 installation; saved-artifact replay uses
only the Python standard library. Existing exact-preimage certificates are
read without rewriting their source artifact. No identical old timed-out
exact-preimage query is rerun.

Actual ciphertext identity, including rounded discrepancy
----------------------------------------------------------
Let C(A) be the Boolean-reduced distinct-cubic coefficient matrix for the
three independent same-secret ordinary-source masks. For public tensor t,
target triple I and gadget weight w, form the centered INTEGER residues

  r_J = centered((C(A)*t)_J - w*[J=I] mod q).

Split each residue as r_J=8*d_J+epsilon_J, using d_J=floor((r_J+4)/8).
Then -4<=epsilon_J<=3, including negative residues and half-modulus ties.
This is integer rounding; it does not invert eight modulo a power of two.
No divisibility-by-eight restriction is placed on t or the target weight.

The three-block compiler from the preceding implementation initially has
phase sum_J (C(A)*t)_J*S_J + t[e1,e2,e3]. Subtract the public degree-at-most-
two correction using these ACTUAL ciphertext coordinates:
  b       -= sum_J d_J;
  c_i     += 2*sum_{J contains i} d_J;
  T_ij    += 4*sum_{J contains i,j} d_J for i<j.
All entries, including the original diagonal/lower matrix entries, remain
in the materialized ciphertext. For the ORIGINAL binary secret S the exact
law becomes

  phase_S(ct) = w*S_I
                + sum_J d_J*product_{j in J}(2*S_j-1)
                + sum_J epsilon_J*S_J
                + t[e1,e2,e3]                                      (mod q).

For supported source errors |e_rj|<=E, its integer residual has the public
bound

  Jbound = E^3*||t||_1 + ||d||_1 + ||epsilon||_1.

The executable also adds an independent ordinary-source recursive zero
ciphertext before publication. Its supported phase error is at most
(n+1)*E, so the COMPLETE control bound used in the refresh test is
Jbound+(n+1)*E. That term is not omitted from any table-level budget.
The coefficient builder and public compiler receive no S or source errors.
The private oracle data in the saved fixtures are validation witnesses,
not published protocol inputs.

Positive public candidate search
--------------------------------
For each E the builder searches the full-rank augmented integer lattice

  L_alpha = {(alpha*t, C(A)*t+q*z): t in Z^(m^3), z in Z^(n choose 3)}

near target (0,w*e_I), with alpha in the fixed order 8*E^3,2*E^3,32*E^3.
The block-triangular basis uses alpha*I and q*I; no invertibility of C(A)
is needed. Thus even-pivot and infeasible EXACT target cases still have
valid approximate candidates. LLL uses delta=3/4 and MPFR GSO precision
max(192,4*bits). Every reduced basis row is checked for modular membership.
Babai outputs integer basis coordinates; the nearby vector is reconstructed
as an exact integer combination, then its tensor/residue equations are
checked again. Floating-point proximity is never accepted as a certificate.

The candidate set contains the zero tensor, a saved matching-mask exact
certificate when available, and delivered vectors from the three scales.
Selection minimizes the exact Jbound, with ties in insertion order. Exact
certificates are a finite benchmark cache, not an assumed uniform short-
preimage oracle. At the intended sufficiently large family dimensions the
toy cache has no matching instance. Every skipped or timed-out optimizer
is recorded; its missing candidates are not called infeasible or optimal.

The zero tensor is a useful explicit baseline: at the top gadget weight
its centered quadratic correction has error bound q/16. It therefore does
not pass a stronger reusable budget merely by erasing the target control.
Tiny gadget weights can be absorbed as explicitly bounded discrepancy;
this does not invoke the old low-level deletion theorem for a new error law.

Parameter study and runtime rules
---------------------------------
The main grid uses n=3,4,6,8; m=ceil(3*n/4); bit lengths 12,24,64,128;
and E=2^ceil(sqrt(bits)),1,3 in that priority order. There is one seeded
public mask triple per (n,m,bits), using the established seed convention
20261019+1000*n+100*m+bits. No mask is resampled to improve an outcome.
The family-scaled E values are 16,32,256,4096. These still use TOY n, not
the production family's matching binary dimensions.

Each radius table contains every distinct cubic triple and every gadget
level 0 through bits-1. Public coefficient/error certificates cover that
full Cartesian product. Optimizer completion is reported separately:
the zero baseline makes a certificate available even when an optimizer
has missing output, but it does not make that optimizer completed.

The reduction cap is 60 seconds per LLL call; the parameter-case SEARCH
cap is 180 seconds; the global generation cap is two hours. Basis
membership verification and candidate solving use the remaining case
budget after LLL finishes. Final certificate canonicalization and oracle
validation are accounted separately from search time in each case's
search_elapsed_seconds/total_elapsed_seconds. Partial delivered batches
survive worker termination. Only this script's own workers are killed.
The artifact is atomically checkpointed after each parameter case.

Additional source preimage checks have a 15-second cap within the case
budget, or reuse a checked matching-mask saved certificate. For each
radius they test whether every original bit is recovered by exact public
half-modulus preimages with 4*E*||pi_i||_1<q. A failing or missing filter
is not evidence of hardness. A passing filter concerns the ordinary
public INPUT; it is a direct view attack if that prefix is published,
not a claimed attack on the unchanged production family.

Complete-coordinate view fixtures
---------------------------------
For each radius table, max(bits,number-of-triples) seeded oracle fixtures
explicitly cover every level and every triple marginally. This is NOT
exhaustive checking of every triple/level/secret/error combination.
All triple/level pairs instead have the public coefficient certificates
and the universal supported algebraic bound above.

Each fixture checks an actual complete T,c,b output, the additional zero
source, and a separately generated three-column ordinary-source public
table under the same S. The public compiler retains every table coordinate
by identity. The three-column table is a TOY preservation fixture; it is
not substituted for the full family sample count needed for statistical
masking. All public sources and any published auxiliary data still need
the complete joint source simulation before production integration.

Independent replay reconstructs cubic coefficients by Boolean finite
differences, separately from the generator's assignment enumeration.
It verifies modular residues, unique centered ranges, integer decomposition
and exact norms without trusting a lattice solver. A direct unoptimized
integer formula reconstructs every output coordinate independently of the
optimized compiler. Original-secret phases, zero-source laws, source-recovery
vectors, coverage and exact table-level budget arithmetic are rechecked.
Fixture RNG schedules are reconstructed so omitted failures or cherry-picked
oracle tapes would not replay.

Theorem gate and production disposition
---------------------------------------
The intended family still has n=2*(parameter+1)^5, q=2^((parameter+1)^2),
E=2^(parameter+1), with m=ceil(3*n/4) for this candidate. For a public
whole-table gate, let rows=n^2+n+1, growth=rows*bits, and lookup the least
power of two at least eight times rows. A sufficient fixed control cap is

 B_parameter = floor((q-1)/(4*lookup^2*rows*growth*(growth+1)^2)).

The public block passes only if every required tensor certificate has
Jbound+(n+1)*E <= B_parameter. Correctness and the source-prefix constraints
must use this same bound and count all ordinary-source samples. No empirical
frequency over the toy grid proves a family success-probability bound.

The unproved central target is that the SPECIFIED efficient public algorithm
passes this complete-table gate with probability at least 1/4 eventually.
Existence of suitable tensors would not alone prove this algorithmic claim.
Polynomial bit-lengths and finite LLL outputs do not establish the required
polynomial-time implementation/reduction closure in Lean either.

If that probability theorem were proved, 4*(parameter+1) independent blocks
would have failure at most (3/4)^(4*(parameter+1)) <= 2^(-(parameter+1)).
The real sampler could use the original correct private control generator
only on total failure, while the simulator emits a dummy view there. The
coupling cost would then be negligible. This arithmetic is checked, but
its missing probability premise is NOT assumed or discharged by finite data.
No production fallback or conditional Lean security interface was added.

The complete ordinary-source public view, uniform-source table marginal,
ordinary-to-binary conversion costs, actual reusable error bound and PPT
closure would all have to be instantiated after that theorem gate. The
family audit records these obligations as unproved. No independent SIS,
binary-LWE, circular/KDM, trapdoor or leakage hardness premise is introduced.
The full ordinary-LWE-only security goal remains unfinished.


Why a generic nearest-plane theorem does not discharge the gate
---------------------------------------------------------------
The primary AFP entry by Eric Ren, Sage Binder and Katherine Kosaian
(26 September 2024) describes the standard 2^(dimension/2) approximation
guarantee for an LLL-reduced basis and formalizes the nearest-plane
correctness argument in Isabelle:
https://isa-afp.org/entries/Babai_Nearest_Plane.html
This is a useful reference for a future algorithm/correctness port, not a
Lean theorem for this compiler or a random-tensor norm theorem.

For s=parameter+1>=2, n=2*s^5 and m=ceil(3*n/4)>=s^5. The augmented
lattice dimension d=m^3+(n choose 3) is at least s^15, whereas log2(q)=s^2.
Thus d>=2*log2(q), and the generic approximation factor is already at
least q. A factor of that size, by itself, supplies no useful absolute
noise guarantee below q divided by the refresh overhead. This does NOT
prove that actual Babai outputs fail, or that no approximate compiler can
work. It explains why the finite successes and a generic lattice theorem
cannot replace the missing specialized success-probability/output bound.

2026-10-19 -- EXACT ALL-TARGET BASIS CERTIFICATE AND A PROFILE CRITERION
======================================================================

Previous-turn classification and concrete progress
---------------------------------------------------
The preceding approved approximate-compiler implementation made progress:
it produced public error certificates, validated full output coordinates,
and exposed the remaining family probability/efficiency gate. Its generation
and replay processes are terminal. This continuation does not restart any
of its timed-out queries. Instead it derives a sufficient all-target bound
from a returned integer basis and checks that bound using exact arithmetic.
This strengthens the finite evidence and identifies a specific remaining
mathematical target; it does not complete the full security objective.

Files:
  scripts/check-gsw-approximate-basis-bound.py
  docs/GSWApproximateBasisBoundExploration.json

A universal supported bound for one returned basis
--------------------------------------------------
Write p=m^3, r=(n choose 3), d=p+r, alpha=8*E^3. Let B be an integer basis
for the augmented lattice {(alpha*t,C(A)*t+q*z)}, with orthogonal
Gram--Schmidt vectors B_i^* and squared lengths g_i. Set S=sum_i g_i.
Use EXACT nearest-plane rounding, not an assumption about floating-point
rounding. For every target (0,v) the final residual e has coordinates
beta_i along B_i^* with |beta_i|<=1/2. Hence

  ||e||_2^2 = sum_i beta_i^2*g_i <= S/4,
  ||e||_1 <= sqrt(d*S)/2,
  ||e||_1 <= (sum_i ||B_i^*||_1)/2.

The zero head of the target is essential for the compiler bound:
||e_head||_1=alpha*||t||_1. Centering tail residues modulo q cannot
increase their absolute values. For the compiler's integer rounding
r_J=8*d_J+epsilon_J, |epsilon_J|<=4, the triangle inequality gives

  |d_J|+|epsilon_J| <= |r_J|/8 + 9/2.

Therefore the previous actual integer residual bound, for EVERY control
coefficient target v and every supported original-key source error, is

  Jbound <= ||e||_1/8 + 9*r/2
         <= sqrt(d*S)/16 + 9*r/2.

There is also the bound (sum_i ||B_i^*||_1)/16+9*r/2. The checker takes
the smaller of conservative integer ceilings of these two expressions.
It adds the independent recursive-zero bound (n+1)*E in the SAME existing
whole-table refresh budget. No target-by-target union bound, new error
independence premise or secret-dependent test is used.

The geometric residual statement holds for arbitrary lattice targets.
The displayed compiler interpretation is asserted for targets with zero
head, including all (0,w*e_I). It is a supported bound for the exact
nearest-plane variant, not a theorem silently attributing exact rounding
semantics to every previous MPFR computation.

Exact arithmetic, independent replay and finite checks
-------------------------------------------------------
Generation revisits only n=3,4, scale-8 input problems whose corresponding
lattice search was already recorded COMPLETE in the preceding artifact.
It extracts 24 small integer bases. It uses the saved masks, not newly
resampled or selected successful masks. No source-hardness conclusion is
drawn from restricting to these cases.

Every basis row is checked for membership in the original augmented
lattice. Its Gram--Schmidt norm product is exactly alpha^(2*p)*q^(2*r),
checking the squared determinant and full lattice index. The certificate
contains the integer basis, squared orthogonal norms, their sum and the
all-target integer bound. Rational values are encoded with hexadecimal
numerators/denominators, avoiding machine floats and decimal digit limits.

Nearest-plane rounding uses rational dot products and integer floor of
value+1/2. All 3,420 required triple/level targets have saved integer basis
combinations. Their residual orthogonal coordinates, norm identity and
supported compiler bounds are checked exactly. Replay does NOT rerun LLL:
it independently reconstructs the orthogonal data by rational LDL
factorization of the integer Gram matrix and checks orthogonality.
It then verifies each saved integer combination, plane coordinates,
compiler residue/rounding/norm certificate and the all-target budget.
Source preimage equations and matching-radius recovery margins are also
rechecked, rather than trusting a copied recovery flag.

The finite outcomes are:
  24 exact integer basis certificates;
  3,420 exact target witnesses;
  9 bases whose UNIVERSAL bound passes the finite refresh budget;
  0 passing bases without matching-radius source recovery certificates;
  3,371 exact-rule tensors equal to the saved floating-rule tensors.

The 49 differing tensors are not labeled errors in the previous compiler.
Thirty-three are opposite-sign top-weight tensors; sixteen occur at other
weights. Basis/rounding choices can differ, and both sets have independently
valid residue/noise certificates. The new proof uses its explicit exact
rounding semantics. The previous floating outputs are not retrospectively
claimed to have this exact deterministic trace.

All 131,073 signed-residue inequalities 8*(|d|+|epsilon|)<=|r|+36 pass.
A two-dimensional nonorthogonal lattice tests all 289 targets in [-8,8]^2
against exact nearest-plane residual bounds and the independent LDL data.
The dimension inequality m^3>=2*(n choose 3) is checked for n=3..4096;
the general elementary derivation is given below. These are executable
checks of the displayed mathematical reasoning, not new Lean theorems.
Generation exits zero in 54.547 seconds. No difficult timed-out lattice
problem was retried to obtain these new certificates.

A sufficient profile target for the intended asymptotic family
--------------------------------------------------------------
Let Delta=det(B)=alpha^p*q^r. Define the normalized mean-square profile

  P_real = S / (d*Delta^(2/d)).

AM--GM gives P_real>=1. The artifact records the LEAST integer P>=P_real,
checked without roots by S^d <= (d*P)^d*Delta^2 and failure of that
inequality for P-1. The finite profiles here have P between 2 and 3.
That observation is only about these small bases; it is not an asymptotic
bound, an estimated success probability, or an independence assumption.

From the L2 certificate,

  Jbound <= d*sqrt(P)*Delta^(1/d)/16 + 9*r/2 + 2.

For m=ceil(3*n/4), p>=27*n^3/64 and 2*r<=n^3/3, so p>=2*r and
r/d<=1/3. Since alpha,q>=1,

  Delta^(1/d)=alpha^(p/d)*q^(r/d) <= alpha*q^(1/3).

With s=parameter+1, n=2*s^5, E=2^s, q=2^(s^2), all dimension/refresh
factors are polynomial in s. A POLYNOMIAL bound on P would thus give

  (refresh overhead)*Jbound/q
      <= polynomial(s)*2^(-2*s^2/3+3*s+3),

up to smaller polynomial terms, tending to zero. The additional
(n+1)*E source-zero bound is smaller and also fits eventually. The +2
accounts conservatively for integer ceilings. This supplies a concrete
SUFFICIENT analytic route to the reusable budget, not a necessary condition
or an equivalence to all possible compiler success events.

The remaining mathematical target is to PROVE that a specified polynomial-
time public basis generator, applied to UNIFORM source masks in the actual
family, returns P bounded by some fixed polynomial with probability at
least 1/4 eventually. The statement must concern that algorithm, not merely
the existence of short bases. It cannot be introduced as an independent
hardness, circularity or geometric-heuristic assumption. No such profile
probability proof has been obtained. Generic LLL quality bounds do not
supply it at these dimensions. Formal polynomial-time implementation and
reduction closure also remain obligations.

If that probability theorem were proved, the already specified bounded
repetition and rare private-fallback coupling could be used, followed by
the complete ordinary-source/marginal masking reduction and reusable
correctness integration. The present sufficient condition makes that
missing theorem more explicit; it does not discharge it. No production
sampler, Lean source, assumption, import or security interface is changed.
The full ordinary-LWE-only same-secret bootstrappable security goal remains
unfinished, and the resumed goal remains active.

Reproduction:
  PYTHONPATH=/tmp/fhe-preimage-packages python3 scripts/check-gsw-approximate-basis-bound.py --output docs/GSWApproximateBasisBoundExploration.json
  python3 scripts/check-gsw-approximate-basis-bound.py --verify-existing docs/GSWApproximateBasisBoundExploration.json

bounds, every budget and matching-radius source recovery certificate.
The independent LDL calculation uses rational arithmetic from its first

2026-10-19 -- PROFILE-RESULT APPLICABILITY AND FULL COMPLETION AUDIT
=================================================================

The approved approximate compiler and the subsequent exact basis verifier
are implemented and independently replayed. The remaining obstruction is
a proved success probability for a specified efficient public algorithm in
the intended asymptotic family, followed by its actual security/correctness
integration. This third consecutive resumed goal-work turn audits that same
obstruction. It does not restart timed-out searches or substitute another
finite experiment for the missing probability theorem.

Primary-source applicability checks
------------------------------------
1. Ding, Kim, Takagi, Wang and Yang, A physical study of the LLL algorithm,
   arXiv:2106.02158v3, especially Sections 2.5 and 3:
   https://arxiv.org/html/2106.02158v3
   The paper proves results for stochastic sandpile models and compares
   their statistics with LLL. Its Conjecture 4 is the proposed transfer of
   mixing properties to LLL on generic inputs. The model's independent
   update variables cannot be treated as proved properties of LLL on our
   tensor-mask input distribution. In particular, this supplies no proved
   polynomial bound on our normalized mean-square profile with probability
   at least 1/4 for a specified polynomial-time basis generator.

2. Laarhoven, Asymptotics of Random-Lattice Enumeration: Beyond HKZ via
   Dense Sublattices, arXiv:2610.15265v1, 11 October 2026, especially
   Sections 1.2, 2.3 and the proof of Theorem 1.8:
   https://arxiv.org/html/2610.15265v1
   The lattice law is Haar measure on determinant-one real lattices, not
   our discrete correlated L(alpha,C(A),q) law. The result concerns
   enumeration and CVPP costs. The CVPP construction retains a basis as
   advice and permits unrestricted preprocessing; its query time remains
   superpolynomial. Polynomial space and polynomial basis encoding length
   do not establish polynomial-time public generation. No transfer from
   that Haar law, nor the required generator/profile probability theorem,
   has been proved here. Thus this result cannot instantiate our gate.

These are applicability failures for the checked results, not a claim that
all literature has been exhausted or that another construction is impossible.
The finite integer profiles between 2 and 3 remain evidence for those 24
bases only. The profile bound is sufficient, not necessary for every possible
compiler; failure to prove it does not refute the broader approximate route.

Requirement-by-requirement completion audit
-------------------------------------------
* Actual same-secret repeated refresh correctness: proved for the existing
  recursive family by RecursiveFeatureFHECorrectness.
  eventually_family_correctExp_probability_one. This does not prove
  confidentiality, nor instantiate the new approximate sampler.
* Complete public-view security: unfinished. The current family theorem in
  RecursiveFeatureLowLevelHybrid.family_oneTime_abs_signedAdvantage_le
  still contains deletedReplacementAdvantage for all surviving controls.
  That term has not been proved negligible under ordinary LWE.
* Ordinary-source simulator: GSWCorrelatedMasking.
  ordinary_source_simulator_game_abs_advantage_le is a checked conditional
  game bound for an explicit positive simulator with a uniform full-table
  marginal. A compatible efficient production sampler has not been supplied.
* New compiler's reusable noise: GSWTranscriptCompiler.noiseBound_bootstrap
  still requires herror for the actual joint source error and discrepancy.
  Exact finite certificates and the sufficient basis-profile inequality
  do not establish the required family probability bound. Bounded repetition
  and private-fallback coupling remain conditional on that missing theorem.
* Efficiency and hardness class: LWE.Security.HardAgainst applies to its
  allowed adversary class. Formal polynomial-time implementation and closure
  of the actual composed reduction have not been established. Finite game
  inequalities, short-basis existence, unrestricted preprocessing and a
  floating-point implementation are not substitutes for this obligation.

No requirement has been weakened to an independent circular/KDM/geometric
assumption or to an acyclic key schedule. The full security objective is not
complete. There is no verified ongoing computation or established theorem
that now unlocks production integration; that step needs a new mathematical
argument or a different proved compatible construction. The same blocker
has persisted through approximate implementation, exact-profile analysis,
and this applicability audit, so the resumed goal is marked BLOCKED.
